## Canada Labour Code {#canada-labour-code .Title-of-Act}

[R.S.C.]{.abbr title="Revised Statutes of Canada"}, 1985, c. L-2
:::

An Act to consolidate certain statutes respecting labour
::::

::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
## [Short Title]{.HTitleText1} {#h-339479 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short title

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** This Act may be cited as
the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 1
:::

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-339484 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** In this Act,

[[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Canada Industrial Relations
    Board established by section 9; ([Conseil]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[external adjudicator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[external adjudicator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person
    appointed under subsection 12.001(1); ([arbitre
    externe]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[federal work, undertaking or business]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[federal work, undertaking or business]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
    any work, undertaking or business that is within the legislative
    authority of Parliament, including, without restricting the
    generality of the foregoing,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a work, undertaking or business operated or
      carried on for or in connection with navigation and shipping,
      whether inland or maritime, including the operation of ships and
      transportation by ship anywhere in Canada,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a railway, canal, telegraph or other work or
      undertaking connecting any province with any other province, or
      extending beyond the limits of a province,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a line of ships connecting a province with any
      other province, or extending beyond the limits of a province,

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a ferry between any province and any other
      province or between any province and any country other than
      Canada,

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} aerodromes, aircraft or a line of air
      transportation,

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a radio broadcasting station,

    - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a bank or an authorized foreign bank within the
      meaning of section 2 of the [Bank Act](/eng/acts/B-1.01),

    - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a work or undertaking that, although wholly
      situated within a province, is before or after its execution
      declared by Parliament to be for the general advantage of Canada
      or for the advantage of two or more of the provinces,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a work, undertaking or business outside the
      exclusive legislative authority of the legislatures of the
      provinces, and

    - [(j)]{.lawlabel} a work, undertaking or activity in respect of
      which federal laws within the meaning of section 2 of the [Oceans
      Act](/eng/acts/O-2.4) apply pursuant to section 20 of that Act and
      any regulations made pursuant to paragraph 26(1)(k) of that
      Act; ([entreprises fédérales]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Head]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Head]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Head of Compliance and
    Enforcement designated under subsection
    122.21(1); ([chef]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Minister of
    Labour. ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 2
- 1990, c. 44, s. 17
- 1996, c. 31, s. 89
- 1999, c. 28, s. 169
- [2017, c. 20, s. 318](#1036790-1192509){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036790-1192509.html"}

  ajax-after="1040149-1281905.html"}
:::

## [PART I]{.HLabel1}[Industrial Relations]{.HTitleText1} {#h-339501 .Part}

**[[Preamble]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-Preamble .sectionLabel}** 

**[[]{.sectionLabel}]{#s- .sectionLabel}** WHEREAS there is a long
tradition in Canada of labour legislation and policy designed for the
promotion of the common well-being through the encouragement of free
collective bargaining and the constructive settlement of disputes;

AND WHEREAS Canadian workers, trade unions and employers recognize and
support freedom of association and free collective bargaining as the
bases of effective industrial relations for the determination of good
working conditions and sound labour-management relations;

AND WHEREAS the Government of Canada has ratified Convention No. 87 of
the International Labour Organization concerning Freedom of Association
and Protection of the Right to Organize and has assumed international
reporting responsibilities in this regard;

AND WHEREAS the Parliament of Canada desires to continue and extend its
support to labour and management in their cooperative efforts to develop
good relations and constructive collective bargaining practices, and
deems the development of good industrial relations to be in the best
interests of Canada in ensuring a just share of the fruits of progress
to all;

NOW THEREFORE, Her Majesty, by and with the advice and consent of the
Senate and House of Commons of Canada, enacts as follows:

::: HistoricalNote
- 1972, c. 18, Preamble
:::

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-339510 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this
  Part,

  [[arbitration board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[arbitration board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an arbitration
      board constituted by or pursuant to a collective agreement or by
      agreement between the parties to a collective agreement and
      includes an arbitration board the chairperson of which is
      appointed by the Minister under this Part; ([conseil
      d'arbitrage]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[arbitrator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[arbitrator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a sole arbitrator
      selected by the parties to a collective agreement or appointed by
      the Minister under this Part; ([arbitre]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[bargaining agent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[bargaining agent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a trade union that has been certified by the
        Board as the bargaining agent for the employees in a bargaining
        unit and the certification of which has not been revoked, or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other trade union that has entered into a
        collective agreement on behalf of the employees in a bargaining
        unit

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the term of which has not expired, or

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of which the trade union has, by
          notice given pursuant to subsection 49(1), required the
          employer to commence collective bargaining; ([agent
          négociateur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[bargaining unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[bargaining unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a unit

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determined by the Board to be appropriate for
        collective bargaining, or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to which a collective agreement
        applies; ([unité de négociation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2017, c. 20, s.
      319\]]{.Repealed}

  [[collective agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[collective agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an agreement in
      writing entered into between an employer and a bargaining agent
      containing provisions respecting terms and conditions of
      employment and related matters; ([convention
      collective]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[conciliation board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[conciliation board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a board
      established by the Minister under paragraph 72(1)(c); ([commission
      de conciliation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[conciliation commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[conciliation commissioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person
      appointed by the Minister under paragraph
      72(1)(b); ([commissaire-conciliateur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[conciliation officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[conciliation officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person
      appointed by the Minister under paragraph
      72(1)(a); ([conciliateur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[dependent contractor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[dependent contractor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the owner, purchaser or lessee of a vehicle
        used for hauling, other than on rails or tracks, livestock,
        liquids, goods, merchandise or other materials, who is a party
        to a contract, oral or in writing, under the terms of which they
        are

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} required to provide the vehicle by means of
          which they perform the contract and to operate the vehicle in
          accordance with the contract, and

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} entitled to retain for their own use from
          time to time any sum of money that remains after the cost of
          their performance of the contract is deducted from the amount
          they are paid, in accordance with the contract, for that
          performance,

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a fisher who, pursuant to an arrangement to
        which the fisher is a party, is entitled to a percentage or
        other part of the proceeds of a joint fishing venture in which
        the fisher participates with other persons, and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any other person who, whether or not employed
        under a contract of employment, performs work or services for
        another person on such terms and conditions that they are, in
        relation to that other person, in a position of economic
        dependence on, and under an obligation to perform duties for,
        that other person; ([entrepreneur dépendant]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[dispute]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[dispute]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a dispute arising in
      connection with the entering into, renewing or revising of a
      collective agreement, in respect of which notice may be given to
      the Minister under section 71; ([différend]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any person employed by an
      employer and includes a dependent contractor and a private
      constable, but does not include a person who performs management
      functions or is employed in a confidential capacity in matters
      relating to industrial relations; ([employé]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[employer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[employer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any person who employs one or more employees,
        and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a dependent contractor, such
        person as, in the opinion of the Board, has a relationship with
        the dependent contractor to such extent that the arrangement
        that governs the performance of services by the dependent
        contractor for that person can be the subject of collective
        bargaining; ([employeur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[employers' organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[employers' organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any
      organization of employers the purposes of which include the
      regulation of relations between employers and
      employees; ([organisation patronale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[lockout]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[lockout]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes the closing of a place of
      employment, a suspension of work by an employer or a refusal by an
      employer to continue to employ a number of their employees, done
      to compel their employees, or to aid another employer to compel
      that other employer's employees, to agree to terms or conditions
      of employment; ([lock-out]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[parties]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[parties]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in relation to the entering into, renewing or
        revising of a collective agreement and in relation to a dispute,
        the employer and the bargaining agent that acts on behalf of the
        employer's employees,

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in relation to a difference relating to the
        interpretation, application, administration or alleged
        contravention of a collective agreement, the employer and the
        bargaining agent, and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in relation to a complaint to the Board under
        this Part, the complainant and any person or organization
        against whom or which the complaint is
        made; ([parties]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[private constable]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[private constable]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person appointed
      as a police constable under Part IV.1 of the [Railway Safety
      Act](/eng/acts/R-4.2); ([agent de police privé]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[professional employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[professional employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an employee
      who

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is, in the course of their employment, engaged
        in the application of specialized knowledge ordinarily acquired
        by a course of instruction and study resulting in graduation
        from a university or similar institution, and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is, or is eligible to be, a member of a
        professional organization that is authorized by statute to
        establish the qualifications for membership in the
        organization; ([membre de profession libérale]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[strike]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[strike]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a cessation of work or a
      refusal to work or to continue to work by employees, in
      combination, in concert or in accordance with a common
      understanding, and a slowdown of work or other concerted activity
      on the part of employees in relation to their work that is
      designed to restrict or limit output; ([grève]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[trade union]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[trade union]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any organization of
      employees, or any branch or local thereof, the purposes of which
      include the regulation of relations between employers and
      employees; ([syndicat]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a group of two or more
      employees. ([unité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee status preserved

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person ceases to be an employee within the meaning
  of this Part by reason only of their ceasing to work as the result of
  a lockout or strike or by reason only of their dismissal contrary to
  this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 3
- 1996, c. 10, s. 234
- 1998, c. 10, s. 182, c. 26, ss. 1, 59(E)
- 1999, c. 31, ss. 149(E), 162(E)
- 2007, c. 19, s. 60
- 2015, c. 3, s. 15(F)
- [2017, c. 20, s. 319](#1036795-1192511){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036795-1192511.html"}
:::

:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-339557 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of Part

**[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies in
respect of employees who are employed on or in connection with the
operation of any federal work, undertaking or business, in respect of
the employers of all such employees in their relations with those
employees and in respect of trade unions and employers' organizations
composed of those employees or employers.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 108
- 1972, c. 18, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Crown corporations

- **[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Part applies in respect of any corporation established to perform any
  function or duty on behalf of the Government of Canada and in respect
  of the employees of any such corporation, except any such corporation,
  and the employees thereof, that the Governor in Council excludes from
  the operation of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, pursuant to subsection
  (1), exclude from the operation of this Part only those corporations
  in respect of which a minister of the Crown, the Treasury Board or the
  Governor in Council is authorized to establish or to approve some or
  all of the terms and conditions of employment of persons employed
  therein.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Addition of name to Schedule

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the Governor in Council excludes any
  corporation from the operation of this Part, the Governor in Council
  shall, by order, add the name of that corporation to Schedule IV or V
  to the [Financial Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 5
- 2003, c. 22, s. 107
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 5"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Canadian carriers

**[[5.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.1 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies in
respect of any Canadian carrier, as defined in section 2 of the
[Telecommunications Act](/eng/acts/T-3.4), that is an agent of Her
Majesty in right of a province and in respect of the employees of the
carrier.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 38, s. 88
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employees of Her Majesty

**[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** Except as provided by
section 5, this Part does not apply in respect of employment by Her
Majesty in right of Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1972, c. 18, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presumption

- **[[6.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person --- other than a person who performs management functions or is
  employed in a confidential capacity in matters relating to industrial
  relations --- who is paid remuneration by an employer is presumed to
  be their employee unless the contrary is proved by the employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The presumption does not apply for the purposes of a
  prosecution under this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 17, s. 235](#1467797-1481273){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1467797-1481273.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

**[[6.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6.2 .sectionLabel}** If, in any proceeding
under this Part other than a prosecution, an employer alleges that a
person is not their employee, the burden of proof is on the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 17, s. 235](#1467797-1481277){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1467797-1481277.html"}
:::

### [Major Projects]{.HTitleText2} {#h-339579 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Major projects

**[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in this Part shall
be construed so as to prevent the establishment of agreements on a
project basis and where all the parties in a collective bargaining
relationship identify themselves to the Minister as being engaged in a
project that the Minister determines to be a major project, the Minister
and the Board shall act as expeditiously as possible to facilitate the
collective bargaining process involving those parties.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1984, c. 39, s. 22
:::

### [DIVISION I]{.HLabel2}[Basic Freedoms]{.HTitleText2} {#h-339584 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee freedoms

- **[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  employee is free to join the trade union of their choice and to
  participate in its lawful activities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer freedoms

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employer is free to join the employers'
  organization of their choice and to participate in its lawful
  activities.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 8
- 1999, c. 31, s. 162(E)
:::

### [DIVISION II]{.HLabel2}[Canada Industrial Relations Board]{.HTitleText2} {#h-339592 .Subheading}

#### [Establishment and Organization]{.HTitleText3} {#h-339593 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Establishment of Board

- **[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A board
  is established, to be known as the Canada Industrial Relations Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition of Board

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board is composed of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Chairperson, to hold office on a full-time basis;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} two or more Vice-Chairpersons, to hold office on a
    full-time basis, and any other Vice-Chairpersons, to hold office on
    a part-time basis, that the Governor in Council considers necessary
    to discharge the responsibilities of the Board;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} not more than six other members, of which not more
    than three represent employees, and of which not more than three
    represent employers, to hold office on a full-time basis;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other part-time members, representing, in equal
    numbers, employees and employers, that the Governor in Council
    considers necessary to discharge the responsibilities of the Board;
    and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any other full-time or part-time members that the
    Governor in Council considers necessary to assist the Board in
    carrying out its functions under Parts II, III and IV.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 9
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
- [2017, c. 20, s. 320](#1036796-1192513){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036796-1192513.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-9-20190729.html "Link to previous version of section 9"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of Chairperson and
Vice-Chairpersons

- **[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chairperson and Vice-Chairpersons of the Board are to be appointed by
  the Governor in Council, on the recommendation of the Minister, to
  hold office during good behaviour for terms not exceeding five years
  each, subject to removal by the Governor in Council at any time for
  cause.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of other members

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), the members of the Board
  other than the Chairperson and the Vice-Chairpersons are to be
  appointed by the Governor in Council on the recommendation of the
  Minister after consultation by the Minister with the organizations
  representative of employees or employers that the Minister considers
  appropriate, to hold office during good behaviour for terms not
  exceeding three years each, subject to removal by the Governor in
  Council at any time for cause.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The members of the Board appointed pursuant to
  paragraph 9(2)(e) are to be appointed by the Governor in Council, on
  the recommendation of the Minister, to hold office during good
  behaviour for terms not exceeding three years each, subject to removal
  by the Governor in Council at any time for cause.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement for appointment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The members of the Board must be Canadian citizens or
  permanent residents within the meaning of subsection 2(1) of the
  [Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chairperson and Vice-Chairpersons

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson and Vice-Chairpersons must have
  experience and expertise in industrial relations.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 10
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
- 2001, c. 27, s. 215
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Residence of members

- **[[10.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The full-time members of the Board
  must reside in the National Capital Region as described in the
  schedule to the [National Capital Act](/eng/acts/N-4) or within the
  distance from the National Capital Region that is determined by the
  Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by order, exempt a
  member from the requirement set out in subsection (1), subject to any
  conditions that the Governor in Council may prescribe.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
- 2017, c. 20, s. 321
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 10.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Full-time occupation

- **[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  full-time members of the Board must not hold any other employment or
  office in respect of which they receive any remuneration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Part-time occupation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A part-time Vice-Chairperson, or a member appointed
  pursuant to paragraph 9(2)(e), must not hold any other employment or
  office in respect of which they receive any remuneration and that is
  inconsistent with their duties under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 11
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reappointment

- **[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  member of the Board is eligible for reappointment on the expiration of
  any term of office in the same or another capacity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Completion of duties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a member of the Board ceases to be a member of
  the Board for any reason other than removal, the member may, despite
  anything in this Part, at the request of the Chairperson, carry out
  and complete any duties or responsibilities that the member would
  otherwise have had if the member had not ceased to be a member, in
  connection with any matter that came before the Board while the member
  was still a member of the Board and in respect of which there was any
  proceeding in which the member participated as a member.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 12
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of external adjudicator

- **[[12.001]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.001
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may, if the
  Chairperson considers it advisable, appoint an external adjudicator to
  determine any matter that comes before the Board under section 87.4 or
  Part II, III or IV.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers, duties and functions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An external adjudicator has all the powers, duties
  and functions that are conferred on the Board by this Act with respect
  to any matter for which they have been appointed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision of external adjudicator

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An order or decision made or a direction issued by an
  external adjudicator under this Act is deemed to be an order or
  decision made or a direction issued by the Board, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration and expenses

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An external adjudicator shall be paid the
  remuneration and the fees that may be fixed by the Chairperson and is
  entitled to be paid reasonable travel and living expenses incurred by
  them in the course of their duties while absent from their ordinary
  place of residence.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 322](#1036806-1192524){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036806-1192524.html"}
- [2024, c. 12, s. 1](#1460053-1532509){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1460053-1532509.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.001-20210101.html "Link to previous version of section 12.001"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Functions of Chairperson

- **[[12.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson has supervision over
  and direction of the work of the Board, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the assignment and reassignment of matters that the
    Board is seized of to panels;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the composition of panels and the assignment of
    Vice-Chairpersons to preside over panels;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the determination of the date, time and place of
    hearings;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of the work of the Board; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the management of the Board's internal affairs.

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2014, c. 20, s. 416\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may delegate to a Vice-Chairperson
  any of the Chairperson's powers, duties and functions under subsection
  (1).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2014, c. 20, s. 416\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
- 2014, c. 20, s. 416
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.01-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 12.01"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meetings

- **[[12.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.02
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson convenes and
  presides over any meeting of the Board concerning the making of
  regulations under section 15.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quorum

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the following
  persons constitute a quorum:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to paragraph (b), the Chairperson, two
    Vice-Chairpersons and two other members representing, respectively,
    employees and employers; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at a meeting held for the making of regulations
    respecting matters that are not governed by Part I, the Chairperson,
    two Vice-Chairpersons and, if two or more full-time members have
    been appointed under paragraph 9(2)(e), two of those members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Equal representation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If, at a meeting referred to in subsection (1) held
  for the making of regulations respecting matters that are governed by
  Part I, there is an unequal number of members representing employers
  and employees, the Chairperson shall designate an equal number of
  members who are authorized to vote on the making of those regulations
  and who represent employers and employees respectively.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members not permitted to vote

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Members who represent employees or employers are not
  permitted to vote on the making of regulations respecting matters that
  are not governed by Part I.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
- [2017, c. 20, s. 323](#1036816-1192533){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036816-1192533.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.02-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 12.02"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acting Chairperson

**[[12.03]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.03 .sectionLabel}** If the Chairperson
of the Board is absent or unable to act or the office of Chairperson is
vacant, a Vice-Chairperson designated by the Minister acts as
Chairperson for the time being, and a Vice-Chairperson so designated has
and may exercise all the powers and perform all the duties and functions
of the Chairperson.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration

- **[[12.04]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.04
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The full-time members of the Board
  shall be paid any remuneration, and the part-time members of the Board
  and members of the Board carrying out duties and responsibilities
  under subsection 12(2) shall be paid any fees, that may be fixed by
  the Governor in Council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Travel and living expenses

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The members of the Board are entitled to be paid
  reasonable travel and living expenses incurred by them in the course
  of their duties under this Act while absent from, in the case of
  full-time members, their ordinary place of work and, in the case of
  part-time members and members carrying out duties and responsibilities
  under subsection 12(2), their ordinary place of residence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation

**[[12.05]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.05 .sectionLabel}** Each member of the
Board is deemed to be an employee for the purposes of the [Government
Employees Compensation Act](/eng/acts/G-5) and to be employed in the
federal public administration for the purposes of any regulations made
under section 9 of the [Aeronautics Act](/eng/acts/A-2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
- 2003, c. 22, s. 224(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.05-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 12.05"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation of liability

**[[12.051]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.051 .sectionLabel}** The Chairperson,
Vice-Chairpersons, other members and external adjudicators are not
personally liable, either civilly or criminally, for anything done or
omitted to be done by them in good faith in the exercise or purported
exercise of any power, or in the performance or purported performance of
any duty or function, conferred on them under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 20, s. 324
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-12.051-20170622.html "Link to previous version of section 12.051"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiries

**[[12.06]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.06 .sectionLabel}** The Chairperson may
request the Minister to decide whether any member of the Board should be
subject to remedial or disciplinary measures for any reason set out in
paragraphs 12.14(2)(a) to (d).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measures

**[[12.07]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.07 .sectionLabel}** On receipt of the
request, the Minister may take one or more of the following measures:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} obtain, in an informal and expeditious manner, any
  information that the Minister considers necessary;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} refer the matter for mediation, where the Minister is
  satisfied that the issues in relation to the request may be
  appropriately resolved by mediation;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} request the Governor in Council to have an inquiry
  held under section 12.08; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} advise the Chairperson that the Minister considers
  that it is not necessary to take further measures under this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of inquirer

**[[12.08]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.08 .sectionLabel}** On receipt of a
request referred to in paragraph 12.07(c), the Governor in Council may,
on the recommendation of the Minister of Justice, appoint a judge of a
superior court to conduct the inquiry.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

**[[12.09]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.09 .sectionLabel}** The judge has all
the powers, rights and privileges that are vested in a superior court,
including the power

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to issue a summons requiring any person to appear at
  the time and place mentioned in the summons to testify about all
  matters within that person's knowledge relative to the inquiry and to
  produce any document or thing relative to the inquiry; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to administer oaths and examine any person on oath.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staff

**[[12.10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.10 .sectionLabel}** The judge may
engage the services of counsel and other persons having technical or
specialized knowledge to assist the judge in conducting the inquiry,
establish the terms and conditions of their engagement and, with the
approval of the Treasury Board, fix and pay their remuneration and
expenses.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry in public

- **[[12.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  an inquiry must be conducted in public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality of inquiry

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The judge may, on application, take any appropriate
  measures and make any order that the judge considers necessary to
  ensure the confidentiality of the inquiry if, after having considered
  all available alternate measures, the judge is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there is a real and substantial risk that matters
    involving public security will be disclosed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is a real and substantial risk to the
    fairness of the inquiry such that the need to prevent disclosure
    outweighs the societal interest that the inquiry be conducted in
    public; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} there is a serious possibility that the life,
    liberty or security of a person will be endangered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality of application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the judge considers it appropriate, the judge
  may take any measures and make any order that the judge considers
  necessary to ensure the confidentiality of a hearing held in respect
  of an application under subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules of evidence

- **[[12.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In conducting an inquiry, the judge
  is not bound by any legal or technical rules of evidence and may
  receive, and base a decision on, evidence presented in the proceedings
  that the judge considers credible or trustworthy in the circumstances
  of the case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Intervenors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An interested party may, with leave of the judge,
  intervene in an inquiry on the terms and conditions that the judge
  considers appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to be heard

**[[12.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.13 .sectionLabel}** The member who is
the subject of the inquiry must be given reasonable notice of the
subject-matter of the inquiry and of the time and place of any hearing
and must be given an opportunity, in person or by counsel, to be heard
at the hearing, to cross-examine witnesses and to present evidence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to Minister

- **[[12.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After an inquiry has been completed,
  the judge shall submit a report containing the judge's findings and
  recommendations, if any, to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommendations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The judge may, in the report, recommend that the
  member be suspended without pay or removed from office or that any
  other disciplinary measure or any remedial measure be taken if, in the
  opinion of the judge, the member

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has become incapacitated from the proper execution
    of that office by reason of infirmity;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has been guilty of misconduct;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has failed in the proper execution of that office;
    or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} has been placed, by conduct or otherwise, in a
    position that is incompatible with the due execution of that office.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transmission of report to Governor in
Council

**[[12.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.15 .sectionLabel}** Where the Minister
receives a report of an inquiry in which the judge makes a
recommendation, the Minister shall send the report to the Governor in
Council, who may, where the Governor in Council considers it
appropriate, suspend the member without pay, remove the member from
office or impose any other disciplinary measure or any remedial measure.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Head office

**[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** The head office of the
Board must be in the National Capital Region as described in the
schedule to the [National Capital Act](/eng/acts/N-4).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 13
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
- 2014, c. 20, s. 417
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 13"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[13.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13.1 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2014, c.
20, s. 417\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 13.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Panels

- **[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) and
  (3.1), a panel of not fewer than three members, at least one of whom
  is the Chairperson or a Vice-Chairperson, may determine any matter
  that comes before the Board under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Equal representation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a panel formed under subsection (1) is composed
  of one or more members representing employees, an equal number of
  members representing employers must also form part of the panel and
  vice versa.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- single member

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson or a Vice-Chairperson may alone
  determine a matter that comes before the Board under this Part with
  respect to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an uncontested application or question;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a question referred to in paragraph 16(p);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a complaint made under subsection 97(1) in respect
    of an alleged contravention of section 37 or 69 or any of paragraphs
    95(f) to (i);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a request for an extension of time for instituting
    a proceeding;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a preliminary proceeding; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any other matter, if the Chairperson determines
    that it is appropriate because of the possibility of prejudice to a
    party, such as undue delay, or if the parties consent to a
    determination by the Chairperson or a Vice-Chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Single person --- Parts II, III and IV

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson, a Vice-Chairperson or a member
  appointed under paragraph 9(2)(e) may alone determine a matter that
  comes before the Board under Part II, III or IV.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed panel

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson, a Vice-Chairperson or another member
  who determines a matter under subsection (3) or (3.1) is deemed to be
  a panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers, duties and functions

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A panel has all the powers, duties and functions that
  are conferred on the Board by this Act with respect to any matter
  assigned to the panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chairperson of the panel

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson is the chairperson of any panel
  formed under subsection (1) or, where the Chairperson is not a member
  of the panel, he or she designates a Vice-Chairperson to be the
  chairperson of the panel.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 14
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
- [2017, c. 20, s. 325](#1036828-1192544){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036828-1192544.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14-20190729.html "Link to previous version of section 14"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation of proceeding

**[[14.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.1 .sectionLabel}** In the event of the
death or incapacity of a member of a panel formed under subsection 14(1)
who represents either employees or employers, the chairperson of the
panel may determine any matter that was before the panel and the
chairperson's decision is deemed to be the decision of the panel.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision of panel

- **[[14.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  decision made by a majority of the members of a panel or, where there
  is no majority, by the chairperson of the panel is a decision of the
  Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a decision is to be made under this Part, the
  panel shall make it and give notice of it to the parties no later than
  90 days after the day on which the panel reserved the decision or
  within any further period that may be determined by the Chairperson.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 2
- [2017, c. 20, s. 326](#1036835-1192547){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036835-1192547.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.2-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 14.2"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Powers and Duties]{.HTitleText3} {#h-339783 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** The Board may make
regulations of general application respecting

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the establishment of rules of procedure for its
  pre-hearing proceedings and hearings;

- [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} the use of means of telecommunication that permit
  the parties and the Board or its members to communicate simultaneously
  for pre-hearing conferences, hearings and Board meetings;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the determination of units appropriate for collective
  bargaining;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the certification of trade unions as bargaining
  agents for bargaining units;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of representation votes;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the specification of the period of time after which
  the Board may receive an application from a trade union for
  certification as the bargaining agent for a unit where the Board has
  refused an application from the trade union for certification in
  respect of the same or substantially the same unit;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the specification of the period of time after which
  the Board may receive an application from an employee for revocation
  of a trade union's certification as the bargaining agent for a unit
  where the Board has refused an application for revocation in respect
  of the same unit;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the hearing or determination of any application,
  complaint, question, dispute, difference or appeal that may be made or
  referred to the Board;

- [(g.1)]{.lawlabel} an expeditious procedure and matters that may be
  determined under that procedure;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} the forms to be used in respect of any proceeding
  that may come before the Board;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} the time within which and the circumstances under
  which the Board may exercise its powers under section 18;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} any inquiry that the Board may make under subsection
  34(2);

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} the form in which and the period during which
  evidence and information may be presented to the Board in connection
  with any proceeding that may come before it;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} the specification of the time within which and the
  parties or persons to whom notices and other documents shall be sent
  and the circumstances in which such notices or other documents shall
  be deemed to have been given or received by the Board or any party or
  person;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} the determination of the form in which and the period
  during which evidence as to

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the membership of any employees in a trade union,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any objection by employees to the certification of
    a trade union, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any signification by employees that they no
    longer wish to be represented by a trade union

  shall be presented to the Board on an application made to it pursuant
  to this Part;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} the criteria for determining whether an employee is a
  member of a trade union;

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which evidence referred to in
  paragraph (m) may be received by the Board as evidence that any
  employees wish or do not wish to have a particular trade union
  represent them as their bargaining agent, including the circumstances
  in which the evidence so received by the Board may not be made public
  by the Board;

- [(o.1)]{.lawlabel} the conditions for valid strike or lockout votes;

- [(p)]{.lawlabel} the authority of any person to act on behalf of the
  Board and the matters and things to be done and the action to be taken
  by that person, including the authority of an employee of the
  Administrative Tribunals Support Service of Canada to make decisions
  on uncontested applications or questions;

- [(p.1)]{.lawlabel} the manner and criteria for selecting external
  adjudicators; and

- [(q)]{.lawlabel} any other matters and things that may be incidental
  or conducive to the proper performance of the duties of the Board
  under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 15
- 1998, c. 26, s. 3
- 2014, c. 20, s. 418
- [2017, c. 20, s. 327](#1036842-1192552){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036842-1192552.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15-20141101.html "Link to previous version of section 15"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General power to assist parties

- **[[15.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Board, any member of the Board
  or any external adjudicator --- or an employee of the Administrative
  Tribunals Support Service of Canada who is authorized by the Board ---
  may, if the parties agree, assist the parties in resolving any issues
  in dispute at any stage of a proceeding and by any means that the
  Board considers appropriate, without prejudice to the Board's power to
  determine issues that have not been settled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaratory opinions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board, on application by an employer or a trade
  union, may give declaratory opinions.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 4
- 2014, c. 20, s. 419
- [2017, c. 20, s. 328](#1036847-1192554){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036847-1192554.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15.1-20141101.html "Link to previous version of section 15.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Board

**[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** The Board has, in
relation to any proceeding before it, power

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to summon and enforce the attendance of witnesses and
  compel them to give oral or written evidence on oath and to produce
  such documents and things as the Board deems requisite to the full
  investigation and consideration of any matter within its jurisdiction
  that is before the Board in the proceeding;

- [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} to order pre-hearing procedures, including
  pre-hearing conferences that are held in private, and direct the
  times, dates and places of the hearings for those procedures;

- [(a.2)]{.lawlabel} to order that a hearing or a pre-hearing conference
  be conducted using a means of telecommunication that permits the
  parties and the Board to communicate with each other simultaneously;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to administer oaths and solemn affirmations;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} to receive and accept such evidence and information
  on oath, affidavit or otherwise as the Board in its discretion sees
  fit, whether admissible in a court of law or not;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} to examine, in accordance with any regulations of the
  Board, such evidence as is submitted to it respecting the membership
  of any employees in a trade union seeking certification;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} to examine documents forming or relating to the
  constitution or articles of association of

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a trade union or council of trade unions that is
    seeking certification, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any trade union forming part of a council of trade
    unions that is seeking certification;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} to make such examination of records and such
  inquiries as it deems necessary;

- [(f.1)]{.lawlabel} to compel, at any stage of a proceeding, any person
  to provide information or produce the documents and things that may be
  relevant to a matter before it, after providing the parties the
  opportunity to make representations;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} to require an employer to post and keep posted in
  appropriate places, or to transmit by any electronic means that the
  Board deems appropriate, any notice that it considers necessary to
  bring to the attention of any employees any matter relating to the
  proceeding;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} subject to such limitations as the Governor in
  Council may, in the interests of defence or security, prescribe by
  regulation, to enter any premises of an employer where work is being
  or has been done by employees and to inspect and view any work,
  material, machinery, appliances or articles therein and interrogate
  any person respecting any matter that is before the Board in the
  proceeding;

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} to order, at any time before the proceeding has been
  finally disposed of by the Board, that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a representation vote or an additional
    representation vote be taken among employees affected by the
    proceeding in any case where the Board considers that the taking of
    such a vote would assist the Board to decide any question that has
    arisen or is likely to arise in the proceeding, whether or not such
    a representation vote is provided for elsewhere in this Part, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the ballots cast in any representation vote
    ordered by the Board pursuant to subparagraph (i) or any other
    provision of this Part be sealed in ballot boxes and not counted
    except as directed by the Board;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} to enter on the premises of an employer for the
  purpose of conducting representation votes during working hours;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} to authorize any person to do anything that the Board
  may do under paragraphs (a) to (h), (j), or (m) and to report to the
  Board thereon;

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} to adjourn or postpone the proceeding from time to
  time;

- [(l.1)]{.lawlabel} to defer deciding any matter, where the Board
  considers that the matter could be resolved by arbitration or an
  alternate method of resolution;

- [(m)]{.lawlabel} to abridge or extend the time for doing any act,
  filing any document or presenting any evidence in connection with a
  proceeding;

- [(m.1)]{.lawlabel} to extend the time limits set out in this Act for
  instituting a proceeding;

- [(m.2)]{.lawlabel} to make any order and give any direction that the
  Board considers appropriate for the purpose of expediting proceedings
  or preventing an abuse of process;

- [(n)]{.lawlabel} to amend or permit the amendment of any document
  filed in connection with the proceeding;

- [(o)]{.lawlabel} to add a party to the proceeding at any stage of the
  proceeding;

- [(o.1)]{.lawlabel} to summarily refuse to hear, or dismiss, a matter
  for want of jurisdiction or lack of evidence;

- [(p)]{.lawlabel} to decide for all purposes of this Part any question
  that may arise in the proceeding, including, without restricting the
  generality of the foregoing, any question as to whether

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a person is an employer or an employee,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a person performs management functions or is
    employed in a confidential capacity in matters relating to
    industrial relations,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a person is a member of a trade union,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} an organization or association is an employers'
    organization, a trade union or a council of trade unions,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a group of employees is a unit appropriate for
    collective bargaining,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} a collective agreement has been entered into,

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} any person or organization is a party to or bound
    by a collective agreement, and

  - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} a collective agreement is in operation; and

- [(q)]{.lawlabel} to decide any question that may arise in a proceeding
  under Part II, III or IV.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 16
- 1998, c. 26, s. 5
- [2017, c. 20, s. 329](#1036856-1192559){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036856-1192559.html"}
- [2024, c. 12, s. 2](#1460057-1532511){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1460057-1532511.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-16-20210101.html "Link to previous version of section 16"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination without oral hearing

**[[16.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16.1 .sectionLabel}** The Board may decide
any matter before it without holding an oral hearing.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 6
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of the wishes of the
majority of the employees

**[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** Where the Board is
required, in connection with any application made under this Part, to
determine the wishes of the majority of the employees in a unit, it
shall determine those wishes as of the date of the filing of the
application or as of such other date as the Board considers appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1977-78, c. 27, s. 41
- 1980-81-82-83, c. 47, s. 27
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review or amendment of orders

**[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** The Board may review,
rescind, amend, alter or vary any order or decision made by it, and may
rehear any application before making an order in respect of the
application.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 119
- 1972, c. 18, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of structure of bargaining units

- **[[18.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  application by the employer or a bargaining agent, the Board may
  review the structure of the bargaining units if it is satisfied that
  the bargaining units are no longer appropriate for collective
  bargaining.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement of parties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Board reviews, pursuant to subsection (1) or
  section 35 or 45, the structure of the bargaining units, the Board

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} must allow the parties to come to an agreement,
    within a period that the Board considers reasonable, with respect to
    the determination of bargaining units and any questions arising from
    the review; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may make any orders it considers appropriate to
    implement any agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Board is of the opinion that the agreement
  reached by the parties would not lead to the creation of units
  appropriate for collective bargaining or if the parties do not agree
  on certain issues within the period that the Board considers
  reasonable, the Board determines any question that arises and makes
  any orders it considers appropriate in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of orders

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (3), the Board may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determine which trade union shall be the bargaining
    agent for the employees in each bargaining unit that results from
    the review;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} amend any certification order or description of a
    bargaining unit contained in any collective agreement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if more than one collective agreement applies to
    employees in a bargaining unit, decide which collective agreement is
    in force;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} amend, to the extent that the Board considers
    necessary, the provisions of collective agreements respecting expiry
    dates or seniority rights, or amend other such provisions;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if the conditions of paragraphs 89(1)(a) to (d)
    have been met with respect to some of the employees in a bargaining
    unit, decide which terms and conditions of employment apply to those
    employees until the time that a collective agreement becomes
    applicable to the unit or the conditions of those paragraphs are met
    with respect to the unit; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} authorize a party to a collective agreement to give
    notice to bargain collectively.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of orders

**[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** Where the Board may make
any decision or issue any order, prescribe any term or condition or do
any other thing in relation to any person or organization, the Board may
do so generally or in any particular case or class of cases.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 19
- [2017, c. 20, s. 330](#1036863-1192563){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036863-1192563.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 19"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim orders

**[[19.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.1 .sectionLabel}** The Board may, on
application by a trade union, an employer or an affected employee, make
any interim order that the Board considers appropriate for the purpose
of ensuring the fulfilment of the objectives of this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 8
- [2017, c. 20, s. 330](#1036863-1192566){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036863-1192566.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 19.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim decision

- **[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where,
  in order to dispose finally of an application or complaint, it is
  necessary for the Board to determine two or more issues arising
  therefrom, the Board may, if it is satisfied that it can do so without
  prejudice to the rights of any party to the proceeding, issue a
  decision resolving only one or some of those issues and reserve its
  jurisdiction to dispose of the remaining issues.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision final

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A decision referred to in subsection (1) is, except
  as stipulated by the Board, final.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
  [[decision]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[decision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  includes an order, a direction, a determination and a declaration.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 20
- [2017, c. 20, s. 331](#1036865-1192570){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036865-1192570.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-20-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 20"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exercise of powers, duties and functions

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** The Board shall exercise
the powers and perform the duties and functions that are conferred or
imposed on it by this Act, or that may be incidental to the attainment
of the objects of this Act, including the making of orders requiring
compliance with the provisions of this Act, with any regulation made
under this Act or with any decision made in respect of a matter before
the Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 21
- [2017, c. 20, s. 332](#1036867-1192574){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036867-1192574.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-21-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 21"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Review and Enforcement of Orders]{.HTitleText3} {#h-339909 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order and decision final

- **[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Part and to any
  regulations made under paragraph 111.01(1)(g), every order or decision
  made by the Board under this Part is final and is not to be questioned
  or reviewed in any court, except in accordance with the [Federal
  Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7) on the grounds referred to in paragraph
  18.1(4)(a), (b) or (e) of that Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Standing of Board

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Board has standing to appear in proceedings
  referred to in subsection (1) for the purpose of making submissions
  regarding the standard of review to be used with respect to decisions
  of the Board and the Board's jurisdiction, policies and procedures.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No review by [certiorari]{.otherLang
  lang="la"}, etc.

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as permitted by subsection (1), no order,
  decision or proceeding of the Board made or carried on under or
  purporting to be made or carried on under this Part shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be questioned, reviewed, prohibited or restrained,
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be made the subject of any proceedings in or any
    process of any court, whether by way of injunction,
    [certiorari]{.otherLang lang="la"}, prohibition, [quo
    warranto]{.otherLang lang="la"} or otherwise,

  on any ground, including the ground that the order, decision or
  proceeding is beyond the jurisdiction of the Board to make or carry on
  or that, in the course of any proceeding, the Board for any reason
  exceeded or lost its jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 22
- 1990, c. 8, s. 56
- 1998, c. 26, s. 9
- 2002, c. 8, s. 182
- [2017, c. 20, s. 333](#1036869-1192577){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036869-1192577.html"}
- [2024, c. 12, s. 3](#1460059-1532513){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1460059-1532513.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-22-20190729.html "Link to previous version of section 22"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing in Federal Court

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board shall, on the request in writing of any person or organization
  affected by any order or decision of the Board made under this Part,
  file a copy of the order or decision, exclusive of reasons, in the
  Federal Court, unless, in the opinion of the Board,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there is no indication of failure or likelihood of
    failure to comply with the order or decision; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is other good reason why the filing of the
    order or decision in the Federal Court would serve no useful
    purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registration of order and proceedings
  thereon

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Board files a copy of any order or decision
  in the Federal Court pursuant to subsection (1), it shall specify in
  writing to the Court that the copy of the order or decision is filed
  pursuant to that subsection and, where the Board so specifies, the
  copy of the order or decision shall be accepted for filing by, and
  registered in, the Court without further application or other
  proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of registration of order or
  decision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} When a copy of any order or decision of the Board is
  registered pursuant to subsection (2), the order or decision has the
  same force and effect as a judgment obtained in the Federal Court and,
  subject to this section and the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7),
  all proceedings may be taken thereon by any person or organization
  affected thereby as if the order or decision were a judgment of that
  Court.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 23
- 1990, c. 8, s. 57
- 2002, c. 8, s. 182
- [2017, c. 20, s. 334](#1036871-1192580){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036871-1192580.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-23-20030702.html "Link to previous version of section 23"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing in provincial superior court

**[[23.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23.1 .sectionLabel}** The Board may, on
application by a person or organization affected by an order or decision
of the Board made under this Part, file a copy of the order or decision,
exclusive of reasons, in the superior court of a province. Section 23
applies, with any modifications that the circumstances require, to an
order or decision filed in such a superior court.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 10
- [2017, c. 20, s. 335](#1036873-1192583){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036873-1192583.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-23.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 23.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION III]{.HLabel2}[Acquisition and Termination of Bargaining Rights]{.HTitleText2} {#h-339937 .Subheading}

#### [Application for Certification]{.HTitleText3} {#h-339938 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for certification

- **[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  trade union seeking to be certified as the bargaining agent for a unit
  that the trade union considers constitutes a unit appropriate for
  collective bargaining may, subject to this section and any regulations
  made by the Board under paragraph 15(e), apply to the Board for
  certification as the bargaining agent for the unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), an application by a trade
  union for certification as the bargaining agent for a unit may be made

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where no collective agreement applicable to the
    unit is in force and no trade union has been certified under this
    Part as the bargaining agent for the unit, at any time;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where no collective agreement applicable to the
    unit is in force but a trade union has been certified under this
    Part as the bargaining agent for the unit, after the expiration of
    twelve months from the date of that certification or, with the
    consent of the Board, at any earlier time;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where a collective agreement applicable to the unit
    is in force and is for a term of not more than three years, only
    after the commencement of the last three months of its operation;
    and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where a collective agreement applicable to the unit
    is in force and is for a term of more than three years, only after
    the commencement of the thirty-fourth month of its operation and
    before the commencement of the thirty-seventh month of its operation
    and, thereafter, only

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} during the three month period immediately
      preceding the end of each year that the collective agreement
      continues to operate after the third year of its operation, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} after the commencement of the last three months
      of its operation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No application during strike or lockout

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An application for certification under subsection (2)
  in respect of a unit must not, except with the consent of the Board,
  be made during a strike or lockout that is not prohibited by this Part
  and that involves employees in the unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms or conditions of employment not
  to be changed

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where an application by a trade union for
  certification as the bargaining agent for a unit is made in accordance
  with this section, no employer of employees in the unit shall, after
  notification that the application has been made, alter the rates of
  pay, any other term or condition of employment or any right or
  privilege of such employees until

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application has been withdrawn by the trade
    union or dismissed by the Board, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} thirty days have elapsed after the day on which the
    Board certifies the trade union as the bargaining agent for the
    unit,

  except pursuant to a collective agreement or with the consent of the
  Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 24
- 1993, c. 42, s. 1(F)
- 1998, c. 26, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

**[[24.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24.1 .sectionLabel}** A trade union that is
not certified but has entered into a collective agreement the term of
which has not expired may, despite paragraphs 24(2)(c) and (d), make an
application for certification at any time, in respect of the unit to
which the collective agreement applies or substantially the same unit.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 12
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where certification prohibited

- **[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding anything in this
  Part, where the Board is satisfied that a trade union is so dominated
  or influenced by an employer that the fitness of the trade union to
  represent employees of the employer for the purpose of collective
  bargaining is impaired, the Board shall not certify the trade union as
  the bargaining agent for any unit comprised of employees of the
  employer and any collective agreement between the trade union and the
  employer that applies to any such employees shall be deemed not to be
  a collective agreement for the purposes of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding anything in this Part, where the
  Board is satisfied that a trade union denies membership in the trade
  union to any employee or class of employees in a bargaining unit by
  virtue of a policy or practice that the trade union applies relating
  to qualifications for membership in the trade union, the Board shall
  not certify the trade union as the bargaining agent for the bargaining
  unit and any collective agreement between the trade union and the
  employer of the employees in the bargaining unit that applies to the
  bargaining unit shall be deemed not to be a collective agreement for
  the purposes of this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 134
- 1972, c. 18, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where certification prohibited

**[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** The Board shall not
certify a trade union as, and a trade union shall not act as, the
bargaining agent for both a bargaining unit comprised of private
constables and a bargaining unit comprised of employees other than
private constables if any or all of the employees in both such
bargaining units are employed by the same employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 135
- 1972, c. 18, s. 1
:::

#### [Determination of Bargaining Units]{.HTitleText3} {#h-339974 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of appropriate unit

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a trade union applies under section 24 for certification as the
  bargaining agent for a unit that the trade union considers appropriate
  for collective bargaining, the Board shall determine the unit that, in
  the opinion of the Board, is appropriate for collective bargaining.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether a unit constitutes a unit that
  is appropriate for collective bargaining, the Board may include any
  employees in or exclude any employees from the unit proposed by the
  trade union.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Professional employees

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a trade union applies under section 24 for
  certification as the bargaining agent for a unit comprised of or
  including professional employees, the Board, subject to
  subsections (2) and (4), shall determine that the unit appropriate for
  collective bargaining is a unit comprised of only professional
  employees, unless such a unit would not otherwise be appropriate for
  collective bargaining.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In determining that a unit is appropriate for
  collective bargaining under subsection (3), the Board may include in
  the unit

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} professional employees of more than one profession;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} employees performing the functions, but lacking the
    qualifications, of a professional employee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Supervisory employees

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a trade union applies for certification as the
  bargaining agent for a unit comprised of or including employees whose
  duties include the supervision of other employees, the Board may,
  subject to subsection (2), determine that the unit proposed in the
  application is appropriate for collective bargaining.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Private constables

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Board shall not include a private constable in a
  unit with other employees.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 125
- 1972, c. 18, s. 1
:::

#### [Certification of Bargaining Agents and Related Matters]{.HTitleText3} {#h-339992 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to certify trade union

**[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** The Board shall, subject
to this Part, certify a trade union as the bargaining agent for a
bargaining unit if the Board

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} has received from the trade union an application for
  certification as the bargaining agent for a unit;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} has determined the unit that constitutes a unit
  appropriate for collective bargaining; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} is satisfied that, as of the date of the filing of
  the application or of any other date that the Board considers
  appropriate, a majority of the employees in the unit wish to have the
  trade union represent them as their bargaining agent.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 28
- 2014, c. 40, s. 2
- 2017, c. 12, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-28-20150616.html "Link to previous version of section 28"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representation vote

- **[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board may, in any case, for the purpose of satisfying itself as to
  whether employees in a unit wish to have a particular trade union
  represent them as their bargaining agent, order that a representation
  vote be taken among the employees in the unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons not in a unit

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Any person whose services are being used contrary
  to subsection 94(4) is not an employee in the unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandatory vote

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a trade union applies for certification as the
  bargaining agent for a unit in respect of which no other trade union
  is the bargaining agent, and the Board is satisfied that not less than
  35% and not more than 50% of the employees in the unit are members of
  the trade union, the Board shall order that a representation vote be
  taken among the employees in the unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of union membership

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the Board is satisfied that a trade union has
  an established practice of admitting persons to membership without
  regard to the eligibility requirements of its charter, constitution or
  by-laws, the Board may disregard those requirements in determining
  whether a person is a member of a trade union.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 29
- 1998, c. 26, s. 13
- 2014, c. 40, s. 3
- 2017, c. 12, s. 2
- [2024, c. 12, s. 4](#1460063-1532516){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1460063-1532516.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29-20170622.html "Link to previous version of section 29"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conduct of vote

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  the Board orders that a representation vote be taken among employees
  in a unit, the Board shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determine the employees that are eligible to vote;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} make such arrangements and give such directions as
    the Board considers necessary for the proper conduct of the
    representation vote, including the preparation of ballots, the
    method of casting and counting ballots and the custody and sealing
    of ballot boxes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Choice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Board orders that a representation vote be
  taken on an application by a trade union for certification as the
