  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any lockout or strike not prohibited by this Part
    that is in progress when the Board or other person or body in charge
    of conducting the vote notifies the parties in writing of the
    employees' acceptance shall cease forthwith.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers respecting vote

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Board or other person or body in charge of
  conducting the vote shall determine any question that arises under
  this section, including any question relating to the conduct of the
  vote or the determination of its result.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 42, s. 2
:::

#### [Access to Employees]{.HTitleText3} {#h-341022 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for access order

- **[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where the Board receives from a
  trade union an application for an order granting an authorized
  representative of the trade union access to employees living in an
  isolated location on premises owned or controlled by their employer or
  by any other person, the Board may make an order granting the
  authorized representative of the trade union designated in the order
  access to the employees on the premises of their employer or such
  other person, as the case may be, that are designated in the order if
  the Board determines that access to the employees

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} would be impracticable unless permitted on premises
    owned or controlled by their employer or by such other person; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is reasonably required for purposes relating to
    soliciting union memberships, the negotiation or administration of a
    collective agreement, the processing of a grievance or the provision
    of a union service to employees.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board shall, in every order made under subsection
  (1), specify the method of access to the employees, the times at which
  access is permitted and the periods of its duration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1972, c. 18, s. 1
- 1977-78, c. 27, s. 69.1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Communication with off-site workers

- **[[109.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On application by a trade union, the
  Board may, by order, require an employer to give an authorized
  representative of the trade union mentioned in the order, or the
  Board, or both, the names and addresses of employees whose normal
  workplace is not on premises owned or controlled by their employer and
  authorize the trade union to communicate with those employees, by
  electronic means or otherwise, if the Board is of the opinion that
  such communication is required for purposes relating to soliciting
  trade union memberships, the negotiation or administration of a
  collective agreement, the processing of a grievance or the provision
  of a trade union service to employees.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} must specify the method of communication, the times
    of day and the periods during which the communication is authorized,
    and the conditions that must be met in order to ensure the
    protection of the privacy and the safety of affected employees and
    to prevent the abusive use of information; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may include a requirement that the employer, in
    accordance with any terms and conditions that the Board establishes,
    transmit the information that the union wishes to communicate to the
    employees by means of any electronic communications system that the
    employer uses to communicate with the employees.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Board transmission

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Board is of the opinion that the privacy and
  safety of affected employees cannot otherwise be protected, the Board
  may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide each employee with the opportunity to
    refuse the giving of their name and address to the representative of
    the trade union that the Board authorizes and, if the employee does
    not so refuse, may transmit that name and address to the authorized
    representative; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} transmit the information that the union wishes to
    communicate to the employees in the manner it considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of names and addresses

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The names and addresses of employees provided under
  subsection (1) shall not be used unless it is for a purpose consistent
  with this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 50
:::

#### [Access to Financial Statements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-341047 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Financial statement of trade union and
employers' organization

- **[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every trade union and every
  employers' organization shall, forthwith on the request of any of its
  members, provide the member, free of charge, with a copy of a
  financial statement of its affairs to the end of the last fiscal year,
  certified to be a true copy by its president and treasurer or by its
  president and any other officer responsible for the handling and
  administration of its funds.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any financial statement provided under subsection (1)
  shall contain information in sufficient detail to disclose accurately
  the financial condition and operations of the trade union or
  employers' organization for the fiscal year for which it was prepared.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaint to Board where failure to
  provide financial statement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Board, on the complaint of any member of a trade
  union or employers' organization that it has failed to comply with
  subsection (1), may make an order requiring the trade union or
  employers' organization to file with the Board, within the time set
  out in the order, a statement in such form and with such particulars
  as the Board may determine.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order of the Board

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Board may make an order requiring a trade union
  or employers' organization to provide a copy of a statement filed
  under subsection (3) to such members of the trade union or employers'
  organization as the Board in its discretion directs.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1977-78, c. 27, s. 70
- 1980-81-82-83, c. 47, s. 53(F)
- 1984, c. 40, s. 79(F)
:::

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-341059 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing to or by whom and in what manner any
  notice, request or report that may be given or made to or received by
  the Minister shall be given, made or received;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribing in what form and manner any notice or
  report that is authorized or required to be given or sent by the
  Minister, a conciliation commissioner, a conciliation board or an
  Industrial Inquiry Commission shall be given or sent and what shall
  constitute sufficient service of such notice or report on the person
  to whom it is given or sent;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} designating, with respect to any notice or request
  authorized or required to be given or sent by the Minister, the
  officer who may give or send the notice or request on behalf of the
  Minister;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the form and content of a notice to
  commence collective bargaining;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the form and content of a notice under
  section 71 and prescribing any additional information that is to be
  furnished with such a notice;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the form and content of a notice under
  section 87.2 and prescribing any additional information that is to be
  furnished with such a notice;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} prescribing a time limit for the purposes of
  subsection 99.01(1), as well as rules respecting the Board's
  jurisdiction, and the validity of a decision or order made by it,
  after the time limit;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1998, c. 26, s. 51\] ]{.Repealed}

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the form and content of any written
  request to the Minister under subsection 57(2) or (4) and prescribing
  any additional information that is to be furnished with such a
  request;

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the manner in which and the time within
  which a copy of an order or decision referred to in section 59 shall
  be filed with the Minister;

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the circumstances in which copies of
  orders and decisions filed with the Minister pursuant to section 59
  may be examined by members of the public and the fees, if any, to be
  charged for providing copies thereof; and

- [(l)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the manner in which a report of a
  conciliation commissioner or a conciliation board may be released by
  the Minister to the parties to a dispute pursuant to paragraph 77(a).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 111
- 1998, c. 26, s. 51
- [2024, c. 12, s. 13](#1460102-1532587){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1460102-1532587.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-111-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 111"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administrative monetary penalties

- **[[111.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make
  regulations establishing an administrative monetary penalties scheme
  for the purpose of promoting compliance with subsections 94(4) and
  (6), including regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} designating as a violation the contravention of
    subsection 94(4) or (6);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the administrative monetary penalties
    that may be imposed for a violation, including in relation to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the amount, or range of amounts, of the
      administrative monetary penalties that may be imposed on employers
      or classes of employers,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the factors to be taken into account in imposing
      an administrative monetary penalty,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the payment of administrative monetary
      penalties that have been imposed, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the recovery, as a debt, of unpaid
      administrative monetary penalties;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the persons or classes of persons who
    are considered a party to the violation and the amount, or range of
    amounts, of the administrative monetary penalties for which they are
    liable;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting what constitutes sufficient proof that a
    violation was committed;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting the powers, duties and functions of the
    Board and of any person or class of persons who may exercise powers
    or perform duties or functions with respect to the scheme, including
    the designation of such persons or classes of persons by the Board;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} respecting the proceedings in respect of a
    violation, including in relation to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} commencing the proceedings,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the defences that may be available in respect of
      a violation, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which the proceedings may
      be brought to an end; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} respecting reviews or appeals of any orders or
    decisions in the proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Violation or offence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an act or omission may be proceeded with as a
  violation or as an offence, proceeding with it in one manner precludes
  proceeding with it in the other.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 12, s. 14](#1460104-1532608){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1460104-1532608.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

**[[111.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111.1 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may
delegate to the head of the Federal Mediation and Conciliation Service
his or her powers of appointment under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 52
:::

#### [Miscellaneous]{.HTitleText3} {#h-341079 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents as evidence

- **[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  document purporting to contain or to be a copy of any order or
  decision of the Board and purporting to be signed by a member of the
  Board is admissible in any court in evidence without proof of the
  signature or official character of the person appearing to have signed
  the document and without further proof thereof.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate of Minister is evidence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A certificate purporting to be signed by the Minister
  or an official of the Federal Mediation and Conciliation Service
  stating that a report, request or notice was or was not received or
  given by the Minister pursuant to this Part and, if so received or
  given, stating the date on which it was so received or given, is
  admissible in any court in evidence without proof of the signature or
  official character of the person appearing to have signed the
  certificate and without further proof thereof.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 112
- 1998, c. 26, s. 53
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Late report not invalid

**[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** The failure of a
conciliation officer, conciliation commissioner or conciliation board to
report to the Minister within a period limited by this Part does not
invalidate the proceeding or terminate the authority of the conciliation
officer, conciliation commissioner or conciliation board.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1972, c. 18, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defect in form or irregularity

**[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114 .sectionLabel}** No proceeding under
this Part is invalid by reason only of a defect in form or a technical
irregularity.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1972, c. 18, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Collective agreement to be filed

**[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** Each party to a
collective agreement shall, forthwith on its execution, file one copy of
the collective agreement with the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1972, c. 18, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration and expenses

**[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** The members of an
Industrial Inquiry Commission, the members of a conciliation board and
every person not employed in the federal public administration who acts
as a conciliation officer or conciliation commissioner or who functions
under this Part in any other capacity at the request of the Minister,
except as an arbitrator or arbitration board chairperson, shall be paid
such remuneration and expenses as may be fixed by the Governor in
Council by regulation or by order.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 116
- 1998, c. 26, s. 59(E)
- 2003, c. 22, s. 224(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-116-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 116"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons deemed not to be employed in
public service

**[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117 .sectionLabel}** Unless the Governor in
Council otherwise orders in a case or class of cases, a person appointed
under this Part shall be deemed not to be employed in the public service
for the purposes of the [Public Service Superannuation
Act](/eng/acts/P-36).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 117
- 2003, c. 22, s. 225(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-117-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 117"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Witness fees and expenses

**[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** A person who is
summoned by the Board, a conciliation board, a conciliation commissioner
or an Industrial Inquiry Commission to attend as a witness in any
proceeding taken under this Part, and who so attends, is entitled to be
paid an allowance for expenses and a witness fee, determined in
accordance with the scale for the time being in force with respect to
witnesses in civil suits in the superior court of the province in which
the proceeding is being taken.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1972, c. 18, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Not required to give evidence --- Part I

- **[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  member of a conciliation board or no conciliation officer,
  conciliation commissioner, officer or employee employed in the federal
  public administration or person appointed by the Board or the Minister
  under this Part shall be required to give evidence in any civil
  action, suit or other proceeding respecting information obtained in
  the discharge of their duties under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Not required to give evidence --- Act

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} No member of the Board or no external adjudicator
  shall be required to give evidence in any civil action, suit or other
  proceeding respecting information obtained in the discharge of their
  duties under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief Administrator and employees not
  required to give evidence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Neither the Chief Administrator nor an employee of
  the Administrative Tribunals Support Service of Canada shall be
  required to give evidence in any civil action, suit or other
  proceeding respecting information obtained in the discharge of their
  duties in providing services to the Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 119
- 1999, c. 31, s. 162(E)
- 2003, c. 22, s. 224(E)
- 2014, c. 20, s. 420
- [2017, c. 20, s. 336](#1036875-1192588){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036875-1192588.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-119-20141101.html "Link to previous version of section 119"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No disclosure

**[[119.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119.1 .sectionLabel}** For greater
certainty, the following may not be disclosed without the consent of the
person who made them:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} notes or draft orders or decisions of the Board or
  any of its members, of an external adjudicator or of an arbitrator or
  arbitration board chairperson appointed by the Minister under this
  Part; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} notes or draft reports of persons appointed by the
  Minister under this Part to assist in resolving disputes or
  differences, or of persons authorized or designated by the Board to
  assist in resolving complaints or issues in dispute before the Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 26, s. 54
- [2017, c. 20, s. 337](#1036877-1192590){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036877-1192590.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-119.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 119.1"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Arrangements with Provinces]{.HTitleText3} {#h-341124 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where uniform provincial legislation

- **[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where this Part and legislation
  enacted by the legislature of a province are substantially uniform,
  the Minister may, on behalf of the Government of Canada, with the
  approval of the Governor in Council, enter into an agreement with the
  government of the province to provide for the administration of the
  legislation of the province by officers and employees employed in the
  federal public administration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement for administration by Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An agreement made pursuant to subsection (1) in
  respect of the administration of any legislation of a province may
  provide

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the administration by Canada of the legislation
    of the province with respect to any particular work, undertaking or
    business;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the Minister may, on behalf of the province,
    exercise the powers conferred or perform the duties imposed under
    the legislation of the province;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that the members of the Board, or officers and
    employees employed in the federal public administration, may
    exercise the powers conferred or perform the duties imposed under
    the legislation of the province; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} for payment by the government of the province to
    the Government of Canada for expenses incurred by the Government of
    Canada in the administration of the legislation of the province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where powers or duties conferred by
  provincial legislation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where an agreement has been entered into between the
  Government of Canada and the government of a province in respect of
  any legislation of the province, the Minister, the members of the
  Board and any officers or employees employed in the federal public
  administration may, if the legislation so provides and the Governor in
  Council so orders, exercise the powers and perform the duties
  specified in the legislation or agreement.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 120
- 2003, c. 22, s. 224(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-120-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 120"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Annual Reports]{.HTitleText3} {#h-341138 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual report of Board

- **[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board shall, on or before January 31 next following the end of each
  fiscal year, submit to the Minister a report on the activities of the
  Board during the immediately preceding fiscal year and the Minister
  shall cause the report to be laid before Parliament within fifteen
  days after the receipt thereof or, if Parliament is not then sitting,
  on any of the first fifteen days next thereafter that either House of
  Parliament is sitting.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1996, c. 11, s. 65\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 121
- 1996, c. 11, s. 65
:::

#### [Application of Provincial Laws]{.HTitleText3} {#h-341145 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provincial Crown corporations

**[[121.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121.1 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may by regulation direct that this Part applies in respect of
any employment, or any class or classes of employment, on or in
connection with a work or undertaking set out in the regulation that is,
or is part of, a corporation that is an agent of Her Majesty in right of
a province and whose activities are regulated, in whole or in part,
pursuant to the [Nuclear Safety and Control Act](/eng/acts/N-28.3).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 12, s. 1
- 1997, c. 9, s. 125
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusion from application

- **[[121.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may by
  regulation exclude, in whole or in part, from the application of any
  of the provisions of this Part any employment, or any class or classes
  of employment, on or in connection with a work or undertaking set out
  in the regulation whose activities are regulated, in whole or in part,
  pursuant to the [Nuclear Safety and Control Act](/eng/acts/N-28.3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On the recommendation of the Minister, the Governor
  in Council may make regulations respecting any matter relating to
  industrial relations, including the prevention of a work stoppage or
  the continuation or resumption of operations, in relation to
  employment that is subject to a regulation made pursuant to subsection
  (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporation of provincial law

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A regulation made under subsection (2) incorporating
  by reference, in whole or in part, an Act of the legislature of a
  province or an instrument made under such an Act may incorporate the
  Act or instrument as amended to a certain date or from time to time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of regulation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A regulation made under subsection (2) may apply

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} generally, with respect to all employment that is
    subject to a regulation made pursuant to subsection (1); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to any class or classes of employment that are
    subject to a regulation made pursuant to subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administration and enforcement

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A regulation made under subsection (2) incorporating
  an Act or instrument shall, after consultation by the Minister with
  the appropriate provincial minister, be administered and enforced by
  the person or authority that is responsible for the administration and
  enforcement of the Act or instrument.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and penalty

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (7), every person who
  contravenes a regulation made under subsection (2) by contravening a
  provision of an Act of the legislature of a province that, or an
  instrument made under such an Act that, is incorporated by the
  regulation is guilty of an offence against this Act and liable to the
  same punishment as is imposed by or under an Act of that legislature
  for the contravention of that provision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defence

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} No person may be convicted of an offence or subjected
  to a punishment for a contravention described in subsection (6) unless
  it is proved that, at the time of the alleged contravention,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the incorporated Act or instrument was reasonably
    accessible to the person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reasonable steps had been taken to ensure that the
    incorporated Act or instrument was accessible to persons likely to
    be affected by the regulation; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the incorporated Act or instrument had been
    published in the official gazette of the province or as otherwise
    authorized by the legislature of the province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The prosecution of a contravention described in
  subsection (6) shall be commenced by the attorney general of the
  province in which the offence was committed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 12, s. 1
- 1997, c. 9, s. 125
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of [Statutory Instruments
Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

**[[121.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121.3 .sectionLabel}** Subsection 5(1) of
the [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22) does not apply to a
regulation made pursuant to section 121.1 or 121.2.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 12, s. 1
:::

Definition of [[regulation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- **[[121.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this section,
  [[regulation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a regulation made under
  subsection 121.2(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Bargaining agents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A bargaining agent that represents a bargaining unit
  immediately before the time when a regulation is made to which the
  employees in the bargaining unit are subject continues, at that time,
  to represent the bargaining unit for the purposes of the application
  of the regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Collective agreements continued

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every collective agreement that is in force
  immediately before the time when a regulation is made that applies to
  employees who are subject to the collective agreement continues in
  force, at that time, under the regulation until its term expires.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to bargain

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A notice to bargain given under this Part is deemed,
  at the time when a regulation is made to which the employees who are
  affected by the notice to bargain are subject, to have been given
  pursuant to the regulation on the day on which it was given.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acquired rights, etc.

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Any rights, privileges or duties acquired under this
  Part by the bargaining unit, bargaining agent, employer or employees
  before the time when a regulation is made are deemed to have been
  acquired pursuant to the regulation on the day on which they were
  acquired.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decisions

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A person or authority that, under an Act of the
  legislature of a province, is competent to decide a matter that is
  contemplated by this section in relation to a provision of an Act of
  the legislature of a province or an instrument made under such an Act
  may, on application by the employer or bargaining agent or, where the
  person or authority considers it appropriate, an employee, decide any
  matter that is contemplated by this section in relation to a
  regulation incorporating that provision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 12, s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[121.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121.5 .sectionLabel}** Notwithstanding
section 121.4, the Governor in Council may make regulations respecting
any matter referred to in that section in relation to a regulation made
under subsection 121.2(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 12, s. 1
:::

## [PART II]{.HLabel1}[Occupational Health and Safety]{.HTitleText1} {#h-341197 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-341198 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this Part,

  [[appeals officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[appeals officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2017, c.
      20, s. 338\]]{.Repealed}

  [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2017, c. 20, s.
      338\]]{.Repealed}

  [[collective agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[collective agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning
      as in section 166; ([convention collective]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[danger]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[danger]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any hazard, condition or
      activity that could reasonably be expected to be an imminent or
      serious threat to the life or health of a person exposed to it
      before the hazard or condition can be corrected or the activity
      altered; ([danger]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person employed by an
      employer; ([employé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[employer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[employer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who employs one or
      more employees and includes an employers' organization and any
      person who acts on behalf of an
      employer; ([employeur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[harassment and violence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[harassment and violence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any action,
      conduct or comment, including of a sexual nature, that can
      reasonably be expected to cause offence, humiliation or other
      physical or psychological injury or illness to an employee,
      including any prescribed action, conduct or comment; ([harcèlement
      et violence]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[hazardous substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[hazardous substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a hazardous
      product and a chemical, biological or physical agent that, by
      reason of a property that the agent possesses, is hazardous to the
      safety or health of a person exposed to it; ([substance
      dangereuse]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[health and safety officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[health and safety officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
      2013, c. 40, s. 176\]]{.Repealed}

  [[health and safety representative]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[health and safety representative]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
      person who is appointed as a health and safety representative
      under section 136; ([représentant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[policy committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[policy committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a policy health and
      safety committee established under section 134.1; ([comité
      d'orientation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[prescribe]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[prescribe]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means prescribe by regulation of
      the Governor in Council or determine in accordance with rules
      prescribed by regulation of the Governor in
      Council; ([règlement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[regional health and safety officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[regional health and safety
      officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2013, c. 40, s.
      176\]]{.Repealed}

  [[regional safety officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[regional safety officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
      2000, c. 20, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

  [[safety]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[safety]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means protection from danger and
      hazards arising out of, linked with or occurring in the course of
      employment; ([sécurité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[safety and health committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[safety and health committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed,
      2000, c. 20, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

  [[safety and health representative]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[safety and health
      representative]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2000, c. 20, s.
      2\]]{.Repealed}

  [[safety officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[safety officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2000, c. 20, s.
      2\]]{.Repealed}

  [[work place]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[work place]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any place where an
      employee is engaged in work for the employee's employer; ([lieu de
      travail]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[work place committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[work place committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a work place
      health and safety committee established under section
      135. ([comité local]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In this Part, [[hazardous
  product]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, [[label]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} and
  [[safety data sheet]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} have the same meanings as in
  section 2 of the [Hazardous Products Act](/eng/acts/H-3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Except where otherwise provided in this Part, all
  other words and expressions have the same meanings as in Part I.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 122
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 1, c. 24 (3rd Supp.), s. 3
- 1993, c. 42, s. 3
- 1998, c. 26, s. 55
- 2000, c. 20, s. 2
- 2013, c. 40, s. 176
- 2014, c. 20, s. 139
- [2017, c. 20, s. 338](#1036882-1192593){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036882-1192593.html"}
- [2018, c. 22, s. 0.1](#1038758-1281730){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1038758-1281730.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-122-20190729.html "Link to previous version of section 122"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Purpose of Part]{.HTitleText2} {#h-341227 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prevention of accidents, injuries and
illnesses

**[[122.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122.1 .sectionLabel}** The purpose of this
Part is to prevent accidents, occurrences of harassment and violence and
physical or psychological injuries and illnesses arising out of, linked
with or occurring in the course of employment to which this Part
applies.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 1
- [2018, c. 22, s. 1](#1038760-1281733){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1038760-1281733.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-122.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 122.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preventive measures

**[[122.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122.2 .sectionLabel}** Preventive measures
should consist first of the elimination of hazards, then the reduction
of hazards and finally, the provision of personal protective equipment,
clothing, devices or materials, all with the goal of ensuring the health
and safety of employees.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2000, c. 20, s. 3
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Head of Compliance and Enforcement

- **[[122.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122.21
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may designate a person
  as Head of Compliance and Enforcement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No designation made

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If no Head is designated under subsection (1), the
  Minister shall exercise the powers and perform the duties and
  functions of the Head.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 536](#1040151-1281912){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040151-1281912.html"}
:::

### [Methods of Communication]{.HTitleText2} {#h-341236 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights of employees

- **[[122.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employee with a special need
  shall be given any direction, notice, information, instruction or
  training that is required to be given to employees under this Part by
  any method of communication that readily permits the employee to
  receive it, including braille, large print, audio tape, computer disk,
  sign language and verbal communication.

- Meaning of [[special need]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, an employee has a
  special need if the employee is affected by a condition that impairs
  their ability to receive any direction, notice, information,
  instruction or training given by a method that would otherwise be
  sufficient under this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2000, c. 20, s. 3
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-341244 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of Part

- **[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding any other Act of
  Parliament or any regulations thereunder, this Part applies to and in
  respect of employment

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on or in connection with the operation of any
    federal work, undertaking or business other than a work, undertaking
    or business of a local or private nature in Yukon, the Northwest
    Territories or Nunavut;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by a corporation established to perform any
    function or duty on behalf of the Government of Canada; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} by a Canadian carrier, as defined in section 2 of
    the [Telecommunications Act](/eng/acts/T-3.4), that is an agent of
    Her Majesty in right of a province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to federal public
  administration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This Part applies to the federal public
  administration and to persons employed in the federal public
  administration to the extent provided for under Part 3 of the [Federal
  Public Sector Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons appointed and their employer

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} This Part applies to persons appointed under
  subsection 128(1) of the [Public Service Employment
  Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01), other than persons appointed by a person
  holding the recognized position of Leader of the Opposition in the
  Senate or Leader of the Opposition in the House of Commons, and to
  their employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to other persons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} This Part applies to any person who is not an
  employee but who performs for an employer to which this Part applies
  activities whose primary purpose is to enable the person to acquire
  knowledge or experience, and to the employer, as if that person were
  an employee of the employer, and every provision of this Part must be
  read accordingly.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 123
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 2
- 1993, c. 28, s. 78, c. 38, s. 89
- 2000, c. 20, s. 4
- 2002, c. 7, s. 97(E)
- 2003, c. 22, s. 110
- 2015, c. 36, s. 87
- 2017, c. 9, s. 55
- [2018, c. 22, s. 2](#1038764-1281736){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1038764-1281736.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-123-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 123"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Canadian Human Rights
Act](/eng/acts/H-6)

**[[123.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123.1 .sectionLabel}** For greater
certainty, nothing in this Part shall be construed so as to abrogate or
derogate from the rights provided for under the [Canadian Human Rights
Act](/eng/acts/H-6).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 3
- [1996, c. 12, s. 2](#1038766-1281741){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1038766-1281741.html"}
- [2018, c. 22, s. 2.1](#1038766-1281742){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1038766-1281742.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-123.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 123.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presumption

- **[[123.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person who is paid remuneration by
  an employer is presumed to be their employee unless the contrary is
  proved by the employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The presumption does not apply for the purposes of a
  prosecution under this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 17, s. 239](#1467808-1481295){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1467808-1481295.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

**[[123.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123.3 .sectionLabel}** If, in any
proceeding under this Part other than a prosecution, or in any
proceeding under Part IV in respect of a violation that is related to
this Part, an employer alleges that a person is not their employee, the
burden of proof is on the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 17, s. 239](#1467808-1481299){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1467808-1481299.html"}
:::

### [Duties of Employers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-341258 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General duty of employer

**[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** Every employer shall
ensure that the health and safety at work of every person employed by
the employer is protected.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 124
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 4
- 2000, c. 20, s. 5
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Specific duties of employer

- **[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Without restricting the generality
  of section 124, every employer shall, in respect of every work place
  controlled by the employer and, in respect of every work activity
  carried out by an employee in a work place that is not controlled by
  the employer, to the extent that the employer controls the activity,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that all permanent and temporary buildings
    and structures meet the prescribed standards;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} install guards, guard-rails, barricades and fences
    in accordance with prescribed standards;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} except as provided for in the regulations,
    investigate, record and report, in accordance with the regulations,
    all accidents, occurrences of harassment and violence, occupational
    illnesses and other hazardous occurrences known to the employer;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} make readily available to employees, in printed and
    electronic form,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a copy of this Part and a copy of the regulations
      made under this Part that apply to the work place,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a statement of the employer's general policy
      concerning the health and safety at work of employees, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any other information related to health and
      safety that is prescribed or that may be specified by the Head;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 22, s. 3\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} if the information referred to in any of
    subparagraphs (d)(i) to (iii) is made available in electronic form,
    ensure that employees receive appropriate training to enable them to
    have access to the information and, on the request of an employee,
    make a printed copy of the information available;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} keep and maintain in prescribed form and manner
    prescribed health and safety records;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} provide prescribed first-aid facilities and health
    services;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} provide prescribed sanitary and personal
    facilities;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} provide, in accordance with prescribed standards,
    potable water;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the vehicles and mobile equipment used
    by the employees in the course of their employment meet prescribed
    standards;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} provide every person granted access to the work
    place by the employer with prescribed safety materials, equipment,
    devices and clothing;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the use, operation and maintenance of
    the following are in accordance with prescribed standards:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} boilers and pressure vessels,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} escalators, elevators and other devices for
      moving persons or freight,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} all equipment for the generation, distribution
      or use of electricity,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} gas or oil burning equipment or other heat
      generating equipment, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} heating, ventilation and air-conditioning
      systems;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the levels of ventilation, lighting,
    temperature, humidity, sound and vibration are in accordance with
    prescribed standards;

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} comply with prescribed standards relating to fire
    safety and emergency measures;

  - [(p)]{.lawlabel} ensure, in the prescribed manner, that employees
    have safe entry to, exit from and occupancy of the work place;

  - [(q)]{.lawlabel} provide, in the prescribed manner, each employee
    with the information, instruction, training and supervision
    necessary to ensure their health and safety at work;

  - [(r)]{.lawlabel} maintain all installed guards, guard-rails,
    barricades and fences in accordance with prescribed standards;

  - [(s)]{.lawlabel} ensure that each employee is made aware of every
    known or foreseeable health or safety hazard in the area where the
    employee works;

  - [(t)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the machinery, equipment and tools used
    by the employees in the course of their employment meet prescribed
    health, safety and ergonomic standards and are safe under all
    conditions of their intended use;

  - [(u)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the work place, work spaces and
    procedures meet prescribed ergonomic standards;

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} adopt and implement prescribed safety codes and
    safety standards;

  - [(w)]{.lawlabel} ensure that every person granted access to the work
    place by the employer is familiar with and uses in the prescribed
    circumstances and manner all prescribed safety materials, equipment,
    devices and clothing;

  - [(x)]{.lawlabel} comply with every oral or written direction given
    to the employer by the Head or the Board concerning the health and
    safety of employees;

  - [(y)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the activities of every person granted
    access to the work place do not endanger the health and safety of
    employees;

  - [(z)]{.lawlabel} ensure that employees who have supervisory or
    managerial responsibilities are adequately trained in health and
    safety and are informed of the responsibilities they have under this
    Part where they act on behalf of their employer;

  - [(z.01)]{.lawlabel} ensure that members of policy and work place
    committees and health and safety representatives receive the
    prescribed training in health and safety and are informed of their
    responsibilities under this Part;

  - [(z.02)]{.lawlabel} respond as soon as possible to reports made by
    employees under paragraph 126(1)(g);

  - [(z.03)]{.lawlabel} develop, implement and monitor, in consultation
    with the policy committee or, if there is no policy committee, with
    the work place committee or the health and safety representative, a
    prescribed program for the prevention of hazards in the work place
    appropriate to its size and the nature of the hazards in it that
    also provides for the education of employees in health and safety
    matters;

  - [(z.04)]{.lawlabel} where the program referred to in paragraph
    (z.03) does not cover certain hazards unique to a work place,
    develop, implement and monitor, in consultation with the work place
    committee or the health and safety representative, a prescribed
    program for the prevention of those hazards that also provides for
    the education of employees in health and safety matters related to
    those hazards;

  - [(z.05)]{.lawlabel} consult the policy committee or, if there is no
    policy committee, the work place committee or the health and safety
    representative to plan the implementation of changes that might
    affect occupational health and safety, including work processes and
    procedures;

  - [(z.06)]{.lawlabel} consult the work place committee or the health
    and safety representative in the implementation of changes that
    might affect occupational health and safety, including work
    processes and procedures;

  - [(z.07)]{.lawlabel} ensure the availability in the work place of
    premises, equipment and personnel necessary for the operation of the
    policy and work place committees;

  - [(z.08)]{.lawlabel} cooperate with the policy and work place
    committees or the health and safety representative in the execution
    of their duties under this Part;

  - [(z.09)]{.lawlabel} develop health and safety policies and programs
    in consultation with the policy committee or, if there is no policy
    committee, with the work place committee or the health and safety
    representative;

  - [(z.10)]{.lawlabel} respond in writing to recommendations made by
    the policy and work place committees or the health and safety
    representative within thirty days after receiving them, indicating
    what, if any, action will be taken and when it will be taken;

  - [(z.11)]{.lawlabel} provide to the policy committee, if any, and to
    the work place committee or the health and safety representative, a
    copy of any report on hazards in the work place, including an
    assessment of those hazards;

  - [(z.12)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the work place committee or the
    health and safety representative inspects each month all or part of
    the work place, so that every part of the work place is inspected at
    least once each year;

  - [(z.13)]{.lawlabel} when necessary, develop, implement and monitor a
    program for the provision of personal protective equipment,
    clothing, devices or materials, in consultation, except in
    emergencies, with the policy committee or, if there is no policy
    committee, with the work place committee or the health and safety
    representative;

  - [(z.14)]{.lawlabel} take all reasonable care to ensure that all of
    the persons granted access to the work place, other than the
    employer's employees, are informed of every known or foreseeable
    health or safety hazard to which they are likely to be exposed in
    the work place;

  - [(z.15)]{.lawlabel} meet with the health and safety representative
    as necessary to address health and safety matters;

  - [(z.16)]{.lawlabel} take the prescribed measures to prevent and
    protect against harassment and violence in the work place, respond
    to occurrences of harassment and violence in the work place and
    offer support to employees affected by harassment and violence in
    the work place;

  - [(z.161)]{.lawlabel} ensure that employees, including those who have
    supervisory or managerial responsibilities, receive training in the
    prevention of harassment and violence in the work place and are
    informed of their rights and obligations under this Part in relation
    to harassment and violence;

  - [(z.162)]{.lawlabel} undergo training in the prevention of
    harassment and violence in the work place;

  - [(z.163)]{.lawlabel} ensure that the person designated by the
    employer to receive complaints relating to occurrences of harassment
    and violence has knowledge, training and experience in issues
    relating to harassment and violence and has knowledge of relevant
    legislation;

  - [(z.17)]{.lawlabel} post and keep posted, in a conspicuous place or
    places where they are likely to come to the attention of employees,
    the names, work place telephone numbers and work locations of all of
    the members of work place committees or of the health and safety
    representative;

  - [(z.18)]{.lawlabel} provide, within thirty days after receiving a
    request, or as soon as possible after that, the information
    requested from the employer by a policy committee under subsection
    134.1(5) or (6), by a work place committee under subsection 135(8)
    or (9) or by a health and safety representative under subsection
    136(6) or (7); and

  - [(z.19)]{.lawlabel} consult with the work place committee or the
    health and safety representative on the implementation and
    monitoring of programs developed in consultation with the policy
    committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(z.17) does not apply to an employer who
  controls

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a single work place at which fewer than twenty
    employees are normally employed, if all of those employees and the
    health and safety representative normally work at the same time and
    in the same location; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a single work place at which only one employee is
    normally employed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations
  respecting the investigations, records and reports referred to in
  paragraph (1)(c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Former employees

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Except as provided for in the regulations, the
  obligations set out in paragraphs (1)(c) and (z.16) apply to an
  employer in respect of a former employee in relation to an occurrence
  of harassment and violence in the work place if the occurrence becomes
  known to the employer within three months after the day on which the
  former employee ceases to be employed by the employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On application by a former employee, the Head may, in
  the prescribed circumstances, extend the time period referred to in
  subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations --- former employees

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (4), the Governor in
  Council may make regulations respecting an employer's obligations in
  respect of former employees.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 125
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 4, c. 24 (3rd Supp.), s. 4

  ajax-after="1040158-1281915.html"}

  ajax-after="1040775-1282445.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further specific duties of employer

**[[125.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125.1 .sectionLabel}** Without restricting
the generality of section 124 or limiting the duties of an employer
under section 125 but subject to any exceptions that may be prescribed,
every employer shall, in respect of every work place controlled by the
employer and, in respect of every work activity carried out by an
employee in a work place that is not controlled by the employer, to the
extent that the employer controls the activity,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that concentrations of hazardous substances in
  the work place are controlled in accordance with prescribed standards;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that all hazardous substances in the work
  place are stored and handled in the manner prescribed;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensure that all hazardous substances in the work
  place, other than hazardous products, are identified in the manner
  prescribed;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} subject to the [Hazardous Materials Information
  Review Act](/eng/acts/H-2.7), ensure that each hazardous product in
  the work place or each container in the work place in which a
  hazardous product is contained has affixed to it, printed on it,
  attached to it or otherwise applied to it a label that meets the
  prescribed requirements;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} subject to the [Hazardous Materials Information
  Review Act](/eng/acts/H-2.7), make available to every employee, in the
  prescribed manner, a safety data sheet for each hazardous product to
  which the employee may be exposed that meets the requirements set out
  in the regulations made under subsection 15(1) of the [Hazardous
  Products Act](/eng/acts/H-3);

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} where employees may be exposed to hazardous
  substances, investigate and assess the exposure in the manner
  prescribed, with the assistance of the work place committee or the
  health and safety representative; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} ensure that all records of exposure to hazardous
  substances are kept and maintained in the prescribed manner and that
  personal records of exposure are made available to the affected
  employees.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 24 (3rd Supp.), s. 5
- 1993, c. 42, s. 5(F)
- 2000, c. 20, s. 6
- 2014, c. 20, s. 140
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-125.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 125.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer to provide information in
emergency

- **[[125.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall, in respect of
  every work place controlled by the employer and, in respect of every
  work activity carried out by an employee in a work place that is not
  controlled by the employer, to the extent that the employer controls
  that activity, provide, in respect of any hazardous product to which
  an employee may be exposed, as soon as is practicable in the
  circumstances, any information that is included in the safety data
  sheet that is in the employer's possession for the hazardous product
  to any physician or other prescribed medical professional who requests
  that information for the purpose of making a medical diagnosis of, or
  rendering medical treatment to, an employee in an emergency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information to be kept confidential

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any physician or other prescribed medical
  professional to whom information is provided by an employer pursuant
  to subsection (1) shall keep confidential any information specified by
  the employer as being confidential, except for the purpose for which
  it is provided.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 24 (3rd Supp.), s. 5
- 2000, c. 20, s. 7
- 2014, c. 20, s. 141
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-125.2-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 125.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Coal mines

- **[[125.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer of employees employed
  in a coal mine shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} comply with every condition imposed on the employer
    pursuant to paragraph 137.2(2)(b) or (3)(a);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} comply with every provision substituted for a
    provision of the regulations, in respect of the employer, pursuant
    to paragraph 137.2(3)(b);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} permit inspections and tests to be carried out on
    behalf of the employees, in any part of the mine and on any
    machinery or equipment therein, in the prescribed manner and at
    intervals not greater than the prescribed interval; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} as a condition of carrying out any activity for
    which the submission of plans and procedures is prescribed, submit
    to the Coal Mining Safety Commission for approval, in the form and
    manner and at the time prescribed, plans and procedures relating to
    that activity and carry out the activity in conformity with plans
    and procedures as approved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Methods, machinery and equipment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No employer shall require or permit the use in a coal
  mine of any mining method, machinery or equipment in respect of which
  no prescribed safety standards are applicable unless the use thereof
  has been approved pursuant to paragraph 137.2(2)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Searches

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every employer of employees employed in a coal mine
  shall, at intervals not greater than the prescribed interval, for the
  purpose of preventing alcohol, articles for use in smoking and drugs,
  other than drugs exempted by the regulations, from being brought into
  the mine,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} require every person entering an underground
    portion of the mine who is not employed there to submit to a
    personal search conducted in the prescribed manner; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} require a proportion, not less than the prescribed
    proportion, of employees employed in the underground portions of the
    mine to submit to personal searches conducted in the prescribed
    manner.

- Definition of [[coal mine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section and section 137.2,
  [[coal mine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes any work place above ground
  that is used in the operation of the mine and is under the control of
  the employer of employees employed in the mine.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 26 (4th Supp.), s. 1
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[125.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125.4 .sectionLabel}** An employer is
prohibited from treating an employee as if they were not their employee.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 17, s. 240](#1467810-1481303){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1467810-1481303.html"}
:::

### [Duties of Employees]{.HTitleText2} {#h-341360 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Health and safety matters

- **[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} While at work, every employee shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} use any safety materials, equipment, devices and
    clothing that are intended for the employee's protection and
    furnished to the employee by the employer or that are prescribed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} follow prescribed procedures with respect to the
    health and safety of employees;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} take all reasonable and necessary precautions to
    ensure the health and safety of the employee, the other employees
    and any person likely to be affected by the employee's acts or
    omissions;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} comply with all instructions from the employer
    concerning the health and safety of employees;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} cooperate with any person carrying out a duty
    imposed under this Part;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} cooperate with the policy and work place committees
    or the health and safety representative;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} report to the employer any thing or circumstance in
    a work place that is likely to be hazardous to the health or safety
    of the employee, or that of the other employees or other persons
    granted access to the work place by the employer;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} report in the prescribed manner every accident or
    other occurrence arising in the course of or in connection with the
    employee's work that has caused injury to the employee or to any
    other person;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} comply with every oral or written direction of the
    Head or the Board concerning the health and safety of employees; and

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} report to the employer any situation that the
    employee believes to be a contravention of this Part by the
    employer, another employee or any other person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No relief of employer's duties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsection (1) relieves an employer from
  any duty imposed on the employer under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation of liability

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No employee is personally liable for anything done or
  omitted to be done in good faith by the employee when the employee is
  assisting the employer, as requested by the employer, in providing
  first-aid or in carrying out any other emergency measures.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 126
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 4
- 1993, c. 42, s. 6(F)
- 2000, c. 20, s. 8
- 2013, c. 40, s. 178
- [2017, c. 20, s. 340](#1036887-1192597){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036887-1192597.html"}
- [2018, c. 22, s. 4(F)](#1038798-1281763){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1038798-1281763.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 538](#1040163-1281923){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040163-1281923.html"}
:::

### [Employment Safety]{.HTitleText2} {#h-341380 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interference at accident scene prohibited

- **[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), if an
  employee is killed or seriously injured in a work place, no person
  shall, unless authorized to do so by the Head, remove or in any way
  interfere with or disturb any wreckage, article or thing related to
  the incident except to the extent necessary to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} save a life, prevent injury or relieve human
    suffering in the vicinity;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} maintain an essential public service; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} prevent unnecessary damage to or loss of property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No authorization referred to in subsection (1) is
  required where an employee is killed or seriously injured by an
  accident or incident involving

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an aircraft, a vessel, rolling stock or a pipeline,
    if the accident or incident is being investigated under the
    [Aeronautics Act](/eng/acts/A-2), the [Canada Shipping Act,
    2001](/eng/acts/C-10.15) or the [Canadian Transportation Accident
    Investigation and Safety Board Act](/eng/acts/C-23.4); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a motor vehicle on a public highway.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 127
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 4
- 1989, c. 3, s. 45
- 1996, c. 10, s. 235
- 1998, c. 20, s. 29
- 2000, c. 20, s. 9
- 2001, c. 26, s. 305
- 2013, c. 40, s. 179
- [2018, c. 27, s. 539](#1040165-1281925){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040165-1281925.html"}
:::

### [Internal Complaint Resolution Process]{.HTitleText2} {#h-341393 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaint to supervisor

- **[[127.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employee who believes on
  reasonable grounds that there has been a contravention of this Part or
  that there is likely to be an accident, injury or illness arising out
  of, linked with or occurring in the course of employment shall, before
  exercising any other recourse available under this Part, except the
  rights conferred by subsection (8.1) and sections 128, 129 and 132,
  make a complaint to the employee's supervisor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Supervisor or designated person

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} However, in the case of a complaint relating to an
  occurrence of harassment and violence, the employee may make the
  complaint to the employee's supervisor or to the person designated in
  the employer's work place harassment and violence prevention policy.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oral or written complaint

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The complaint may be made orally or in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resolve complaint

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employee and the supervisor or designated person,
  as the case may be, shall try to resolve the complaint between
  themselves as soon as possible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Investigation of complaint

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employee or the supervisor may refer an
  unresolved complaint, other than a complaint relating to an occurrence
  of harassment and violence, to a chairperson of the work place
  committee or to the health and safety representative to be
  investigated jointly

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by an employee member and an employer member of the
    work place committee; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} by the health and safety representative and a
    person designated by the employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The persons who investigate the complaint shall
  inform the employee and the employer in writing, in the form and
  manner prescribed if any is prescribed, of the results of the
  investigation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommendations

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The persons who investigate a complaint may make
  recommendations to the employer with respect to the situation that
  gave rise to the complaint, whether or not they conclude that the
  complaint is justified.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer's duty

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the persons who investigate the complaint conclude
  that the complaint is justified, the employer, on being informed of
  the results of the investigation, shall in writing and without delay
  inform the persons who investigated the complaint of how and when the
  employer will resolve the matter, and the employer shall resolve the
  matter accordingly.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2013, c. 40, s. 180\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral to the Head

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The employee or employer may refer a complaint that
  there has been a contravention of this Part to the Head in the
  following circumstances:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the employer does not agree with the results
    of the investigation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the employer has failed to inform the persons
    who investigated the complaint of how and when the employer intends
    to resolve the matter or has failed to take action to resolve the
    matter;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the persons who investigated the complaint do
    not agree between themselves as to whether the complaint is
    justified; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a complaint relating to an
    occurrence of harassment and violence, the employee and the
    supervisor or designated person, as the case may be, failed to
    resolve the complaint between themselves.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaint to Head

  [(8.1)]{.lawlabel} An employee may make a complaint in writing to the
  Head if they believe that the employer has contravened section 125.4.
  The complaint must be made not later than six months after the day on
  which the complainant knew, or in the opinion of the Head ought to
  have known, of the action or circumstances giving rise to the
  complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Investigation

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The Head shall investigate the complaint referred to
  in subsection (8) or (8.1) unless it relates to an occurrence of
  harassment and violence and the Head is of the opinion that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the complaint has been adequately dealt with
    according to a procedure provided for under this Act, any other Act
    of Parliament or a collective agreement; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the matter is otherwise an abuse of process.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(9.‍1)]{.lawlabel} If the Head is of the opinion that the conditions
  described in paragraph (9)‍(a) or (b) are met, the Head shall inform
  the employer and the employee in writing, as soon as feasible, that
  the Head will not investigate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Combining investigations --- harassment
  and violence

  [(9.2)]{.lawlabel} The Head may combine an investigation into a
  complaint relating to an occurrence of harassment and violence with an
  ongoing investigation relating to the same employer and involving
  substantially the same issues and, in that case, the Head may issue a
  single decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty and power of Head

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} On completion of the investigation, the Head

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} may issue directions to an employer or employee
    under subsection 145(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may, if in the Head's opinion it is appropriate,
    recommend that the employee and employer resolve the matter between
    themselves; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} shall, if the Head concludes that a danger exists
    as described in subsection 128(1), issue directions under subsection
    145(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, nothing in this section
  limits the Head's authority under section 145.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Former employees

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} A former employee may, within the prescribed time,
  make a complaint under subsection (1) relating to an occurrence of
  harassment and violence in the work place, in which case this Part
  applies to the former employee and to the employer as if the former
  employee were an employee, to the extent necessary to finally dispose
  of the complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} On application by a former employee, the Head may,
  in the prescribed circumstances, extend the time period referred to in
  subsection (12).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2000, c. 20, s. 10
- 2013, c. 40, s. 180
