### [DIVISION I]{.HLabel2}[Hours of Work]{.HTitleText2} {#h-342197 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Standard hours of work

- **[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except as otherwise provided by or
  under this Division

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the standard hours of work of an employee shall not
    exceed eight hours in a day and forty hours in a week; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} no employer shall cause or permit an employee to
    work longer hours than eight hours in any day or forty hours in any
    week.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Averaging

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the nature of the work in an industrial
  establishment necessitates irregular distribution of the hours of work
  of an employee, the hours of work in a day and the hours of work in a
  week may be calculated, in such manner and in such circumstances as
  may be prescribed by the regulations, as an average for a period of
  two or more weeks.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of averaging

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The averaged hours of work calculated pursuant to
  subsection (2) remain in effect

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the averaging of hours of work is agreed to
    in writing by an employer and a trade union, for the duration of
    that agreement or for such shorter period as is agreed to by the
    parties; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the averaging of hours of work is not agreed
    to in writing by an employer and a trade union, for no longer than
    three years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General holidays in week

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In a week in which one or more general holidays occur
  that under Division V entitle an employee to holidays with pay in that
  week, the hours of work of the employee in that week shall be reduced
  by the standard hours of work for each general holiday in that week
  and, for the purposes of this subsection, in calculating the time
  worked by an employee in any such week, no account shall be taken of
  any time worked by the employee on the holidays or of any time during
  which the employee was at the disposal of his employer during the
  holidays.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 169
- 1993, c. 42, s. 14
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Break

- **[[169.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee is entitled to and
  shall be granted an unpaid break of at least 30 minutes during every
  period of five consecutive hours of work. If the employer requires the
  employee to be at their disposal during the break period, the employee
  must be paid for the break.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer may postpone or cancel the break set out
  in subsection (1) if it is necessary for the employee to work in order
  to deal with a situation that the employer could not have reasonably
  foreseen and that presents or could reasonably be expected to present
  an imminent or serious

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} threat to the life, health or safety of any person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} threat of damage to or loss of property; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} threat of serious interference with the ordinary
    working of the employer's industrial establishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 444](#1039739-1200969){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039739-1200969.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rest period

- **[[169.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee is entitled to and
  shall be granted a rest period of at least eight consecutive hours
  between work periods or shifts.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), an employer may require that
  an employee work additional hours to their scheduled work periods or
  shifts which would result in them having a rest period of fewer than
  eight consecutive hours between their work periods or shifts if it is
  necessary for the employee to work in order to deal with a situation
  that the employer could not have reasonably foreseen and that presents
  or could reasonably be expected to present an imminent or serious

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} threat to the life, health or safety of any person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} threat of damage to or loss of property; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} threat of serious interference with the ordinary
    working of the employer's industrial establishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 444](#1039739-1200979){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039739-1200979.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Modified work schedule --- collective
agreement

- **[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employer may, in respect of one or more employees subject to a
  collective agreement, establish, modify or cancel a work schedule
  under which the hours exceed the standard hours of work set out in
  paragraph 169(1)(a) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the average hours of work for a period of two or
    more weeks does not exceed forty hours a week; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the schedule, or its modification or cancellation,
    is agreed to in writing by the employer and the trade union.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Modified work schedule

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), an employer may, in
  respect of one or more employees not subject to a collective
  agreement, establish, modify or cancel a work schedule under which the
  hours exceed the standard hours of work set out in paragraph 169(1)(a)
  if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the average hours of work for a period of two or
    more weeks does not exceed forty hours a week; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the schedule, or its modification or cancellation,
    has been approved

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of one employee's schedule, in
      writing by that employee, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of more than one employee's
      schedule, by at least 70% of the affected employees.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Posting of notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a work schedule is to be established, modified
  or cancelled pursuant to subsection (2), the employer shall post a
  notice of the new schedule, or of its modification or cancellation, in
  readily accessible places where it is likely to be seen by the
  affected employees, for at least thirty days before the new schedule
  or its modification or cancellation takes effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (3) does not apply to the establishment,
  modification or cancellation of one employee's work schedule that
  results from a request made under subsection 177.1(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 170
- 1993, c. 42, s. 15
- [2017, c. 33, s. 195](#1037364-1200597){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037364-1200597.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-170-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 170"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum hours of work

- **[[171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employee may be employed in excess of the standard hours of work but,
  subject to sections 172, 176 and 177, and to any regulations made
  pursuant to section 175, the total hours that may be worked by any
  employee in any week shall not exceed forty-eight hours in a week or
  such fewer total number of hours as may be prescribed by the
  regulations as maximum working hours in the industrial establishment
  in or in connection with the operation of which the employee is
  employed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Averaging

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection 169(2) applies in the computation of the
  maximum hours of work in a week prescribed under this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 30
- R.S., c. 17(2nd Supp.), s. 4
- 1977-78, c. 27, s. 6
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum hours of work --- collective
agreement

- **[[172]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employer may, in respect of one or more employees subject to a
  collective agreement, establish, modify or cancel a work schedule
  under which the hours exceed the maximum set out in section 171 or in
  regulations made under section 175 if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the average hours of work for a period of two or
    more weeks does not exceed forty-eight hours a week; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the schedule, or its modification or cancellation,
    is agreed to in writing by the employer and the trade union.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum hours of work

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), an employer may, in
  respect of one or more employees not subject to a collective
  agreement, establish, modify or cancel a work schedule under which the
  hours exceed the maximum set out in section 171 or in regulations made
  under section 175 if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the average hours of work for a period of two or
    more weeks does not exceed forty-eight hours a week; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the schedule, or its modification or cancellation,
    has been approved

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of one employee's schedule, in
      writing by that employee, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of more than one employee's
      schedule, by at least 70% of the affected employees.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Posting of notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a work schedule is to be established, modified
  or cancelled pursuant to subsection (2), the employer shall post a
  notice of the new schedule, or of its modification or cancellation, in
  readily accessible places where it is likely to be seen by the
  affected employees, for at least thirty days before the new schedule
  or its modification or cancellation takes effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (3) does not apply to the establishment,
  modification or cancellation of one employee's work schedule following
  a request made under subsection 177.1(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 172
- 1993, c. 42, s. 16
- [2017, c. 33, s. 196](#1037391-1200607){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037391-1200607.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-172-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 172"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vote

- **[[172.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a work schedule is established,
  modified or cancelled under subsection 170(2) or 172(2), any affected
  employee may, within ninety days after the new schedule or its
  modification or cancellation takes effect, request that the Head
  conduct a vote to determine whether seventy per cent of the affected
  employees approve the new schedule or its modification or
  cancellation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of Head

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a request is made under subsection (1), the Head
  must conduct a secret vote to determine the percentage of the affected
  employees that approves the new schedule or the modification or
  cancellation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A request made under subsection (1), the ballots and
  any other documents relating to the vote are confidential and shall
  not be given to the employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Counting of ballots

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Head must count the ballots in the presence of a
  representative chosen by the affected employees and a representative
  chosen by the employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Communication of result of vote

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Head must inform the employer, by written notice,
  of the result.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of non-approval

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the result of the vote indicates that less than
  70% of the affected employees approve the new schedule or its
  modification or cancellation, the employer must comply with the result
  of the vote within 30 days after being informed of that result by the
  Head.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations
  respecting the conduct of votes under this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
  Act](/eng/acts/S-22) not applicable

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22) does
  not apply in respect of the written notice given by the Head to the
  employer under subsection (5).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 42, s. 16
- [2018, c. 27, s. 570](#1040441-1282109){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040441-1282109.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-172.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 172.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration

- **[[172.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A work schedule that is established
  or modified under subsection 170(1) or 172(1) remains in effect for
  the duration of the written agreement between the employer and the
  trade union.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A work schedule that is established or modified under
  subsection 170(2) or 172(2) remains in effect for three years or for
  such shorter period as is agreed to by the parties.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 42, s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scheduling hours of work

**[[173]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173 .sectionLabel}** Except as may be
otherwise prescribed by the regulations, hours of work in a week shall
be so scheduled and actually worked that each employee has at least one
full day of rest in the week, and, wherever practicable, Sunday shall be
the normal day of rest in the week.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 31
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice --- work schedule

- **[[173.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall provide an
  employee with their work schedule in writing at least 96 hours before
  the start of the employee's first work period or shift under that
  schedule.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to refuse

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), an employee may refuse to
  work any work period or shift in their schedule that starts within 96
  hours from the time that the schedule is provided to them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An employee shall not refuse to work a work period or
  shift if it is necessary for them to work in order to deal with a
  situation that the employer could not have reasonably foreseen and
  that presents or could reasonably be expected to present an imminent
  or serious

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} threat to the life, health or safety of any person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} threat of damage to or loss of property; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} threat of serious interference with the ordinary
    working of the employer's industrial establishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- subsection 177.1(1)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a change to an
  employee's work schedule following a request made under subsection
  177.1(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall not dismiss, suspend, lay off,
  demote or discipline an employee because the employee has refused to
  work a work period or shift under subsection (2) or take such a
  refusal into account in any decision to promote or train the employee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of subsection 196(4)

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subsection 196(4) does not apply in respect of a work
  period or shift that an employee refuses to work under subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application --- collective
  agreement

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply to employees who are
  employed under the terms of a collective agreement that specifies an
  alternate time frame for providing the work schedule or provides that
  this section does not apply to those employees.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 445](#1039741-1200999){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039741-1200999.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shift changes

- **[[173.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If an employer changes a period or
  shift during which an employee is due to work or adds another work
  period or shift to the employee's schedule, the employer shall give
  the employee written notice of the change or addition at least 24
  hours before

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a change, the employee's original
    work period or shift is to begin or, if the work period or shift
    that results from the change is to begin earlier than the original
    work period or shift, before the period or shift that results from
    the change is to begin; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an addition, the work period or
    shift that was added is to begin.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions --- threat

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if the change to or
  addition of a work period or shift is necessary to deal with a
  situation that the employer could not have reasonably foreseen and
  that presents or could reasonably be expected to present an imminent
  or serious

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} threat to the life, health or safety of any person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} threat of damage to or loss of property; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} threat of serious interference with the ordinary
    working of the employer's industrial establishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- subsection 177.1(1)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a change to or
  addition of a work period or shift following a request made under
  subsection 177.1(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 33, s. 197](#1037400-1200621){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037400-1200621.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Overtime pay or time off

- **[[174]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any regulations made
  under section 175, when an employee is required or permitted to work
  overtime, they are entitled to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be paid for the overtime at a rate of wages not
    less than one and one-half times their regular rate of wages; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be granted not less than one and one-half hours of
    time off with pay for each hour of overtime worked, subject to
    subsections (2) to (5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employee is entitled to time off for overtime
  worked only if,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at their request, they and the employer enter into
    an agreement in writing providing for the taking of time off,
    subject to paragraph (b) and subsections (3) to (5), on a date or
    dates agreed on by them and the employer; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the time off is taken within a period of three
    months after the end of the pay period in which the overtime was
    worked, or within any longer period set out in

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the employee is subject to a collective
      agreement, the collective agreement, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the employee is not subject to a collective
      agreement, the agreement referred to in paragraph (a) or any other
      agreement in writing entered into by them and the employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum period

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The longer period referred to in paragraph (2)(b)
  shall not be more than 12 months for an employee who is not subject to
  a collective agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time off not taken within specified
  period

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the employee does not take all or part of the time
  off within the applicable period referred to in paragraph (2)(b), the
  employer shall, within 30 days after the day on which that period
  ends, pay the employee's wages for the overtime for which the time off
  was not taken, at a rate of wages not less than one and one-half times
  the employee's regular rate of wages on the day on which they worked
  the overtime.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination of employment

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If an employee ceases to be employed before the
  employee takes all or part of the time off referred to in paragraph
  (1)(b), the employer shall, within 30 days after the day on which the
  employee ceases to be employed, pay the employee's wages for the
  overtime for which the time off was not taken, at a rate of wages not
  less than one and one-half times the employee's regular rate of wages
  on the day on which the employee worked the overtime.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of section 189

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Section 189 applies for the purposes of this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 174
- [2017, c. 33, s. 197](#1037400-1200642){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037400-1200642.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-174-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 174"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to refuse

- **[[174.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  an employee may refuse to work the overtime requested by the employer
  in order to carry out the employee's family responsibilities referred
  to in paragraph 206.6(1)(b) or (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasonable steps

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employee may refuse to work overtime only if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have taken reasonable steps to carry out their
    family responsibility by other means, so as to enable them to work
    overtime; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} even though the steps referred to in paragraph (a)
    have been taken, they are still required to carry out that
    responsibility during the period of the overtime.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An employee is not to refuse to work overtime if it
  is necessary for them to work overtime to deal with a situation that
  the employer could not have reasonably foreseen and that presents or
  could reasonably be expected to present an imminent or serious

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} threat to the life, health or safety of any person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} threat of damage to or loss of property; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} threat of serious interference with the ordinary
    working of the employer's industrial establishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall not dismiss, suspend, lay off,
  demote or discipline an employee because the employee has refused to
  work overtime under subsection (1) or take such a refusal into account
  in any decision to promote or train the employee.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 33, s. 197](#1037400-1200658){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037400-1200658.html"}
- 2018, c. 27, s. 511
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations for the purpose of this
Division

- **[[175]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-175 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} modifying any provision of this Division for the
    purpose of the application of this Division to classes of employees
    who are employed in or in connection with the operation of any
    industrial establishment if, in the opinion of the Governor in
    Council, the application of those sections without modification

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} would be or is unduly prejudicial to the
      interests of the employees in those classes, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} would be or is seriously detrimental to the
      operation of the industrial establishment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} exempting any class of employees from the
    application of any provision of this Division if the Governor in
    Council is satisfied that it cannot reasonably be applied to that
    class of employees;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} respecting rest periods under section 169.2,
    including defining the terms "shift" and "work period" for the
    purposes of that section;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} providing that section 174 does not apply in
    circumstances where work practices specified in the regulations are
    followed that in the opinion of the Governor in Council make the
    application of that section either unreasonable or inequitable; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} providing for the calculation of hours worked by
    employees of any class who are employed in any industrial
    establishment or in any class of industrial establishment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2017, c. 33, s. 198\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 175
- [2017, c. 33, s. 198](#1037402-1200663){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037402-1200663.html"}
- 2018, c. 27, s. 446
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-175-20181213.html "Link to previous version of section 175"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excess hours under permit

- **[[176]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-176 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  the application of an employer or an employer's organization, the
  Head, having regard to the conditions of employment in any industrial
  establishment and the welfare of the employees, may, by a permit in
  writing, authorize hours to be worked by any class of employees set
  out in the permit in excess of the maximum hours of work specified in
  or prescribed under section 171, established under section 172 or
  prescribed by regulations made under section 175.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Justifying permit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No permit may be issued under subsection (1) unless
  the applicant has satisfied the Head

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that exceptional circumstances exist that justify
    the working of additional hours;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the employer had posted a notice of the
    application for the permit, for at least 30 days before its proposed
    effective date, in places readily accessible to the affected class
    of employees where they were likely to see it; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that the employer had informed the trade union in
    writing of the application for the permit, if those employees are
    represented by a trade union.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of permit

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A permit under subsection (1) shall be issued for the
  period specified therein, which shall not be longer than the period
  during which it is anticipated that the exceptional circumstances that
  justified the permit will continue.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional hours may be specified

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A permit under subsection (1) may specify either

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the total of the number of additional hours in
    excess of the maximum hours specified in or prescribed under section
    171 or by regulations made under section 175, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the additional hours that may be worked in any day
    and in any week during the period of the permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a permit has been issued under this section, the
  employer for whom or on whose behalf the permit was issued shall
  report in writing to the Head, within 15 days after the expiration of
  the period specified in the permit or within such time as the Head may
  fix in the permit, stating the number of employees who worked in
  excess of the maximum hours specified in or prescribed under section
  171 or by regulations made under section 175 and the number of
  additional hours each of them worked.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 176
- 1993, c. 42, s. 17
- [2018, c. 27, s. 571](#1040453-1282119){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040453-1282119.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-176-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 176"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency work

- **[[177]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-177 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  maximum hours of work in a week specified in or prescribed under
  section 171, established pursuant to section 172 or prescribed by
  regulations made under section 175 may be exceeded, but only to the
  extent necessary to prevent serious interference with the ordinary
  working of the industrial establishment affected, in cases of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} accident to machinery, equipment, plant or persons;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} urgent and essential work to be done to machinery,
    equipment or plant; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} other unforeseen or unpreventable circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reporting additional work

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the maximum hours of work in an industrial
  establishment have been exceeded under the authority of subsection
  (1), the employer shall report in writing to the Head, and also to the
  trade union if the affected employees are subject to a collective
  agreement, within 15 days after the end of the month in which the
  maximum was exceeded, stating the nature of the circumstances in which
  the maximum was exceeded, the number of employees who worked in excess
  of the maximum and the number of additional hours each of them worked.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 177
- 1993, c. 42, s. 18
- [2018, c. 27, s. 572](#1040458-1282122){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040458-1282122.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-177-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 177"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION I.1]{.HLabel2}[Flexible Work Arrangements]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1200665 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to request

- **[[177.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-177.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employee who has completed six
  consecutive months of continuous employment with an employer may
  request from the employer a change to the following terms and
  conditions of employment:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the number of hours that the employee is required
    to work;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee's work schedule;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the employee's location of work; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any terms and conditions that apply to the employee
    and that are prescribed by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of request

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The request shall be made in writing and shall
  include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employee's name;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the request is made;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of the change to the terms and
    conditions of employment that is requested;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the date on which the change would take effect and,
    if the change is intended to be temporary, the date on which the
    change would cease to have effect;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an explanation of the effect that, in the
    employee's opinion, the requested change would have on the employer
    and the manner in which, in the employee's opinion, the employer
    could manage that effect; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any information that may be prescribed by
    regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer's decision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An employer to whom a request is made shall make one
  of the following decisions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} grant the request;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} offer to grant the request in part or to make an
    alternative change to the terms and conditions of employment; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} refuse the request on one or more of the following
    grounds:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the requested change would result in additional
      costs that would be a burden on the employer,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the requested change would have a detrimental
      impact on the quality or quantity of work within the employer's
      industrial establishment, on the ability to meet customer demand
      or on any other aspect of performance within that industrial
      establishment,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the employer is unable to reorganize work among
      existing employees or to recruit additional employees in order to
      manage the requested change,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} there would be insufficient work available for
      the employee if the requested change was granted, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} any ground prescribed by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of decision

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall, as soon as possible and not later
  than 30 days after receiving the request, give written notice to the
  employee of their decision. The notice in respect of a decision made
  under paragraph (3)(b) or (c) shall include written reasons for
  refusing the requested change or for not granting a part of it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to change terms and conditions

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The employer may, for the purpose of granting a
  request made by an employee under paragraph (3)(a) or for the purpose
  of giving effect to a written agreement with the employee following an
  offer made under paragraph (3)(b), change the employee's terms and
  conditions of employment. However, when there is any other provision
  under this Part or any provision of any regulations made under this
  Part that authorizes the employer to make a change to those terms and
  conditions, they shall make the change under that provision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Collective agreement

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall not change, under subsection (5), a
  term or condition of employment contained in a collective agreement
  unless the change is agreed to in writing by the employer and the
  trade union.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An employer shall not dismiss, suspend, lay off,
  demote or discipline an employee because the employee has made a
  request under subsection (1) or take such a request into account in
  any decision to promote or train the employee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations limiting
  the number of requests that an employee may make in any year and
  specifying the information that shall be included in a notice under
  subsection (4) or an agreement referred to in subsection (5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, nothing in this section limits
  an employer's duty to accommodate an employee under any other Act of
  Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of section 189

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Section 189 applies for the purposes of this
  Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 33, s. 199](#1037403-1200706){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037403-1200706.html"}
- 2018, c. 27, s. 512
:::

### [DIVISION II]{.HLabel2}[Minimum Wage and Age of Employment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-342321 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum wage

- **[[178]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) to (5),
  an employer shall pay to each employee a wage at a minimum hourly rate
  that is not less than the rate that is determined in accordance with
  section 178.1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Province of employment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the minimum hourly rate that is fixed, from time
  to time, by or under an Act of the legislature of the province where
  the employee is usually employed and that is generally applicable
  regardless of occupation, status or work experience is higher than the
  minimum hourly rate fixed under subsection (1), an employer shall pay
  to each employee a wage at a rate that is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the wages of the employee are paid on an hourly
    basis, not less than that higher minimum hourly rate; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the wages of the employee are paid on any basis
    of time other than hourly, not less than the equivalent of the rate
    under paragraph (a) for the time worked by the employee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Wage rate based on age

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (2)(a), if minimum
  hourly rates for a province are fixed on the basis of age, the minimum
  hourly rate for that province is the highest of those rates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum on other basis than time

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where the wages of an employee are computed and paid
  on a basis other than time or on a combined basis of time and some
  other basis, the Minister may, by order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} fix a standard basis of work to which a minimum
    wage on a basis other than time may be applied; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} fix a minimum rate of wage that in the opinion of
    the Minister is the equivalent of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the minimum rate determined in accordance with
      subsection (2), if that minimum rate is higher than or equal to
      the minimum rate determined in accordance with section 178.1, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the minimum rate determined in accordance with
      section 178.1, if that minimum rate is higher than the minimum
      rate determined in accordance with subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum rate fixed by order to be paid

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Except as otherwise provided by or under this
  Division, the employer shall pay to each employee who is paid on a
  basis other than time or on a combined basis of time and some other
  basis a wage at a rate not less than the minimum rate fixed by order
  under subsection (4).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 178
- 1996, c. 32, s. 1
- [2021, c. 23, s. 246](#1307585-1335843){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1307585-1335843.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-178-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 178"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum wage --- rate

- **[[178.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  minimum hourly rate referred to in subsection 178(1) is \$15.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual adjustment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On April 1 of each year after the year in which this
  section comes into force, the minimum hourly rate is to be adjusted to
  the rate, rounded up to the nearest \$0.05, that is equal to the
  product of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as the case may be,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in respect of April 1 of the year after the year
      in which this section comes into force, \$15, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in respect of April 1 of each subsequent year,
      the rate that is determined in accordance with this section on
      April 1 of the preceding year, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the ratio that the Consumer Price Index for the
    preceding calendar year bears to the Consumer Price Index for the
    calendar year before that preceding calendar year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consumer Price Index

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (2), a reference to
  the Consumer Price Index for any calendar year means the average of
  the all-items Consumer Price Index for Canada, not seasonally
  adjusted, as published by Statistics Canada under the authority of the
  [Statistics Act](/eng/acts/S-19), for each month in that year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No adjustment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (2), the minimum hourly rate is
  not to be adjusted on April 1 of a given year if on that day the rate
  determined in accordance with that subsection is less than, as the
  case may be,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of April 1 of the year after the year in
    which this section comes into force, \$15; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of April 1 of each subsequent year, the
    rate that is determined in accordance with this section on April 1
    of the preceding year.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2021, c. 23, s. 247](#1307590-1335860){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1307590-1335860.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employees under 18 years of age

**[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179 .sectionLabel}** An employer may employ
a person under the age of 18 years only

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in an occupation specified by the regulations; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to the conditions fixed by the regulations
  for employment in that occupation.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 179
- 1996, c. 32, s. 2
- [2018, c. 27, s. 448](#1039748-1401088){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039748-1401088.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-179-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 179"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, R.S.,
1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 6\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations applicable to Division

**[[181]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make regulations for carrying out the purposes and provisions of
this Division and, without restricting the generality of the foregoing,
may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} requiring employers to pay employees who report for
  work at the call of the employer wages for such minimum number of
  hours as may be prescribed, whether or not the employee is called on
  to perform any work after so reporting for work;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} fixing the maximum price to be charged for board,
  whether full or partial, furnished by or on behalf of an employer to
  an employee, or the maximum deduction to be made therefor from the
  wages of the employee by the employer;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} fixing the maximum price to be charged for living
  quarters, either permanent or temporary, furnished by or on behalf of
  an employer to an employee, whether or not those quarters are
  self-contained and whether or not the employer retains general
  possession and custody thereof, or the maximum deduction to be made
  therefor from the wages of the employee by the employer;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} governing the charges or deductions for furnishing
  uniforms or other articles of wearing apparel that an employer may
  require an employee to wear or requiring an employer in any specified
  circumstances to provide, maintain or launder uniforms or other
  articles of wearing apparel that the employer may require an employee
  to wear;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} governing the charges or deductions for furnishing
  any tools or equipment that an employer may require an employee to use
  and for the maintenance and repair of any such tools or equipment;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} specifying, for the purposes of section 179, the
  occupations in which persons under the age of 18 years, or any class
  of persons under that age, may be employed in an industrial
  establishment and fixing the conditions of that employment; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} exempting, on such terms and conditions and for such
  periods as are considered advisable, any employer from the application
  of section 178 in respect of any class of employees who are being
  trained on the job, if the training facilities provided and used by
  the employer are adequate to provide a training program that will
  increase the skill or proficiency of an employee.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 181
- 1996, c. 32, s. 3
- [2018, c. 27, s. 449](#1039753-1401090){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039753-1401090.html"}
:::

### [DIVISION II.1]{.HLabel2}[Breaks for Medical Reasons or Nursing]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1201001 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Medical break

- **[[181.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the regulations, every
  employee is entitled to and shall be granted any unpaid breaks that
  are necessary for medical reasons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On written request by the employer, the employee must
  provide a certificate issued by a health care practitioner setting out
  the length and frequency of the breaks needed for medical reasons and
  any additional information that may be prescribed by regulation.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 450](#1039755-1201008){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039755-1201008.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Nursing break

**[[181.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181.2 .sectionLabel}** Subject to the
regulations, every employee who is nursing is entitled to and shall be
granted any unpaid breaks necessary for them to nurse or to express
breast milk.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 450](#1039755-1201012){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039755-1201012.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[181.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181.3 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} modifying the provisions of sections 181.1 or 181.2
  for the purpose of the application of this Division to any class of
  employees;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} exempting any class of employees from the application
  of section 181.1 or 181.2;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the breaks set out in subsection 181.1(1)
  and section 181.2, including circumstances in which those breaks
  cannot be taken; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting additional information to be included in a
  certificate required under subsection 181.1(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 450](#1039755-1201020){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039755-1201020.html"}
:::

### [DIVISION III]{.HLabel2}[Equal Wages]{.HTitleText2} {#h-342359 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of sections

- **[[182]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of ascertaining whether a discriminatory practice under
  section 11 of the [Canadian Human Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6) is being
  or has been engaged in, sections 249, 250, 252, 253, 254, 255 and 264
  apply, with such modifications as the circumstances require, as if
  this Part expressly required an employer to refrain from that
  discriminatory practice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to Commission

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Head has reasonable grounds at any time for
  believing that an employer is engaging or has engaged in a
  discriminatory practice described in subsection (1), the Head may
  notify the Canadian Human Rights Commission or file a complaint with
  that Commission under section 40 of the [Canadian Human Rights
  Act](/eng/acts/H-6).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 182
- [2018, c. 27, s. 573](#1040460-1282126){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040460-1282126.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-182-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 182"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION IV]{.HLabel2}[Annual Vacations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-342367 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[183]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183 .sectionLabel}** In this Division,

[[vacation pay]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[vacation pay]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the amount an employee is
    entitled to under section 184.01; ([indemnité de congé
    annuel]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[year of employment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[year of employment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means continuous
    employment of an employee by one employer

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for a period of twelve consecutive months
      beginning with the date the employment began or any subsequent
      anniversary date thereafter, or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for a calendar year or other year determined by
      the employer, in accordance with the regulations, in relation to
      an industrial establishment. ([année de service]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 183
- 1993, c. 42, s. 19
- [2018, c. 27, s. 453](#1039761-1201023){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039761-1201023.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-183-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 183"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual vacation with pay

**[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184 .sectionLabel}** Except as otherwise
provided by or under this Division, in respect of every year of
employment by an employer, every employee is entitled to and shall be
granted a vacation with vacation pay of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} at least two weeks if they have completed at least
  one year of employment;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} at least three weeks if they have completed at least
  five consecutive years of employment with the same employer; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} at least four weeks if they have completed at least
  10 consecutive years of employment with the same employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 184
- [2018, c. 27, s. 454](#1039763-1201030){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039763-1201030.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-184-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 184"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calculation of vacation pay

**[[184.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184.01 .sectionLabel}** An employee is
entitled to vacation pay equal to:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} 4% of their wages during the year of employment in
  respect of which they are entitled to the vacation;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} 6% of their wages during the year of employment in
  respect of which they are entitled to the vacation, if they have
  completed at least five consecutive years of employment with the same
  employer; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} 8% of their wages during the year of employment in
  respect of which they are entitled to the vacation, if they have
  completed at least 10 consecutive years of employment with the same
  employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 454](#1039763-1201037){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039763-1201037.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entitlement to vacation in one or more
periods

**[[184.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184.1 .sectionLabel}** A vacation granted
to an employee under this Division is to be taken only in one period or,
if the employee makes a request in writing and the employer approves it
in writing, in more than one period.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 33, s. 200](#1037405-1200711){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037405-1200711.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Granting vacation with pay

**[[185]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-185 .sectionLabel}** The employer of an
employee who under this Division has become entitled to a vacation with
vacation pay

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall grant to the employee the vacation to which the
  employee is entitled, which shall begin not later than ten months
  immediately following the completion of the year of employment for
  which the employee became entitled to the vacation; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall, at any time that is prescribed by the
  regulations, pay to the employee

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the vacation is taken in one period, the
    vacation pay to which the employee is entitled in respect of that
    vacation, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the vacation is taken in more than one period,
    for each period, the proportion of the vacation pay that the
    vacation taken is of the annual vacation to which the employee is
    entitled.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 185
- [2017, c. 33, s. 201](#1037407-1200716){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037407-1200716.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-185-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 185"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vacation pay

**[[186]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186 .sectionLabel}** Vacation pay shall for
all purposes be deemed to be wages.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 42
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General holiday during vacation

**[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187 .sectionLabel}** Where one or more
general holidays occur during a vacation granted to an employee pursuant
to this Division, the vacation to which the employee is entitled under
this Division may be extended by one day for each such holiday, and the
employer shall pay to the employee in addition to the vacation pay the
wages to which the employee is entitled for those general holidays.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 43
- 1977-78, c. 27, s. 12
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interruption

- **[[187.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employee may interrupt a vacation
  granted to them under this Division in order to permit them to take a
  leave of absence under Division VII or VIII or section 247.5 or to be
  absent due to a reason referred to in subsection 239(1) or 239.1(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of section 209.1

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an employee interrupts a vacation to take leave
  under any of sections 205.1, 206, 206.1 and 206.3 to 206.9 and resumes
  the vacation immediately at the end of that leave, section 209.1
  applies to them as if they did not resume the vacation before
  returning to work.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsection 239(7)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an employee interrupts a vacation to be absent due
  to a reason referred to in subsection 239(1) and resumes the vacation
  immediately at the end of that leave, subsection 239(7) applies to
  them as if they did not resume the vacation before returning to work.

- [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2021, c. 26, s. 20\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsections 239.1(3) and
  (4)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If an employee interrupts a vacation to be absent due
  to a reason referred to in subsection 239.1(1) and resumes the
  vacation immediately at the end of that leave, subsections 239.1(3)
  and (4) apply to them as if they did not resume the vacation before
  returning to work.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of sections 247.93 to
  247.95

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If an employee interrupts a vacation to take leave
  under section 247.5 and resumes the vacation immediately at the end of
  that leave, sections 247.93 to 247.95 apply to that employee as if
  they did not resume the vacation before returning to work.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to employer --- interruption of
  vacation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An employee who intends to interrupt their vacation
  shall provide the employer with written notice of the interruption
  before or as soon as possible after the interruption begins.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to employer --- resumption of
  vacation

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An employee who interrupts their vacation and who
  intends to resume it immediately after the interruption ends shall
  provide the employer with written notice of the day on which they
  resume their vacation before or as soon as possible after that day.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 33, s. 202](#1037409-1200733){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037409-1200733.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 455](#1039765-1201040){.wb-lbx

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Postponement

- **[[187.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraph 185(a) or any term
  or condition of employment, an employee may postpone their vacation
  until after the day on which a leave of absence taken under Division
  VII or VIII or section 247.5, or an absence due to a reason referred
  to in subsection 239(1) or 239.1(1), ends.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to employer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employee who intends to postpone their vacation
  shall, as soon as possible, provide the employer with prior written
  notice of the postponement.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 33, s. 202](#1037409-1200740){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037409-1200740.html"}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination of employment during year

**[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188 .sectionLabel}** When an employee ceases
to be employed, the employer shall pay to the employee within 30 days
after the day on which the employee ceases to be employed

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any vacation pay then owing by the employer to the
  employee under this Division in respect of any prior completed year of
  employment; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the applicable percentage, under section 184.01, of
  the wages of the employee during any part of the completed portion of
  their year of employment in respect of which vacation pay has not been
  paid to them.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 188
- 2012, c. 31, s. 219
- [2018, c. 27, s. 456](#1039767-1201045){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039767-1201045.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-188-20140401.html "Link to previous version of section 188"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer

- **[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite the lease or transfer of a
  work, undertaking or business, or any part of a work, undertaking or
  business, from one employer to another employer by sale, merger or
  otherwise, the employment of the employee, before and after the lease
  or transfer, who is employed in or in connection with the operation of
  that work, undertaking or business, is, for the purposes of this
  Division, deemed to be continuous with one employer if the work,
  undertaking or business

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is a federal work, undertaking or business; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} becomes a federal work, undertaking or business due
    to the lease or transfer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retendering

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If, due to a contract being awarded through a
  retendering process, a second employer becomes responsible for
  carrying out any particular federal work, undertaking or business, or
  part of one, that was previously carried out by a first employer, an
  employee who is employed in or in connection with the its operation
  before and after the retendering, is, for the purposes of this
  Division, deemed to be continuously employed with one employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (1.1) do not apply if the
  employee's first day of employment by the second employer is more than
  13 weeks after the day that is the earlier of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employee's last day of employment by the first
    employer; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the federal work, undertaking or
    business is transferred or the first day the second employer carries
    out the federal work, undertaking or business, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period of continuous employment

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, if an employer's work,
  undertaking or business becomes a federal work, undertaking or
  business due to a change in its activities, for the purposes of this
  Division, an employee's period of continuous employment by the
  employer includes any period in which the work, undertaking or
  business in or in connection with the operation of which the employee
  is employed was not a federal work, undertaking or business.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calculation of period of employment

  [(1.4)]{.lawlabel} If subsection (1) or (1.1) applies in respect of an
  employee, any period between their employment by the first employer
  and their employment by the second employer is not included in the
  calculation of their period of continuous employment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(1.5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of an employee's employment by the
  second employer, this section does not apply in respect of the
  calculation of the employee's entitlement under section 230 if the
  first employer complied with that section in respect of their
  employment by that employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- severance pay

  [(1.6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of an employee's employment by the
  second employer, this section does not apply in respect of the
  calculation of their entitlement to severance pay under section 235 if
  the first employer paid them severance pay in respect of their
  employment by that employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inclusion

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), a federal work,
  undertaking or business includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any portion of the federal public administration
    specified from time to time in Schedule I, IV or V to the [Financial
    Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11) that is deleted from one of
    those Schedules and that is established as or becomes a part of a
    corporation or any federal work, undertaking or business to which
    this Part applies; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a portion of the federal public administration
    included in a portion of the federal public administration so
    specified in one of those Schedules that is severed from the portion
    in which it was included and that is established as or becomes a
    part of such a corporation or federal work, undertaking or business.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 189
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 7
- 1996, c. 18, s. 10
- 2003, c. 22, s. 112
- [2018, c. 27, s. 457](#1039769-1201064){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039769-1201064.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-189-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 189"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations in relation to annual
vacations

**[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make regulations for carrying out the purposes and provisions of
this Division and, without restricting the generality of the foregoing,
may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} defining the circumstances and conditions under which
  the rights of an employee under this Division may be waived or the
  enjoyment thereof postponed;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the notices to be given to employees of
  the times when vacations may be taken;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the time when vacation pay shall be paid;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} defining the absences from employment that shall be
  deemed not to have interrupted continuity of employment;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting the determination by the employer of a
  year of employment in relation to any industrial establishment;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} for the calculation and determination of vacation and
  vacation pay in the case of seasonal or temporary employees or in
  other suitable cases;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} providing for the granting of vacation or the payment
  of vacation pay in the event of temporary cessation of employment; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} providing for the application of this Division where,
  owing to illness or other unavoidable absence, an employee has been
  absent from his employment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 190
- 1993, c. 42, s. 20
:::

### [DIVISION V]{.HLabel2}[General Holidays]{.HTitleText2} {#h-342421 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Division.

[[employed in a continuous operation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[employed in a continuous operation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in
    respect of an employee, employment in

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any industrial establishment in which, in each
      seven-day period, operations once begun normally continue without
      cessation until the completion of the regularly scheduled
      operations for that period;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any operations or services concerned with the
      running of trains, planes, ships, trucks or other vehicles,
      whether in scheduled or non-scheduled operations;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any telephone, radio, television, telegraph or
      other communication or broadcasting operations or services; or

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any operation or service normally carried on
      without regard to Sundays or general holidays. ([occupé à un
      travail ininterrompu]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[holiday pay]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[holiday pay]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means pay calculated in
    accordance with section 196. ([indemnité de congé]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[holiday with pay]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[holiday with pay]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a holiday for which
    an employee is entitled to holiday pay. ([congé
    payé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 191
- 2012, c. 31, s. 220
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-191-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 191"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entitlement to holidays

**[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192 .sectionLabel}** Except as otherwise
provided by this Division, every employee is entitled to and shall be
granted a holiday with pay on each of the general holidays falling
within any period of his employment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 48
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General holiday falling on day off

- **[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except as otherwise provided by this
  Division and subject to subsection (2), when a general holiday falls
  on a day that is a non-working day for an employee, the employee is
  entitled to and shall be granted a holiday with pay at some other
  time, which may be by way of addition to his annual vacation or
  granted as a holiday with pay at a time convenient to both the
  employee and the employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternative day for holiday falling on
  non-working Saturday or Sunday

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as otherwise provided by this Division, when
  New Year's Day, Canada Day, National Day for Truth and Reconciliation,
  Remembrance Day, Christmas Day or Boxing Day falls on a Sunday or
  Saturday that is a non-working day, the employee is entitled to and
  shall be granted a holiday with pay on the working day immediately
  preceding or following the general holiday.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 193
- [2021, c. 11, s. 5](#1294216-1313024){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1294216-1313024.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-193-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 193"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption under collective agreement

**[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194 .sectionLabel}** Section 193 does not
apply in respect of any employees who are employed under the terms of a
collective agreement that entitles those employees to at least nine
holidays with pay, exclusive of any annual vacation, in each year.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 50
- 1977-78, c. 27, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Substitution --- employees subject to
collective agreement

- **[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employer may, in respect of one or more employees subject to a
  collective agreement, substitute any other day for a general holiday
  if the substitution is agreed to in writing by the employer and the
  trade union, and the substituted day shall, for that employee or those
  employees, be deemed to be a general holiday for the purposes of this
  Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Substitution --- employees not subject
  to collective agreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), an employer may, in
  respect of one or more employees not subject to a collective
  agreement, substitute any other day for a general holiday and the
  substituted day shall, for that employee or those employees, be deemed
  to be a general holiday for the purposes of this Part, if the
  substitution has been approved

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a substitution that affects one
    employee, by that employee in writing; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a substitution that affects more
    than one employee, by at least 70% of the affected employees.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Posting of notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If any other day is to be substituted for a general
  holiday under subsection (2), the employer shall post a notice of the
  substitution in readily accessible places where it is likely to be
  seen by the affected employees, for at least 30 days before the
  substitution takes effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (3) does not apply to a substitution in
  respect of one employee following a request made under subsection
  177.1(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 195
- 1993, c. 42, s. 21
- [2017, c. 33, s. 203](#1037418-1200844){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037418-1200844.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-195-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 195"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voting, duration

**[[195.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195.1 .sectionLabel}** Sections 172.1 and
172.2 apply, with such modifications as the circumstances require, in
respect of the substitution of a general holiday pursuant to this
Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 42, s. 21
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Holiday pay

- **[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (4),
  an employer shall, for each general holiday, pay an employee holiday
  pay equal to at least one twentieth of the wages, excluding overtime
  pay, that the employee earned with the employer in the four-week
  period immediately preceding the week in which the general holiday
  occurs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employees on commission

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employee whose wages are paid in whole or in part
  on a commission basis and who has completed at least 12 weeks of
  continuous employment with an employer shall, for each general
  holiday, be paid holiday pay equal to at least one sixtieth of the
  wages, excluding overtime pay, that they earned in the 12-week period
  immediately preceding the week in which the general holiday occurs.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 27, s. 458\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuous operation employee not
  reporting for work

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An employee who is employed in a continuous operation
  is not entitled to holiday pay for a general holiday

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on which they do not report for work after having
    been called to work on that day; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for which they make themselves unavailable to work
    when the conditions of employment in the industrial establishment in
    which they are employed

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} require them to be available, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} allow them to make themselves unavailable.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 27, s. 458\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 196
- 2012, c. 31, s. 221
- [2018, c. 27, s. 458](#1039783-1201068){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039783-1201068.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-196-20150316.html "Link to previous version of section 196"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional pay for holiday work

- **[[197]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-197 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employee who is required to work on a day on which they are entitled
  to holiday pay shall be paid, in addition to the holiday pay for that
  day, wages at a rate equal to at least one and one-half times their
  regular rate of wages for the time that they work on that day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employment in continuous operation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employee employed in a continuous operation who is
  required to work on a day on which they are entitled to holiday pay
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be paid in accordance with subsection (1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be given a holiday with pay at some other time,
    either by adding it to their annual vacation or by granting it at a
    time convenient to both the employee and the employer; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be paid holiday pay for the first day on which they
    do not work after that day, if a collective agreement that is
    binding on the employer and the employee so provides.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 27, s. 459\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 197
- 1993, c. 42, s. 22(F)
- 2001, c. 34, s. 18(F)
- 2012, c. 31, s. 221
- [2018, c. 27, s. 459](#1039787-1201070){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039787-1201070.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-197-20150316.html "Link to previous version of section 197"){.PITLink}
:::

31, s. 221\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Holiday work for managers, etc.

**[[199]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199 .sectionLabel}** Despite section 197, an
employee excluded from the application of Division I under subsection
167(2) who is required to work on a day on which they are entitled to
holiday pay shall be given a holiday with pay at some other time, either
by adding it to their annual vacation or by granting it at a time
convenient to both the employee and the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 199
- 2012, c. 31, s. 222
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Holiday pay deemed to be wages

**[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200 .sectionLabel}** Holiday pay granted to
an employee is for all purposes deemed to be wages.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 200
- 2012, c. 31, s. 222
:::

**[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201 .sectionLabel}** Section 189 applies for
the purposes of this Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 201
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 8

### [DIVISION VI]{.HLabel2}[Multi-employer Employment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-342505 .Subheading}

Definition of [[multi-employer employment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- **[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this Division, [[multi-employer employment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, as
  more particularly defined by the regulations, means employment in any
  occupation or trade in which, by custom of that occupation or trade,
  any or all employees would in the usual course of a working month be
  ordinarily employed by more than one employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} defining more particularly the expression
    "multi-employer employment"; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} modifying, to the extent that the Governor in
    Council considers necessary, the provisions of Division I.1, IV, V,
    VII, VIII, X, XI, XIII or XIV so that, as far as practicable,
    employees engaged in multi-employer employment will be entitled to
    the same rights and benefits under that Division as employees
    employed by one employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem, application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any regulation made pursuant to subsection (2) may be
  made applicable to all federal works, undertakings or businesses or
  particularly to one or more such works, undertakings or businesses or
  such classes thereof or classes of employees thereof as may be
  specified in the regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 203
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 9
- [2017, c. 33, s. 204](#1037425-1200846){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037425-1200846.html"}
:::
