Version](section-203-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 203"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION VII]{.HLabel2}[Maternity-related Reassignment and Leave and Other Leaves]{.HTitleText2} {#h-342517 .Subheading}

#### [Maternity-related Reassignment and Leave]{.HTitleText3} {#h-342518 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reassignment and job modification

- **[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employee who is pregnant or nursing may, during the period from the
  beginning of the pregnancy to the end of the twenty-fourth week
  following the birth, request the employer to modify her job functions
  or reassign her to another job if, by reason of the pregnancy or
  nursing, continuing any of her current job functions may pose a risk
  to her health or to that of the foetus or child.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employee's request under subsection (1) must be
  accompanied by a certificate from a health care practitioner of the
  employee's choice indicating the expected duration of the potential
  risk and the activities or conditions to avoid in order to eliminate
  the risk.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 204
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 9
- 1993, c. 42, s. 26
- [2018, c. 27, s. 463](#1039794-1201074){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039794-1201074.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-204-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 204"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer's obligations

- **[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employer to whom a request has been made under subsection 204(1) shall
  examine the request in consultation with the employee and, where
  reasonably practicable, shall modify the employee's job functions or
  reassign her.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights of employee

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employee who has made a request under subsection
  204(1) is entitled to continue in her current job while the employer
  examines her request, but, if the risk posed by continuing any of her
  job functions so requires, she is entitled to and shall be granted a
  leave of absence with pay at her regular rate of wages until the
  employer

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} modifies her job functions or reassigns her, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} informs her in writing that it is not reasonably
    practicable to modify her job functions or reassign her,

  and that pay shall for all purposes be deemed to be wages.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Onus of proof

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The onus is on the employer to show that a
  modification of job functions or a reassignment that would avoid the
  activities or conditions indicated in the certificate issued under
  subsection 204(2) is not reasonably practicable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee to be informed

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the employer concludes that a modification of job
  functions or a reassignment that would avoid the activities or
  conditions indicated in the certificate is not reasonably practicable,
  the employer shall so inform the employee in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Status of employee

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An employee whose job functions are modified or who
  is reassigned shall be deemed to continue to hold the job that she
  held at the time of making the request under subsection 204(1), and
  shall continue to receive the wages and benefits that are attached to
  that job.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee's right to leave

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An employee referred to in subsection (4) is entitled
  to and shall be granted a leave of absence for the duration of the
  risk as indicated in the certificate.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 205
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 9
- 1993, c. 42, s. 26
- [2018, c. 27, s. 464](#1039801-1201081){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039801-1201081.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-205-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 205"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entitlement to leave

**[[205.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.1 .sectionLabel}** An employee who is
pregnant or nursing is entitled to and shall be granted a leave of
absence during the period from the beginning of the pregnancy to the end
of the twenty-fourth week following the birth, if she provides the
employer with a certificate issued by a health care practitioner of her
choice indicating that she is unable to work by reason of the pregnancy
or nursing and indicating the duration of that inability.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 42, s. 26
- [2018, c. 27, s. 465](#1039806-1201084){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039806-1201084.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-205.1-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 205.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee's duty to inform employer

**[[205.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.2 .sectionLabel}** An employee whose
job functions have been modified, who has been reassigned or who is on a
leave of absence shall give at least two weeks' notice in writing to the
employer of any change in the duration of the risk or in the inability
as indicated in the certificate issued by a health care practitioner,
unless there is a valid reason why that notice cannot be given, and the
notice must be accompanied by a new certificate.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 42, s. 26
- [2018, c. 27, s. 465](#1039806-1201087){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039806-1201087.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-205.2-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 205.2"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Maternity Leave]{.HTitleText3} {#h-342552 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entitlement to leave

- **[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee is entitled to and
  shall be granted a leave of absence from employment of up to 17 weeks,
  which leave may begin not earlier than 13 weeks prior to the estimated
  date of her confinement and end not later than 17 weeks following the
  actual date of her confinement, if the employee provides her employer
  with a certificate of a health care practitioner certifying that she
  is pregnant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of period

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If the confinement has not occurred during the 17
  weeks of her leave of absence, the leave of absence is extended until
  the date of her confinement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of period --- child in
  hospital

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If, during the period of 17 weeks following the date
  of confinement, the child who was born is hospitalized, the period is
  extended by the number of weeks during which the child is
  hospitalized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An extension under subsection (2) must not result in
  the period being longer than 52 weeks.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 206
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 10
- 1993, c. 42, s. 26
- 2012, c. 27, s. 3
- 2017, c. 20, s. 259
- [2018, c. 27, s. 466](#1039808-1201089){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039808-1201089.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-206-20171203.html "Link to previous version of section 206"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Parental Leave]{.HTitleText3} {#h-342566 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entitlement to leave

- **[[206.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  every employee is entitled to and shall be granted a leave of absence
  from employment of up to 63 weeks to care for a newborn child of the
  employee or a child who is in the care of the employee for the purpose
  of adoption under the laws governing adoption in the province in which
  the employee resides.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period when leave may be taken

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The leave of absence granted under this section may
  only be taken during the 78-week period beginning

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a new-born child of the employee, at
    the option of the employee, on the day the child is born or comes
    into the actual care of the employee; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an adoption, on the day the child
    comes into the actual care of the employee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of period

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The period referred to in subsection (2) is
  extended by the number of weeks during which the employee is on leave
  under any of sections 206.3 to 206.5 and 206.9, is absent due to a
  reason referred to in subsection 239(1) or 239.1(1) or is on leave
  under any of paragraphs 247.5(1)(a), (b) and (d) to (g).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of period --- child in
  hospital

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} If the child referred to in subsection (1) is
  hospitalized during the period referred to in subsection (2), the
  period is extended by the number of weeks during which the child is
  hospitalized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2.3)]{.lawlabel} An extension under subsection (2.1) or (2.2) must
  not result in the period being longer than 104 weeks.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interruption

  [(2.4)]{.lawlabel} The employee may interrupt the leave referred to in
  subsection (1) in order to permit the employee to take leave under any
  of sections 206.3 to 206.5 and 206.9, to be absent due to a reason
  referred to in subsection 239(1) or 239.1(1) or to take leave under
  any of paragraphs 247.5(1)(a), (b) and (d) to (g).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resumption

  [(2.5)]{.lawlabel} The leave referred to in subsection (1) resumes
  immediately after the interruption ends.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggregate leave --- employees

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The aggregate amount of leave that may be taken by
  more than one employee under this section in respect of the same birth
  or adoption shall not exceed 71 weeks, but the amount of leave that
  may be taken by one employee under this section in respect of the same
  birth or adoption shall not exceed 63 weeks.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- medical leave

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Except to the extent that it is inconsistent with
  subsection 239(7), section 209.1 applies to an employee who
  interrupted the leave referred to in subsection (1) in order to be
  absent due to a reason referred to in subsection 239(1).

- [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2021, c. 26, s. 22\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- work-related illness or
  injury

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Except to the extent that it is inconsistent with
  subsections 239.1(3) and (4), section 209.1 applies to an employee who
  interrupted the leave referred to in subsection (1) in order to be
  absent due to a reason referred to in subsection 239.1(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- member of reserve force

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 209.1, sections 247.93 to 247.95
  apply to an employee who interrupted the leave referred to in
  subsection (1) in order to take leave under any of paragraphs
  247.5(1)(a), (b) and (d) to (g).

::: HistoricalNote

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggregate leave --- maternity and
parental

**[[206.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206.2 .sectionLabel}** The aggregate
amount of leave that may be taken by more than one employee under
sections 206 and 206.1 in respect of the same birth shall not exceed 86
weeks, but the aggregate amount of leave that may be taken by one
employee under those sections in respect of the same birth shall not
exceed 78 weeks.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2000, c. 14, s. 42
- 2017, c. 20, s. 261

#### [Compassionate Care Leave]{.HTitleText3} {#h-342598 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[206.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,
  [[care]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, [[family
  member]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} and [[support]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}
  have, subject to the regulations, the same meanings as in the
  regulations made under the [Employment Insurance Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6)
  and [[week]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the period between midnight on
  Saturday and midnight on the immediately following Saturday.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entitlement to leave

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) to (8), every employee is
  entitled to and shall be granted a leave of absence from employment of
  up to 28 weeks to provide care or support to a family member of the
  employee if a health care practitioner issues a certificate stating
  that the family member has a serious medical condition with a
  significant risk of death within 26 weeks from

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day the certificate is issued; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the leave was commenced before the certificate
    was issued, the day the leave was commenced.

- [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 27, s. 468\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period when leave may be taken

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The leave of absence may only be taken during the
  period

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that starts with

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the first day of the week in which the
      certificate is issued, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the leave was commenced before the
      certificate was issued, the first day of the week in which the
      leave was commenced if the certificate is valid from any day in
      that week; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that ends with the last day of the week in which
    either of the following occurs, namely,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the family member dies, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the period of 52 weeks following the first day
      of the week referred to in paragraph (a) ends.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate not necessary

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, but subject to subsection
  (3), for leave under this section to be taken after the end of the
  period of 26 weeks set out in subsection (2), it is not necessary for
  a health care practitioner to issue an additional certificate under
  that subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shorter period

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a shorter period is prescribed by regulation for
  the purposes of subsection 23.1(5) or 152.06(4) of the [Employment
  Insurance Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the certificate referred to in subsection (2) must
    state that the family member has a serious medical condition with a
    significant risk of death within that period; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that shorter period applies for the purposes of
    subparagraph (3)(b)(ii).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expiry of shorter period

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} When a shorter period referred to in subsection (4)
  has expired in respect of a family member, no further leave may be
  taken under this section in respect of that family member until the
  minimum number of weeks prescribed for the purposes of subsection
  12(4.3) or 152.14(7) of the [Employment Insurance
  Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6) has elapsed.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2014, c. 20, s. 242\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggregate leave --- more than one
  employee

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The aggregate amount of leave that may be taken by
  two or more employees under this section in respect of the care or
  support of the same family member shall not exceed 28 weeks in the
  period referred to in subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation --- section 206.4

  [(7.1)]{.lawlabel} No leave may be taken by one or more employees
  under subsection 206.4(2) or (2.1) before the end of the leave taken
  under subsection (2) in respect of the same person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of certificate

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If requested in writing by the employer within 15
  days after an employee's return to work, the employee must provide the
  employer with a copy of the certificate referred to in subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The references in this section to provisions that are
  in Part VII.1 of the [Employment Insurance Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6) apply
  only in relation to employees who are self-employed persons referred
  to in paragraph (b) of the definition [[self-employed
  person]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 152.01(1) of that Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2003, c. 15, s. 27
- 2009, c. 33, s. 30
- 2014, c. 20, s. 242
- 2015, c. 36, s. 73
- 2017, c. 20, s. 262
- [2018, c. 27, s. 468](#1039836-1201105){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039836-1201105.html"}
:::

Version](section-206.3-20171203.html "Link to previous version of section 206.3"){.PITLink}

#### [Leave Related to Critical Illness]{.HTitleText3} {#h-342635 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[206.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,
  [[care]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, [[critically ill
  adult]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, [[critically ill
  child]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, [[family member]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}
  and [[support]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} have, subject to the
  regulations, the same meanings as in the regulations made under the
  [Employment Insurance Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6) and
  [[week]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} has the same meaning as in subsection
  206.3(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave --- 37 weeks

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employee who is a family member of a critically
  ill child is entitled to and shall be granted a leave of absence from
  employment of up to 37 weeks in order to care for or support that
  child if a health care practitioner has issued a certificate that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} states that the child is a critically ill child and
    requires the care or support of one or more of their family members;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sets out the period during which the child requires
    that care or support.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave --- 17 weeks

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee who is a family member of a
  critically ill adult is entitled to and shall be granted a leave of
  absence from employment of up to 17 weeks in order to care for or
  support that adult if a health care practitioner has issued a
  certificate that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} states that the adult is a critically ill adult and
    requires the care or support of one or more of their family members;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sets out the period during which the adult requires
    that care or support.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 27, s. 469\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period when leave may be taken  ---
   child

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The period during which the employee may take a leave
  of absence

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} begins on the first day of the week in which either
    of the following falls:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the first certificate is issued
      in respect of the child or adult, as the case may be, that meets
      the requirements of subsection (2) or (2.1), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the leave begins before the day on which the
      certificate is issued, the day from which the health care
      practitioner certifies that the child or adult, as the case may
      be, is critically ill; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ends on the last day of the week in which either of
    the following occurs:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the child or adult, as the case may be, dies, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the expiry of 52 weeks following the first day
      of the week referred to in paragraph (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggregate leave --- employees

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The aggregate amount of leave that may be taken by
  employees under this section during the period referred to in
  subsection (4) must not exceed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the same critically ill child, 37
    weeks; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the same critically ill adult, 17
    weeks.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No leave may be taken by one or more employees under
  subsection (2.1) before the end of the period referred to in
  subsection (4) if leave was granted under subsection (2) in respect of
  the same person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation --- section 206.3

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} No leave may be taken by one or more employees under
  section 206.3 before the end of the leave taken under subsection (2)
  or (2.1) in respect of the same person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 27, s. 5
- 2017, c. 20, s. 263
- [2018, c. 27, s. 469](#1039854-1201112){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039854-1201112.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-206.4-20171203.html "Link to previous version of section 206.4"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Leave Related to Death or Disappearance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-342667 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[206.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in
  this section.

  [[child]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[child]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is under 25 years
      of age. ([enfant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[crime]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[crime]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an offence under the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46), other than one that is excluded by the
      regulations. ([crime]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[parent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[parent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, with respect to a child, means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person who, in law, is a parent of the child;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person, other than a person referred to in
        paragraph (a), who, in law

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has custody of the child or, in Quebec,
          parental authority over the child,

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is the guardian of the child or, in Quebec,
          the tutor or curator to the person of the child, or

        - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} has [[decision-making
          responsibility]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in
          subsection 2(1) of the [Divorce Act](/eng/acts/D-3.4), in
          respect of the child;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person with whom the child is placed for the
        purposes of adoption under the laws governing adoption in the
        province in which the person resides; or

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a person prescribed to be a parent by
        regulations made under paragraph
        209.4(f). ([parent]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave --- death of child

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employee is entitled to and shall be granted a
  leave of absence from employment of up to 156 weeks if the employee is
  the parent of a child who has died and it is probable, considering the
  circumstances, that the child died as a result of a crime.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave  ---  child who has disappeared

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every employee is entitled to and shall be granted a
  leave of absence from employment of up to 156 weeks if the employee is
  the parent of a child who has disappeared and it is probable,
  considering the circumstances, that the child disappeared as a result
  of a crime.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An employee is not entitled to a leave of absence if
  they are charged with the crime.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period when leave may be taken

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The period during which the employee may take a leave
  of absence

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} begins on the day on which the death or
    disappearance, as the case may be, occurs; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ends 156 weeks after the day on which the death or
    disappearance, as the case may be, occurs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disappearance of child

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraph (5)(b), in the case of a child who
  disappears and who is subsequently found, the period referred to in
  subsection (5) ends

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on the 14th day after the day on which the child is
    found, if the child is found during the 156-week period, but no
    later than the end of the 156-week period; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 156 weeks after the day on which the disappearance
    occurs if subsection (2) applies to the child.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a leave under this section
  ends on the day on which the circumstances are such that it is no
  longer probable that the death or disappearance was the result of a
  crime.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggregate leave  ---  employees

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The aggregate amount of leave that may be taken by
  employees under this section in respect of the same death or
  disappearance of a child --- or the same children who die or disappear
  as a result of the same event --- must not exceed 156 weeks.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 27, s. 6
- [2018, c. 27, s. 470](#1039864-1201117){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039864-1201117.html"}
- [2021, c. 23, s. 249](#1307598-1310171){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1307598-1310171.html"}
- [2023, c. 26, s. 282](#1410019-1416554){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1410019-1416554.html"}
:::

#### [Personal Leave]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1200847 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave --- five days

- **[[206.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee is entitled to and
  shall be granted a leave of absence from employment of up to five days
  in every calendar year for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2021, c. 27, s. 6\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} carrying out responsibilities related to the health
    or care of any of their family members;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} carrying out responsibilities related to the
    education of any of their family members who are under 18 years of
    age;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} addressing any urgent matter concerning themselves
    or their family members;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} attending their citizenship ceremony under the
    [Citizenship Act](/eng/acts/C-29); and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any other reason prescribed by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave with pay

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the employee has completed three consecutive
  months of continuous employment with the employer, the employee is
  entitled to the first three days of the leave with pay at their
  regular rate of wages for their normal hours of work, and such pay
  shall for all purposes be considered to be wages.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division of leave

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The leave of absence may be taken in one or more
  periods. The employer may require that each period of leave be of not
  less than one day's duration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer may, in writing and no later than 15
  days after an employee's return to work, request that the employee
  provide documentation to support the reasons for the leave. The
  employee shall provide that documentation only if it is reasonably
  practicable for them to obtain and provide it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations for the
  purposes of this section, including regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} setting out the other reasons for taking leave
    under paragraph (1)(f);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} defining the expressions "regular rate of wages"
    and "normal hours of work"; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} specifying the persons who are the employee's
    family members.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 33, s. 206](#1037427-1200869){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037427-1200869.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 514](#1037427-1200870){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037427-1200870.html"}
- [2021, c. 27, s. 6](#1334200-1380628){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1334200-1380628.html"}
:::

Version](section-206.6-20190901.html "Link to previous version of section 206.6"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Leave for Victims of Family Violence]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1200871 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[206.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206.7
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in
  subsection (2).

  [[child ]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[child ]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is under 18
      years of age. ([enfant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[parent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[parent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
      subsection 206.5(1) but does not include a curator to the
      person. ([parent]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave --- 10 days

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employee who is a victim of family violence or
  who is the parent of a child who is a victim of family violence is
  entitled to and shall be granted a leave of absence from employment of
  up to 10 days in every calendar year, in order to enable the employee,
  in respect of such violence,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to seek medical attention for themselves or their
    child in respect of a physical or psychological injury or
    disability;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to obtain services from an organization which
    provides services to victims of family violence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to obtain psychological or other professional
    counselling;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to relocate temporarily or permanently;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} to seek legal or law enforcement assistance or to
    prepare for or participate in any civil or criminal legal
    proceeding; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} to take any measures prescribed by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave with pay

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} If the employee has completed three consecutive
  months of continuous employment with the employer, the employee is
  entitled to the first five days of the leave with pay at their regular
  rate of wages for their normal hours of work, and such pay shall for
  all purposes be considered to be wages.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An employee is not entitled to a leave of absence
  with respect to any act of family violence if the employee is charged
  with an offence related to that act or if it is probable, considering
  the circumstances, that the employee committed that act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division of leave

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The leave of absence may be taken in one or more
  periods. The employer may require that each period of leave be of not
  less than one day's duration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The employer may, in writing and no later than 15
  days after an employee's return to work, request the employee to
  provide documentation to support the reasons for the leave. The
  employee shall provide that documentation only if it is reasonably
  practicable for them to obtain and provide it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations defining
  the expressions "regular rate of wages" and "normal hours of work" for
  the purposes of subsection (2.1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 33, s. 206](#1037427-1200894){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037427-1200894.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 514](#1037427-1200895){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037427-1200895.html"}
- [2021, c. 23, s. 250](#1307609-1310170){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1307609-1310170.html"}
:::

:::

#### [Leave for Traditional Aboriginal Practices]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1200896 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave --- five days

- **[[206.8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206.8
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee who is an Aboriginal
  person and who has completed three consecutive months of continuous
  employment with an employer is entitled to and shall be granted a
  leave of absence from employment of up to five days in every calendar
  year, in order to enable the employee to engage in traditional
  Aboriginal practices, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} hunting;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} fishing;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} harvesting; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any practice prescribed by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division of leave

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The leave of absence may be taken in one or more
  periods. The employer may require that each period of leave be not
  less than one day's duration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employer may, in writing and no later than 15
  days after an employee's return to work, request the employee to
  provide documentation that shows the employee as an Aboriginal person.
  The employee shall provide that documentation only if it is reasonably
  practicable for him or her to obtain and provide it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
  [[Aboriginal]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,
  [[Aboriginal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means Indian, Inuit or Métis.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 33, s. 206](#1037427-1200911){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037427-1200911.html"}
:::

#### [Leave for Court or Jury Duty]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1201118 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entitlement to leave

**[[206.9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206.9 .sectionLabel}** Every employee is
entitled to and shall be granted a leave of absence from employment to
attend court to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} act as a witness in a proceeding;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} act as a juror in a proceeding; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} participate in a jury selection process.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 471](#1039866-1201125){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039866-1201125.html"}
:::

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-342696 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification to employer

- **[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee who intends to take a
  leave of absence from employment under section 206 or 206.1 shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} unless there is a valid reason for not doing so,
    give at least four weeks notice in writing to the employer before
    the day on which the leave is to begin; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} inform the employer in writing of the length of
    leave intended to be taken.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- valid reason

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If there is a valid reason for not providing notice
  in accordance with paragraph (1)(a), the employee shall notify the
  employer in writing as soon as possible that the employee intends to
  take a leave of absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change in length of leave

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employee who intends to take or who is on a
  leave of absence from employment under section 206 or 206.1 shall
  provide the employer with notice in writing of at least four weeks of
  any change in the length of leave intended to be taken, unless there
  is a valid reason why that notice cannot be given, in which case the
  employee shall provide the employer with notice in writing as soon as
  possible.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 207
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 10
- 1993, c. 42, s. 28
- 2017, c. 20, s. 264
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-207-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 207"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum periods of leave

**[[207.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207.01 .sectionLabel}** Subject to the
regulations, a leave of absence under any of sections 206.3 to 206.5 may
only be taken in one or more periods of not less than one week's
duration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 20, s. 243
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interruption

- **[[207.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207.02
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employee may interrupt a leave of
  absence referred to in any of sections 206.3 to 206.5 in order to be
  absent due to a reason referred to in subsection 239(1) or 239.1(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resumption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The interrupted leave resumes immediately after the
  interruption ends.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- medical leave

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Except to the extent that it is inconsistent with
  subsection 239(7), section 209.1 applies to an employee who
  interrupted the leave in order to be absent due to a reason referred
  to in subsection 239(1).

- [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2021, c. 26, s. 23\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception  --- work-related illness or
  injury

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Except to the extent that it is inconsistent with
  subsections 239.1(3) and (4), section 209.1 applies to an employee who
  interrupted the leave in order to be absent due to a reason referred
  to in subsection 239.1(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 20, s. 243
- [2018, c. 27, s. 472](#1039868-1201128){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039868-1201128.html"}
- [2020, c. 5, s. 40](#1240919-1241941){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1240919-1241941.html"}
- [2020, c. 12, s. 4.4](#1260909-1261937){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1260909-1261937.html"}
- [2021, c. 23, s. 343](#1308102-1387039){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1308102-1387039.html"}
- [2021, c. 23, s. 345](#1308114-1387155){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1308114-1387155.html"}
- [2021, c. 26, s. 23](#1334449-1335513){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1334449-1335513.html"}
- [2021, c. 26, s. 26](#1334468-1387157){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1334468-1387157.html"}

[Previous
Version](section-207.02-20220508.html "Link to previous version of section 207.02"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to employer  --- interruption of
leave

- **[[207.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employee who intends to interrupt
  their leave under subsection 206.1(2.4) or 207.02(1) shall provide the
  employer with a notice in writing of the interruption before or as
  soon as possible after it begins.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to employer  --- resumption of
  leave

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employee shall provide the employer with a notice
  in writing of the day on which they resume their leave before or as
  soon as possible after that day.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 27, s. 7
- 2014, c. 20, s. 244
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-207.1-20121214.html "Link to previous version of section 207.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification to employer --- interruption
for child's hospitalization

- **[[207.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employee who intends to interrupt
  their maternity or parental leave in order to return to work as a
  result of the hospitalization of his or her child shall provide the
  employer with a notice in writing of the interruption as soon as
  possible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer's decision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall, within one week after receiving
  the notice, advise the employee in writing of the employer's decision
  to accept or refuse the employee's return to work.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the employer refuses the interruption or does not
  advise the employee within the week referred to in subsection (2), the
  leave under section 206 or 206.1 is extended by the number of weeks
  during which the child is hospitalized. The aggregate amounts of leave
  referred to in subsection 206.1(3) and section 206.2 are extended by
  the same number of weeks.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer may, in writing and no later than 15
  days after an employee's return to work, require the employee to
  provide a certificate issued by a health care practitioner attesting
  to the child's hospitalization.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}End of interruption

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An employee who intends to return to their maternity
  or parental leave after an interruption shall, as soon as possible,
  advise the employer in writing of the date on which the maternity or
  parental leave is to resume.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The extension referred to in subsection (3) applies
  only once in respect of the same child.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 27, s. 7
- 2017, c. 20, s. 265
- [2018, c. 27, s. 473](#1039870-1201131){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039870-1201131.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-207.2-20171203.html "Link to previous version of section 207.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to employer of leave

- **[[207.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee who takes a leave of
  absence from employment under any of sections 206.3 to 206.9 shall, as
  soon as possible, provide the employer with a notice in writing of the
  reasons for the leave and the length of the leave that they intend to
  take.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of change in length of leave

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employee who is on a leave of absence from
  employment under any of sections 206.3 to 206.9 shall, as soon as
  possible, provide the employer with a notice in writing of any change
  in the length of the leave that they intend to take.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice --- leave of more than four
  weeks

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the length of the leave taken under any of
  sections 206.3 to 206.5 and 206.9 is more than four weeks, the notice
  in writing of any change in the length of the leave shall be provided
  on at least four weeks' notice, unless there is a valid reason why
  that cannot be done.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documentation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer may require the employee to provide
  documentation in support of the reasons for the leave taken under
  section 206.4, 206.5 or 206.9 and of any change in the length of leave
  that the employee intends to take.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return to work postponed

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If an employee who takes a leave of more than four
  weeks under any of sections 206.3 to 206.5 wishes to shorten the
  length of the leave but does not provide the employer with four weeks'
  notice, the employer may postpone the employee's return to work for a
  period of up to four weeks after the day on which the employee informs
  the employer of the new end date of the leave. If the employer informs
  the employee that their return to work is postponed, the employee is
  not entitled to return to work until the day that is indicated by the
  employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed part of leave

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The period of the postponement is deemed to be part
  of the leave.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 27, s. 8
- 2014, c. 20, s. 245
- 2017, c. 20, s. 266
- [2017, c. 33, s. 207](#1037434-1200917){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037434-1200917.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 474](#1039879-1201139){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039879-1201139.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-207.3-20171203.html "Link to previous version of section 207.3"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[208]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), no
  employer shall require an employee to take a leave of absence from
  employment because the employee is pregnant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer may require a pregnant employee to take a
  leave of absence from employment if the employee is unable to perform
  an essential function of her job and no appropriate alternative job is
  available for that employee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Length of leave

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A pregnant employee who is unable to perform an
  essential function of her job and for whom no appropriate alternative
  job is available may be required to take a leave of absence from
  employment only for such time as she is unable to perform that
  essential function.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The burden of proving that a pregnant employee is
  unable to perform an essential function of her job rests with the
  employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 208
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 10
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[208.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208.1 .sectionLabel}** Regardless of the
time at which an employee makes a request under section 204, the rights
and obligations provided under sections 204 and 205 take precedence over
the application of subsection 208(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 42, s. 29
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to notice of employment
opportunities

**[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209 .sectionLabel}** Every employee who
intends to or is required to take a leave of absence from employment
under this Division is entitled, on written request therefor, to be
informed in writing of every employment, promotion or training
opportunity that arises during the period when the employee is on leave
of absence from employment and for which the employee is qualified, and
on receiving such a request every employer of such an employee shall so
inform the employee.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 209
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 10
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Resumption of employment in same position

- **[[209.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee who takes or is
  required to take a leave of absence from employment under this
  Division is entitled to be reinstated in the position that the
  employee occupied when the leave of absence from employment commenced,
  and every employer of such an employee shall, on the expiration of any
  such leave, reinstate the employee in that position.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Comparable position

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where for any valid reason an employer cannot
  reinstate an employee in the position referred to in subsection (1),
  the employer shall reinstate the employee in a comparable position
  with the same wages and benefits and in the same location.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Wages and benefits affected by
  reorganization

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where an employee takes leave under this Division
  and, during the period of that leave, the wages and benefits of the
  group of employees of which that employee is a member are changed as
  part of a plan to reorganize the industrial establishment in which
  that group is employed, that employee is entitled, on being reinstated
  in employment under this section, to receive the wages and benefits in
  respect of that employment that that employee would have been entitled
  to receive had that employee been working when the reorganization took
  place.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of changes in wages and benefits

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer of every employee who is on a leave of
  absence from employment under this Division and whose wages and
  benefits would be changed as a result of a reorganization referred to
  in subsection (3) shall notify the employee in writing of that change
  as soon as possible.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 10
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to benefits

- **[[209.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The pension, health and disability
  benefits and the seniority of any employee who takes or is required to
  take a leave of absence from employment under this Division shall
  accumulate during the entire period of the leave.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contributions by employee

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where contributions are required from an employee in
  order for the employee to be entitled to a benefit referred to in
  subsection (1), the employee is responsible for and must, within a
  reasonable time, pay those contributions for the period of any leave
  of absence under this Division unless, before taking leave or within a
  reasonable time thereafter, the employee notifies the employer of the
  employee's intention to discontinue contributions during that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contributions by employer

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} An employer who pays contributions in respect of a
  benefit referred to in subsection (1) shall continue to pay those
  contributions during an employee's leave of absence under this
  Division in at least the same proportion as if the employee were not
  on leave unless the employee does not pay the employee's
  contributions, if any, within a reasonable time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to pay contributions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of calculating the pension, health
  and disability benefits of an employee in respect of whom
  contributions have not been paid as required by subsections (2) and
  (2.1), the benefits shall not accumulate during the leave of absence
  and employment on the employee's return to work shall be deemed to be
  continuous with employment before the employee's absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed continuous employment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of calculating benefits of an
  employee who takes or is required to take a leave of absence from
  employment under this Division, other than benefits referred to in
  subsection (1), employment on the employee's return to work shall be
  deemed to be continuous with employment before the employee's absence.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 10, c. 43 (3rd Supp.), s. 1
- 2001, c. 34, s. 21(F)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of leave

**[[209.21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.21 .sectionLabel}** Notwithstanding
the provisions of any income-replacement scheme or any insurance plan in
force at the workplace, an employee who takes a leave of absence under
this Division is entitled to benefits under the scheme or plan on the
same terms as any employee who is absent from work for health-related
reasons and is entitled to benefits under the scheme or plan.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 42, s. 30
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Status of certificate

**[[209.22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.22 .sectionLabel}** A certificate
issued by a health care practitioner under this Division is conclusive
proof of the statements contained in it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 42, s. 30
- [2018, c. 27, s. 475](#1039886-1201142){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039886-1201142.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-209.22-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 209.22"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[209.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No employer shall dismiss, suspend,
  lay off, demote or discipline an employee because the employee is
  pregnant or has applied for leave of absence in accordance with this
  Division or take into account the pregnancy of an employee or the
  intention of an employee to take leave of absence from employment
  under this Division in any decision to promote or train the employee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The prohibitions set out in subsection (1) also apply
  in respect of an employee who has taken a leave of absence under any
  of sections 206.3 to 206.9.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 10
- 2003, c. 15, s. 28
- 2012, c. 27, s. 9
- [2017, c. 33, s. 208](#1037439-1200920){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037439-1200920.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 476](#1039888-1201145){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039888-1201145.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-209.3-20130101.html "Link to previous version of section 209.3"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[209.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.4 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} specifying the absences from employment that are
  deemed not to have interrupted continuous employment referred to in
  any of sections 206.6 to 206.8;

- [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2017, c. 20, s. 267\]]{.Repealed}

- [(a.2)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the maximum number of periods of leave
  of absence that an employee may take under any of sections 206.3 to
  206.5;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} specifying what does, or does not, constitute an
  essential function of a job referred to in section 208;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} specifying what does not constitute a valid reason
  for not reinstating an employee in the position referred to in
  subsection 209.1(2);

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} enlarging the meaning of
  [[care]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} and [[support]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}
  in subsections 206.3(1) and 206.4(1), and of [[critically ill
  adult]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} and [[critically ill
  child]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 206.4(1);

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} prescribing other persons to be included in the
  meanings of [[family member]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsections
  206.3(1) and 206.4(1);

- [(e.1)]{.lawlabel} adapting the terminology of the definitions of
  [[care]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, [[critically ill
  adult]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, [[critically ill
  child]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, [[family member]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}
  and [[support]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in the regulations made under
  the [Employment Insurance Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6) for the purposes of
  the definitions of those terms in subsections 206.3(1) and 206.4(1) of
  this Act;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} prescribing offences to be excluded from the
  definition of [[crime]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 206.5(1)
  and prescribing other persons to be included in the definition of
  [[parent]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in that subsection;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} prescribing shorter periods of consecutive months of
  continuous employment for the purposes of subsections 206.6(2),
  206.7(2.1) and 206.8(1);

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} prescribing cases, other than the one set out in
  subsection 206.5(4), in which an employee is not entitled to a leave
  of absence and cases in which an employee is entitled to a leave of
  absence even if they are charged with the crime;

- [(h.1)]{.lawlabel} defining [[family violence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}
  for the purposes of section 206.7;

- [(h.2)]{.lawlabel} prescribing cases, other than those set out in
  subsection 206.7(3), in which an employee is not entitled to a leave
  of absence and cases in which, despite that subsection, an employee is
  entitled to a leave of absence under subsection 206.7(2);

- [(h.3)]{.lawlabel} prescribing documentation that the employer may
  request under any of subsections 206.6(4), 206.7(5) and 206.8(3);

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} prescribing documentation that the employer may
  require under subsection 207.3(4);

- [(j)]{.lawlabel} specifying the circumstances in which a leave under
  this Division may be interrupted; and

- [(k)]{.lawlabel} extending the period within which a leave under this
  Division may be taken.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 10
- 1993, c. 42, s. 31
- 2003, c. 15, s. 29
- 2012, c. 27, s. 10
- 2014, c. 20, s. 246
- 2017, c. 20, s. 267
- [2017, c. 33, s. 209](#1037448-1200926){.wb-lbx
