[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of section 189

**[[209.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.5 .sectionLabel}** Section 189 applies
for the purposes of this Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 10
:::

### [DIVISION VIII]{.HLabel2}[Bereavement Leave]{.HTitleText2} {#h-342840 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee entitled

- **[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee is entitled to and
  shall be granted, in the event of the death of a member of their
  immediate family or a family member in respect of whom the employee
  is, at the time of the death, on leave under section 206.3 or 206.4, a
  leave of absence from employment of up to 10 days that may be taken
  during the period that begins on the day on which the death occurs and
  ends six weeks after the latest of the days on which any funeral,
  burial or memorial service of the deceased person occurs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} At the request of the employee, the employer may
  extend, in writing, the period during which the leave of absence from
  employment may be taken.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division of leave

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The leave of absence may be taken in one or two
  periods. The employer may require that any period of leave be of not
  less than one day's duration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to employer

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} Every employee who takes the leave of absence
  shall, as soon as possible, provide the employer with written notice
  of the beginning of any period of leave of absence and of the length
  of that leave.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Bereavement leave with pay

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the employee has completed three consecutive
  months of continuous employment with the employer, the employee is
  entitled to the first three days of the leave with pay at their
  regular rate of wages for their normal hours of work, and such pay
  shall for all purposes be considered to be wages.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} defining the expression "immediate family" for the
    purposes of subsection (1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} defining the expressions "regular rate of wages"
    and "normal hours of work" for the purposes of subsection (2); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of this Division, defining the
    absences from employment that shall be deemed not to have
    interrupted continuity of employment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of section 189

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Section 189 applies for the purposes of this
  Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 210
- [2017, c. 33, s. 210](#1037455-1200937){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037455-1200937.html"}
- [2021, c. 17, s. 1](#1305438-1327101){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1305438-1327101.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-210-20190901.html "Link to previous version of section 210"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION IX]{.HLabel2}[Group Termination of Employment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-342855 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211 .sectionLabel}** In this Division,

[[joint planning committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[joint planning committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a committee
    established pursuant to section 214; ([comité
    mixte]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[redundant employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[redundant employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an employee whose
    employment is to be terminated pursuant to a notice under section
    212; ([surnuméraire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[trade union]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[trade union]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a trade union that is
    certified under Part I to represent any redundant employee or that
    is recognized by an employer of any redundant employee as the
    bargaining agent for that employee. ([syndicat]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1980-81-82-83, c. 89, s. 31
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of group termination

- **[[212]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-212 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  employer who terminates, either simultaneously or within any period
  not exceeding four weeks, the employment of a group of 50 or more
  employees employed by the employer within a particular industrial
  establishment, or of such lesser number of employees as prescribed by
  regulations applicable to the employer made under paragraph 227(b),
  shall, in addition to any notice required to be given under section
  230, give notice to the Head, in writing, of his intention to so
  terminate at least 16 weeks before the date of termination of the
  employment of the employee in the group whose employment is first to
  be terminated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies of notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A copy of any notice given to the Head under
  subsection (1) must be given immediately by the employer to the
  Minister of Employment and Social Development and the Canada
  Employment Insurance Commission and any trade union representing a
  redundant employee, and if any redundant employee is not represented
  by a trade union, a copy of that notice must be given to the employee
  or immediately posted by the employer in a conspicuous place within
  the industrial establishment in which that employee is employed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A notice referred to in subsection (1) shall set out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date or dates on which the employer intends to
    terminate the employment of any one or more employees;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the estimated number of employees in each
    occupational classification whose employment will be terminated; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} such other information as is prescribed by the
    regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where employer deemed to terminate
  employment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Except where otherwise prescribed by regulation, an
  employer shall, for the purposes of this Division, be deemed to have
  terminated the employment of an employee where the employer lays off
  that employee.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 212
- 1996, c. 11, s. 67
- 2005, c. 34, s. 80
- 2013, c. 40, s. 238
- [2018, c. 27, s. 574](#1040467-1282130){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040467-1282130.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-212-20131212.html "Link to previous version of section 212"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cooperation with Commission

- **[[213]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-213 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employer who gives notice to the Head under section 212 and any trade
  union to which a copy of that notice is given must give the Canada
  Employment Insurance Commission any information requested by it for
  the purpose of assisting any redundant employee and must cooperate
  with the Commission to facilitate the re-establishment in employment
  of that employee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement of benefits

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer who gives notice to the Head under
  section 212 shall give each redundant employee, as soon as possible
  after the notice is so given but in any case not later than two weeks
  before the date of the termination of the employment of the employee,
  a statement in writing setting out, as at the date of the statement,
  his vacation benefits, wages, severance pay and any other benefits and
  pay arising from his employment with that employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 213
- 1996, c. 11, s. 99
- [2018, c. 27, s. 575](#1040472-1282136){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040472-1282136.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-213-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 213"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Establishment of joint planning committee

- **[[214]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-214 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employer who gives notice to the Head under section 212 must, as soon
  as possible after giving the notice, establish a joint planning
  committee consisting of any number of members that is required or
  permitted by this section and sections 215 and 217.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum number of members

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A joint planning committee established under
  subsection (1) shall consist of at least four members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of members

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} At least half of the members of a joint planning
  committee shall be appointed, in accordance with subsections
  215(1), (2) and (3), as representatives of the redundant employees and
  the rest of the members shall be appointed, in accordance with
  subsection 215(5), as representatives of the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 214
- [2018, c. 27, s. 576](#1040474-1282139){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040474-1282139.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-214-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 214"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee representatives

- **[[215]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where all redundant employees are
  represented by a trade union or trade unions, each trade union is
  entitled to appoint at least one member of the joint planning
  committee as a representative of the redundant employees it
  represents.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where no redundant employees are represented by a
  trade union, the employees are entitled to appoint all the members of
  a joint planning committee who are to be their representatives.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where some but not all redundant employees are
  represented by a trade union or trade unions,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} each trade union is entitled to appoint at least
    one member of a joint planning committee as a representative of the
    redundant employees it represents; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employees that are not represented by a trade
    union are entitled to appoint at least one member of a joint
    planning committee as their representative.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Election

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Each person appointed as a member of a joint planning
  committee pursuant to subsection (2) or paragraph (3)(b) shall be
  elected by the redundant employees entitled to appoint the member.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer representatives

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An employer is entitled to appoint, as his
  representatives on a joint planning committee, a number of members not
  exceeding the number of members to be appointed to the committee
  pursuant to subsections (1), (2) and (3).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1980-81-82-83, c. 89, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for appointment

**[[216]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216 .sectionLabel}** The members of a joint
planning committee must be appointed and must convene for their first
sitting within two weeks after the date of the notice given to the Head
under section 212.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 216
- [2018, c. 27, s. 577](#1040476-1282143){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040476-1282143.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-216-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 216"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to appoint

**[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217 .sectionLabel}** If a trade union fails,
or redundant employees fail, to appoint a member to a joint planning
committee as provided in sections 214 and 215, the Head may, on
application of any redundant employee, appoint a member to the committee
in lieu of that trade union or those employees, as the case may be, and
that member is a representative of the redundant employees represented
by the trade union or of the redundant employees who failed to appoint
the member, as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 217
- [2018, c. 27, s. 577](#1040476-1282147){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040476-1282147.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-217-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 217"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of membership

**[[218]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-218 .sectionLabel}** On completion of the
appointment of the members of a joint planning committee, the employer
shall post the names of those members in a conspicuous place within the
industrial establishment in which the redundant employees are employed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1980-81-82-83, c. 89, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

- **[[219]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-219
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Division, a joint
  planning committee may determine its own procedure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Co-chairpersons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The members of a joint planning committee shall elect
  from among themselves two co-chairpersons, one being a representative
  of the redundant employees selected by their representatives and the
  other being a representative of the employer selected by his
  representatives.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sittings

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The co-chairpersons of a joint planning committee
  may, after consultation with the other members of the committee, fix
  the time and place of its sittings and shall notify the members of the
  time and place so fixed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quorum

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A majority of the members of a joint planning
  committee in office, at least half of which majority are
  representatives of the redundant employees, constitutes a quorum, but
  the members shall not proceed in the absence of any member of the
  committee at any sitting unless the absent member has been given
  reasonable notice of the sitting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vacancy

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where any vacancy occurs in the membership of a joint
  planning committee before the committee has completed its work, the
  vacancy shall be filled forthwith in the manner provided in this
  Division for the selection of the person who vacated that membership.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A vacancy in the membership of a joint planning
  committee does not invalidate the constitution of the committee or
  impair the right of the members of the committee in office to act, if
  the number of those members is not less than a quorum.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A decision or other act or thing taken or done by a
  majority of the members of a joint planning committee present at a
  sitting of the committee, if the members present constitute a quorum,
  shall be deemed to have been taken or done by the committee.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 219
- 1998, c. 26, s. 61(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Wages

**[[220]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220 .sectionLabel}** A member of a joint
planning committee is entitled to such time from work as is necessary to
attend sittings of the committee or to carry out any other functions as
such a member, and any time spent by the member in carrying out any
functions as a member shall, for the purpose of calculating wages owing
to the member, be deemed to have been spent at his work.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1980-81-82-83, c. 89, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Object of joint planning committee

- **[[221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-221 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} It
  is the object of a joint planning committee to develop an adjustment
  program to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} eliminate the necessity for the termination of
    employment; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} minimize the impact of the termination of
    employment on the redundant employees and to assist those employees
    in obtaining other employment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of matters considered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In attaining its object under subsection (1), a joint
  planning committee may, unless the members of the committee agree
  otherwise, deal only with such matters as are normally the
  subject-matter of collective agreement in relation to the termination
  of employment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasonable effort

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The members of a joint planning committee shall
  cooperate and make every reasonable effort to develop an adjustment
  program as expeditiously as possible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cooperation with committee

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The employer and any trade union or redundant
  employees who appointed the members of a joint planning committee
  shall cooperate with and assist the committee in developing an
  adjustment program.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1980-81-82-83, c. 89, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Supplying of information

- **[[222]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  employer and any trade union or redundant employees who appointed the
  members of a joint planning committee shall, on request of any member
  of the committee, forthwith provide the committee with such personal
  information relating to any redundant employee as the committee may
  reasonably require for its work.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Head

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Head may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} monitor and, on request, assist in the
    establishment and operation of a joint planning committee; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} attend any sittings of a joint planning committee
    as an observer.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 222
- [2018, c. 27, s. 578](#1040478-1282151){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040478-1282151.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-222-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 222"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to Minister for arbitrator

- **[[223]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-223
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where all members of a joint
  planning committee who are representatives of the redundant employees
  agree to do so or where all members of a joint planning committee who
  are representatives of the employer agree to do so, those members may,
  after six weeks from the date of the notice to the Minister under
  section 212, apply jointly to the Minister for the appointment of an
  arbitrator if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the committee has not then completed developing an
    adjustment program; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the committee has completed developing an
    adjustment program, but those members are not satisfied with the
    program or any part of the program.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form and contents of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application under subsection (1) shall be in
  writing and signed by the members making the application and shall set
  out the matters, if any, in dispute respecting the adjustment program.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1980-81-82-83, c. 89, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of arbitrator

- **[[224]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-224 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, on application under subsection 223(1), appoint an
  arbitrator to assist the joint planning committee in the development
  of an adjustment program and to resolve any matters in dispute
  respecting the adjustment program.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}The Minister shall notify and send a
  statement of matters in dispute

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an arbitrator is appointed under subsection
  (1), the Minister shall forthwith

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} notify, in writing, the joint planning committee of
    the decision to appoint an arbitrator and of the name of the
    arbitrator; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the application under subsection 223(1) sets out
    matters in dispute respecting an adjustment program, send to the
    arbitrator and to the joint planning committee a statement setting
    out any matters in dispute respecting the adjustment program that
    the arbitrator is to resolve.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction on matters included in
  statement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A statement referred to in subsection (2) shall be
  restricted to such of those matters set out in the application under
  subsection 223(1) as the Minister deems appropriate and as are
  normally the subject-matter of collective agreement in relation to
  termination of employment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of arbitrator

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An arbitrator shall assist the joint planning
  committee in the development of an adjustment program and the
  arbitrator, if sent a statement pursuant to subsection (2), shall,
  within four weeks after receiving the statement or such longer period
  as the Minister may specify,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} consider the matters set out in the statement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} render a decision thereon; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} send a copy of the decision with the reasons
    therefor to the joint planning committee and to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An arbitrator may not

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} review the decision of the employer to terminate
    the employment of the redundant employees; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} delay the termination of employment of the
    redundant employees.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of arbitrator

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} In relation to any proceeding before an arbitrator
  under this section, the arbitrator may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determine the procedure to be followed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} administer oaths and solemn affirmations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} receive and accept such evidence and information on
    oath, affidavit or otherwise as the arbitrator sees fit, whether or
    not the evidence is admissible in a court of law;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} make such examination of documents containing
    personal information relating to any redundant employee and such
    inquiries relating to any redundant employee as the arbitrator deems
    necessary;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} require an employer to post and keep posted in
    appropriate places any notice that the arbitrator considers
    necessary to bring to the attention of any redundant employees any
    matter relating to the proceeding; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} authorize any person to do anything described in
    paragraph (b) or (d) that the arbitrator may do and to report to the
    arbitrator thereon.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1980-81-82-83, c. 89, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicable provisions

**[[225]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-225 .sectionLabel}** Sections 58 and 66
apply, with such modifications as the circumstances require, in respect
of a decision of an arbitrator under section 224 as though it were a
decision referred to in those sections.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1980-81-82-83, c. 89, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation of adjustment program

**[[226]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226 .sectionLabel}** On completion of the
development of an adjustment program, the employer shall implement the
program and the joint planning committee and any trade union or
redundant employees who appointed the members of the committee shall
cooperate with and assist the employer in implementing the program.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1980-81-82-83, c. 89, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[227]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make regulations for carrying out the purposes and provisions of
this Division and, without restricting the generality of the foregoing,
may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} exempting employers from the application of this
  Division in respect of the termination of employment of employees
  employed on a seasonal or irregular basis;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} requiring employers employing employees in a
  particular occupational classification, in a particular industry or in
  an industrial establishment that is within an area or region described
  in the regulations, to comply with the provisions of this Division in
  respect of terminations of employment of groups of employees numbering
  less than fifty but greater than a number prescribed in the
  regulations;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} prescribing information to be set out in a notice
  referred to in subsection 212(1); and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} prescribing circumstances in which a lay-off of an
  employee shall not be deemed to be a termination of his employment by
  his employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. 17(2nd Supp.), s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Waiver of application of Division

**[[228]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-228 .sectionLabel}** On the submission of
any person, the Minister may, by order and subject to any terms or
conditions specified in the order, waive the application of this
Division, or any provision thereof, in respect of any industrial
establishment or of any class of employees therein specified in the
order if it is shown to the satisfaction of the Minister that the
application of this Division, or any provision thereof, as the case may
be, in respect of any industrial establishment

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} would be or is unduly prejudicial to the interests of
  the employees therein or to any class of employees therein;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} would be or is unduly prejudicial to the interests of
  the employer of those employees;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} would be or is seriously detrimental to the operation
  of the industrial establishment; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} is not necessary, because measures for the assistance
  of redundant employees at that establishment that are substantially
  the same or to the same effect as the measures established by this
  Division or that provision, as the case may be, have been established
  by collective agreement or otherwise.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. 17(2nd Supp.), s. 16
- 1980-81-82-83, c. 89, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of sections 214 to 226

- **[[229]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-229
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Sections 214 to 226 do not apply in
  respect of any redundant employees who are represented by a trade
  union if the trade union and the employer are bound by a collective
  agreement containing

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provisions that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} specify procedures by which any matters relating
      to the termination of employment in the industrial establishment
      at which those employees are employed may be negotiated and
      finally settled, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} are intended to minimize the impact of
      termination of employment on the employees represented by the
      trade union and to assist those employees in obtaining other
      employment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provisions that specify that those sections do not
    apply in respect of the employees represented by the trade union.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Sections 214 to 226 do not apply in respect of any
  redundant employees who are represented by a trade union if the
  termination of the employment of those employees is the result of
  technological change as defined in subsection 51(1) and sections 52,
  54 and 55 apply or would, but for subsection 51(2), apply to the trade
  union and the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1980-81-82-83, c. 89, s. 33
:::

### [DIVISION X]{.HLabel2}[Individual Terminations of Employment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-343035 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[229.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-229.1 .sectionLabel}** This Division does
not apply to an employee whose termination of employment is by way of
dismissal for just cause.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 483](#1039937-1441157){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039937-1441157.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer's duty

- **[[230]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-230 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employer who terminates the employment of an employee must give the
  employee

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} notice in writing of the employer's intention to
    terminate their employment on a date specified in the notice, at
    least the applicable number of weeks set out in subsection (1.1)
    before that date;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} wages in lieu of notice, at their regular rate of
    wages for their regular hours of work, for at least the applicable
    number of weeks set out in subsection (1.1); or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any combination of notice and amounts of wages in
    lieu of notice so that the total of the number of weeks of notice in
    writing and the number of weeks for which wages are paid in lieu of
    notice is equivalent to at least the applicable number of weeks set
    out in subsection (1.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(1.01)]{.lawlabel} The employer's obligation to give and the
  employee's right to receive notice or wages in lieu of notice under
  subsection (1) apply whether or not the employee has a right to avail
  themselves of any procedure for redress under this Part, including
  under subsection 240(1), with respect to the termination of their
  employment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice period

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The applicable number of weeks for the purposes of
  subsections (1) and (2) is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} two weeks, if the employee has completed at least
    three consecutive months of continuous employment with the employer;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} three weeks, if the employee has completed at least
    three consecutive years of continuous employment with the employer;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} four weeks, if the employee has completed at least
    four consecutive years of continuous employment with the employer;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} five weeks, if the employee has completed at least
    five consecutive years of continuous employment with the employer;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} six weeks, if the employee has completed at least
    six consecutive years of continuous employment with the employer;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} seven weeks, if the employee has completed at least
    seven consecutive years of continuous employment with the employer;
    and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} eight weeks, if the employee has completed at least
    eight consecutive years of continuous employment with the employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to trade union

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an employer is bound by a collective agreement
  that contains a provision authorizing an employee whose position
  becomes redundant to displace another employee on the basis of
  seniority, and the position of an employee who is so authorized
  becomes redundant, the employer must give at least the applicable
  number of weeks' notice set out in subsection (1.1) in writing to the
  trade union that is a party to the collective agreement and to the
  employee that the employee's position has become redundant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights of displaced employee

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, any employee who is
  displaced and whose employment is terminated is entitled to and shall
  be given notice or wages in lieu of notice under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement of benefits

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} An employer must give any employee whose employment
  is terminated a statement in writing that sets out their vacation
  benefits, wages, severance pay and any other benefits and pay arising
  from their employment with the employer as at the date of the
  statement. The statement must be given to the employee

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an employee who receives notice
    under paragraph (1)(a), as soon as possible, but not later than two
    weeks before the date of the termination of their employment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an employee who receives wages in
    lieu of notice under paragraph (1)(b), not later than the date of
    the termination of their employment; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an employee who receives a
    combination of notice and wages in lieu of notice under paragraph
    (1)(c), as soon as possible, but not later than two weeks before the
    date of the termination of their employment unless the period of
    notice is shorter, in which case, the day on which notice is given
    to the employee of the date of the termination of their employment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where employer deemed to terminate
  employment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Except where otherwise prescribed by regulation, an
  employer shall, for the purposes of this Division, be deemed to have
  terminated the employment of an employee when the employer lays off
  that employee.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 230
- [2018, c. 27, s. 485](#1039941-1441160){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039941-1441160.html"}
- [2024, c. 17, s. 249](#1467838-1481326){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1467838-1481326.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-230-20240201.html "Link to previous version of section 230"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions of employment

**[[231]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231 .sectionLabel}** Where notice is given
by an employer pursuant to subsection 230(1), the employer

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall not thereafter reduce the rate of wages or
  alter any other term or condition of employment of the employee to
  whom the notice was given except with the written consent of the
  employee; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall, between the time when the notice is given and
  the date specified therein, pay to the employee his regular rate of
  wages for his regular hours of work.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. 17(2nd Supp.), s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expiration of notice

**[[232]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-232 .sectionLabel}** Where an employee to
whom notice is given by his employer pursuant to subsection 230(1)
continues to be employed by the employer for more than two weeks after
the date specified in the notice, his employment shall not, except with
the written consent of the employee, be terminated except by way of
dismissal for just cause unless the employer again complies with
subsection 230(1) in respect of the employee.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. 17(2nd Supp.), s. 16
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[233]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-233 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing circumstances in which a lay-off of an
  employee shall not be deemed to be a termination of his employment by
  his employer; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s.
  11\]]{.Repealed}

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} defining for the purposes of this Division the
  absences from employment that shall be deemed not to have interrupted
  continuity of employment and the expression "regular hours of work".

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 233
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of section 189

**[[234]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234 .sectionLabel}** Section 189 applies for
the purposes of this Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. 17(2nd Supp.), s. 16
:::

### [DIVISION XI]{.HLabel2}[Severance Pay]{.HTitleText2} {#h-343070 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum rate

- **[[235]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-235 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employer who terminates the employment of an employee who has
  completed twelve consecutive months of continuous employment by the
  employer shall, except where the termination is by way of dismissal
  for just cause, pay to the employee the greater of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} two days wages at the employee's regular rate of
    wages for his regular hours of work in respect of each completed
    year of employment that is within the term of the employee's
    continuous employment by the employer, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} five days wages at the employee's regular rate of
    wages for his regular hours of work.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The employer's obligation to pay and the employee's
  right to receive the amount under subsection (1) apply whether or not
  the employee has a right to avail themselves of any procedure for
  redress under this Part, including under subsection 240(1), with
  respect to the termination of their employment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Circumstances deemed to be termination
  and deemed not to be termination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Division,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} except where otherwise provided by regulation, an
    employer shall be deemed to have terminated the employment of an
    employee when the employer lays off that employee.

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2011, c. 24, s. 167\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 235
- R.S., 1985, c. 32 (2nd Supp.), s. 41
- 2011, c. 24, s. 167
- [2024, c. 17, s. 250](#1467840-1481329){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1467840-1481329.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-235-20121215.html "Link to previous version of section 235"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[236]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-236 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make regulations for the purposes of this Division

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing circumstances in which a lay-off of an
  employee shall not be deemed to be a termination of the employee's
  employment by his employer;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s.
  12\]]{.Repealed}

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} establishing methods for determining whether
  severance benefits provided to an employee under a plan established by
  an employer are equivalent to any benefits required to be paid to the
  employee under this Division; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} defining the absences from employment that shall be
  deemed not to have interrupted continuity of employment and the
  expression "regular hours of work".

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 236
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 12
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of section 189

**[[237]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-237 .sectionLabel}** Section 189 applies for
the purposes of this Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. 17(2nd Supp.), s. 16
:::

### [DIVISION XII]{.HLabel2}[Garnishment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-343094 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[238]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238 .sectionLabel}** No employer shall
dismiss, suspend, lay off, demote or discipline an employee on the
ground that garnishment proceedings may be or have been taken with
respect to the employee.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 238
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 13
:::

### [DIVISION XII.1]{.HLabel2}[Reimbursement of Work-related Expenses]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1418616 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entitlement

- **[[238.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), an
  employee is entitled to and the employer shall provide reimbursement
  of reasonable work-related expenses.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employee is not entitled to be reimbursed for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an expense that is ineligible under any regulation
    made under this Division;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an employee who is subject to a
    collective agreement, an expense that the employee is required to
    pay in accordance with the collective agreement or any other written
    agreement between the trade union and the employer; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an employee who is not subject to a
    collective agreement, an expense that the employee is required to
    pay in accordance with any written agreement between themselves and
    the employer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The employer shall pay the employee any amount that
  is payable under this section

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an employee who is subject to a
    collective agreement, within the time limit set out under the
    collective agreement or any other written agreement between the
    trade union and the employer;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an employee who is not subject to a
    collective agreement, within the time limit set out under a written
    agreement between themselves and the employer; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, within the time limit prescribed
    by regulation.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 486](#1039943-1418631){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039943-1418631.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[238.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238.2 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may make regulations for the purposes of this Division including
regulations prescribing factors to consider in determining if an expense
is or is not work-related and prescribing factors to consider in
determining if an expense is or is not reasonable.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 486](#1039943-1418635){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039943-1418635.html"}
:::

### [DIVISION XIII]{.HLabel2}[Medical Leave]{.HTitleText2} {#h-343099 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entitlement to leave

- **[[239]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employee is entitled to and
  shall be granted a medical leave of absence from employment of up to
  27 weeks as a result of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} personal illness or injury;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} organ or tissue donation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} medical appointments during working hours; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} quarantine.

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2021, c. 23, s. 344\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave with pay

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (1.21) and the regulations,
  an employee earns, as of the first day on which this subsection
  applies to the employee,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} after completing 30 days of continuous employment
    with an employer, three days of medical leave of absence with pay;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} following the period of 30 days referred to in
    paragraph (a), at the beginning of each month after completing one
    month of continuous employment with the employer, one day of medical
    leave of absence with pay.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum of 10 days

  [(1.21)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the regulations, an employee is
  entitled to earn up to 10 days of medical leave of absence with pay in
  a calendar year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rate of wages

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} Each day of medical leave of absence with pay that
  an employee takes must be paid at their regular rate of wages for
  their normal hours of work, and that pay is for all purposes
  considered to be wages.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual carry forward

  [(1.4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the regulations, each day of medical
  leave of absence with pay that an employee does not take in a calendar
  year is to be carried forward to January 1 of the following calendar
  year and decreases, by one, the maximum number of days that can be
  earned in that calendar year under subsection (1.21).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Division of leave with pay

  [(1.5)]{.lawlabel} The medical leave of absence with pay may be taken
  in one or more periods. The employer may require that each period of
  leave be of not less than one day's duration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The employer may, in writing and no later than 15
  days after the return to work of an employee who has taken a medical
  leave of absence of at least five consecutive days, require the
  employee to provide a certificate issued by a health care practitioner
  certifying that the employee was incapable of working for the period
  of their medical leave of absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to employer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an employee intends to take a medical leave of
  absence, they must give written notice to the employer of the day on
  which the leave is to begin and the expected duration of the leave at
  least four weeks before that day, unless there is a valid reason why
  that notice cannot be given, in which case the employee must provide
  the employer with written notice as soon as possible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change --- in length of leave

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An employee must provide the employer with written
  notice of any change in the length of their medical leave of absence
  as soon as possible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employment opportunities

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An employee is entitled, on written request, to be
  informed in writing of every employment, promotion or training
  opportunity that arises during the period when the employee is on a
  medical leave of absence under this Division and for which the
  employee is qualified, and on receiving that request, the employer
  must provide the information to the employee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (7), an employer is prohibited
  from dismissing, suspending, laying off, demoting or disciplining an
  employee because the employee intends to take or has taken a medical
  leave of absence or taking such an intention or absence into account
  in any decision to promote or train the employee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An employer may assign to a different position, with
  different terms and conditions of employment, any employee who, after
  a medical leave of absence, is unable to perform the work performed by
  the employee prior to the absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Benefits continue

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The pension, health and disability benefits and the
  seniority of an employee who is absent from work due to medical leave
  under this Division accumulate during the entire period of the medical
  leave of absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contributions by employee

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If contributions are required from an employee in
  order for the employee to be entitled to a benefit referred to in
  subsection (8), the employee is responsible for and must, within a
  reasonable time, pay those contributions for the period of any medical
  leave of absence unless, at the commencement of the absence or within
  a reasonable time after, the employee notifies the employer of the
  employee's intention to discontinue contributions during that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contributions by employer

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} An employer who pays contributions in respect of a
  benefit referred to in subsection (8) must continue to pay those
  contributions during an employee's medical leave of absence in at
  least the same proportion as if the employee were not absent, unless
  the employee does not pay the employee's contributions, if any, within
  a reasonable time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to pay contributions

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of calculating the pension, health
  and disability benefits of an employee in respect of whom
  contributions have not been paid as required by subsections (9) and
  (10), the benefits do not accumulate during the medical leave of
  absence and employment on the employee's return to work is deemed to
  be continuous with employment before the employee's absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed continuous employment

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of calculating benefits, other than
  benefits referred to in subsection (8), of an employee who is absent
  from work due to medical leave under this Division, employment on the
  employee's return to work shall be deemed to be continuous with
  employment before the employee's absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} defining terms for the purposes of this Division,
    including "regular rate of wages" and "normal hours of work";

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} modifying subsection (1.2), (1.21) or (1.4) if, in
    the opinion of the Governor in Council, employees or classes of
    employees will, despite the modification, earn periods of medical
    leave of absence with pay that are substantially equivalent to the
    period provided for in subsection (1.21); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} providing for employees or classes of employees to
    earn periods of medical leave of absence with pay other than in
    accordance with subsection (1.2) if, in the opinion of the Governor
    in Council, the periods of medical leave of absence with pay are
    substantially equivalent to the period provided for in subsection
    (1.21).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of section 189

  [(14)]{.lawlabel} Section 189 applies for the purposes of this
  Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 239
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 14, c. 43 (3rd Supp.), s. 2
- 1993, c. 42, s. 32
- 2001, c. 34, s. 22(F)
- 2012, c. 27, s. 11
- [2018, c. 27, s. 487](#1039945-1201182){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039945-1201182.html"}

### [DIVISION XIII.1]{.HLabel2}[Work-related Illness and Injury]{.HTitleText2} {#h-343125 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[239.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4) and to the
  regulations made under this Division, no employer shall dismiss,
  suspend, lay off, demote or discipline an employee because of absence
  from work due to work-related illness or injury.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer's obligation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall subscribe to a plan that
  provides an employee who is absent from work due to work-related
  illness or injury with wage replacement, payable at an equivalent rate
  to that provided for under the applicable workers' compensation
  legislation in the employee's province of permanent residence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return to work

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the regulations, the employer shall, where
  reasonably practicable, return an employee to work after the
  employee's absence due to work-related illness or injury.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An employer may assign to a different position, with
  different terms and conditions of employment, any employee who, after
  an absence due to work-related illness or injury, is unable to perform
  the work performed by the employee prior to the absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Benefits continue

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The pension, health and disability benefits and the
  seniority of an employee who is absent from work due to work-related
  illness or injury shall accumulate during the entire period of the
  absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contributions by employee

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where contributions are required from an employee in
  order for the employee to be entitled to a benefit referred to in
  subsection (5), the employee is responsible for and must, within a
  reasonable time, pay those contributions for the period of any absence
  due to work-related illness or injury unless, at the beginning of the
  absence or within a reasonable time thereafter, the employee notifies
  the employer of the employee's intention to discontinue contributions
  during that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contributions by employer

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An employer who pays contributions in respect of a
  benefit referred to in subsection (5) shall continue to pay those
  contributions during an employee's absence due to work-related illness
  or injury in at least the same proportion as if the employee were not
  absent, unless the employee does not pay the employee's contributions,
  if any, within a reasonable time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to pay contributions

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of calculating the pension, health
  and disability benefits of an employee in respect of whom
  contributions have not been paid as required by subsections (6) and
  (7), the benefits shall not accumulate during the absence, and
  employment on the employee's return to work shall be deemed to be
  continuous with employment before the employee's absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed continuous employment

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of calculating benefits, other than
  benefits referred to in subsection (5), of an employee who is absent
  from work due to work-related illness or injury, employment on the
  employee's return to work shall be deemed to be continuous with
  employment before the employee's absence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations for
  carrying out the purposes of this Division and, without restricting
  the generality of the foregoing, may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for determining the duration of the employer's
    obligation under subsection (3);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} providing terms and conditions applicable to the
    employer under subsections (1) and (3) in the event of any
    termination of employment, lay-off or discontinuance of a function
    in an industrial establishment; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} providing for any other terms and conditions
    respecting the application of subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of section 189

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} Section 189 applies for the purposes of this
  Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 42, s. 33
- 2001, c. 34, s. 23(F)
:::

### [DIVISION XIII.2]{.HLabel2}[Long-term Disability Plans]{.HTitleText2} {#h-343154 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer's obligation

- **[[239.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer that provides
  benefits to its employees under a long-term disability plan must
  insure the plan with an entity that is licensed to provide insurance
  under the laws of a province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, an employer may provide those benefits under
  a long-term disability plan that is not insured, in the circumstances
  and subject to the conditions provided for in the regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 19, s. 434
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[239.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239.3 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in
Council may make regulations respecting long-term disability plans,
including regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} specifying what constitutes a long-term disability
  plan; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} specifying the circumstances and conditions referred
  to in subsection 239.2(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 19, s. 434
:::

### [DIVISION XIV]{.HLabel2}[Unjust Dismissal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-343168 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaint

- **[[240]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and
  242(3.1), a person who has been dismissed and considers the dismissal
  to be unjust may make a complaint in writing to the Head if the
  employee

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has completed 12 consecutive months of continuous
    employment by an employer; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is not a member of a group of employees subject to
    a collective agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(1.01)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the fact that an employer
  complies with Divisions X and XI does not

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} affect the employee's rights under this Division;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prevent the Board from deciding under paragraph
    242(3)(a) that the dismissal was unjust or from making any order
    under subsection 242(4), including a reinstatement order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} A person shall not make a complaint under
  subsection (1) if they have made a complaint that is based on
  substantially the same facts under either subsection 246.1(1) or
  247.99(1), unless that complaint has been withdrawn.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for making complaint

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a complaint under
  subsection (1) shall be made within ninety days from the date on which
  the person making the complaint was dismissed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Head may extend the period set out in subsection
  (2)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the Head is satisfied that a complaint was made
    in that period to a government official who had no authority to deal
    with the complaint but that the person making the complaint believed
    the official had that authority; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any circumstances that are prescribed by
    regulation.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 240
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 15
- [2018, c. 27, s. 488](#1039952-1192832){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039952-1192832.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 579](#1040485-1282158){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040485-1282158.html"}
- [2024, c. 17, s. 251](#1467842-1481335){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1467842-1481335.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-240-20210101.html "Link to previous version of section 240"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons for dismissal

- **[[241]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an employer dismisses a person described in subsection 240(1), the
  person who was dismissed or the Head may make a request in writing to
  the employer to provide a written statement giving the reasons for the
  dismissal, and any employer who receives such a request must provide
  the person who made the request with such a statement within 15 days
  after the request is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Head to assist parties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a complaint made under subsection
  240(1), the Head must endeavour to assist the parties to the complaint
  to settle the complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaint not settled within reasonable
  time

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a complaint is not settled under subsection (2)
  within the period that the Head considers to be reasonable in the
  circumstances, the Head must, on the written request of the person who
  made the complaint that the complaint be referred to the Board,
  deliver to the Board the complaint made under subsection 240(1), any
  written statement giving the reasons for the dismissal provided under
  subsection (1) and any other statements or documents that the Head has
  that relate to the complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the person who made the complaint does not reply
  to a written communication from the Head within a period that the Head
  considers to be reasonable in the circumstances and a period of at
  least 30 days, or any longer period that may be prescribed by
  regulation, have elapsed from the day on which the complaint was made,
  the Head may give written notice to the person who made the complaint
  that they have the period of 30 days, or any longer period that may be
  prescribed by regulation, set out in the notice to make a written
  request that the complaint be referred to the Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the regulations, if the person who made
  the complaint does not, within the period set out in the notice, make
  a written request that the complaint be referred to the Board, the
  Head may deem the complaint to be withdrawn.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 241
- [2017, c. 20, s. 353](#1036942-1192680){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036942-1192680.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 489](#1039957-1192837){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039957-1192837.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 580](#1040490-1282170){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040490-1282170.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-241-20190729.html "Link to previous version of section 241"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of complaint

- **[[241.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the Board is satisfied that the
  complainant must take measures before the Board may continue to deal
  with the complaint referred to it under subsection 241(3), it may, at
  any time, suspend consideration of the complaint, in whole or in part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Board suspends consideration of a complaint,
  the Board shall notify the complainant in writing and specify in the
  notice

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the measures that the complainant must take; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period within which they must take those
    measures.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}End of suspension

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The suspension ends when, in the Board's opinion, the
  measures specified in the notice have been taken.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 490](#1039959-1192848){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039959-1192848.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rejection of complaint

- **[[241.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Board may reject a complaint
  referred to it under subsection 241(3), in whole or in part,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the Board is satisfied that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the complaint is not within its jurisdiction,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the complaint is frivolous, vexatious or not
      made in good faith,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the complaint has been settled in writing
      between the employer and the complainant,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} there are other means available to the
      complainant to resolve the subject matter of the complaint that
      the Board considers should be pursued, or

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the subject matter of the complaint has been
      adequately dealt with through recourse obtained before a court,
      tribunal, arbitrator or adjudicator; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if consideration of the complaint was suspended
    under subsection 241.1(1) and if, in the Board's opinion, the
    measures specified in the notice under subsection 241.1(2) were not
    taken within the specified period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of rejection of complaint

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Board rejects a complaint, it shall notify the
  complainant in writing, with reasons.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 490](#1039959-1192862){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039959-1192862.html"}
:::

- **[[242]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-242
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2017, c. 20, s.
  354\]]{.Repealed}

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2017, c. 20, s. 354\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision of the Board

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3.1), the Board, after a
  complaint has been referred to it, shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} consider whether the dismissal of the person who
    made the complaint was unjust and render a decision thereon; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} send a copy of the decision with the reasons
    therefor to each party to the complaint and to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on complaints

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} No complaint shall be considered by the Board under
  subsection (3) in respect of a person if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that person has been laid off because of lack of
    work or because of the discontinuance of a function; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a procedure for redress has been provided under
    Part I or Part II of this Act or under any other Act of Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unjust dismissal

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Board decides under subsection (3) that a
  person has been unjustly dismissed, the Board may, by order, require
  the employer who dismissed the person to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} pay the person compensation not exceeding the
    amount of money that is equivalent to the remuneration that would,
    but for the dismissal, have been paid by the employer to the person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reinstate the person in his employ; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} do any other like thing that it is equitable to
    require the employer to do in order to remedy or counteract any
    consequence of the dismissal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payments under subsection 230(1) or
  235(1)

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, in making an order for
  compensation under subsection (4) the Board may, among other things,
  take into account any amount paid by the employer to the employee
  under subsection 230(1) or 235(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 242
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 16

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order final

- **[[243]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-243
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every order of the Board is final
  and shall not be questioned or reviewed in any court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No review by [certiorari]{.otherLang
  lang="la"}, etc.

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No order shall be made, process entered or proceeding
  taken in any court, whether by way of injunction,
  [certiorari]{.otherLang lang="la"}, prohibition, [quo
  warranto]{.otherLang lang="la"} or otherwise, to question, review,
  prohibit or restrain the Board in any proceedings under section 242.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 243
- [2017, c. 20, s. 355](#1036963-1192696){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036963-1192696.html"}
:::

Version](section-243-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 243"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Enforcement of orders

- **[[244]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-244 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  person affected by an order of the Board under subsection 242(4), or
  the Head on the request of such a person, may, after 14 days from the
  day on which the order is made, or from the day provided in the order
  for compliance, whichever is later, file in the Federal Court a copy
  of the order, exclusive of reasons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On filing in the Federal Court under subsection (1),
  an order of the Board shall be registered in the Court and, when
  registered, has the same force and effect, and all proceedings may be
  taken in respect of it, as if the order were a judgment obtained in
  that Court.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 244
- 1993, c. 42, s. 34(F)
- [2017, c. 20, s. 355](#1036963-1192702){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036963-1192702.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 581](#1040492-1282172){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040492-1282172.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-244-20190729.html "Link to previous version of section 244"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[245]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-245 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Division, the Governor in Council may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} defining the absences from employment that shall be
  deemed not to have interrupted continuity of employment;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribing circumstances for the purposes of
  paragraph 240(3)(b);

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} prescribing periods for the purposes of subsection
  241(4);

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the circumstances under which a complaint
  is not to be deemed to be withdrawn under subsection 241(5); and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the conditions that are to be met before
  a complaint may be deemed to be withdrawn under subsection 241(5).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 245
- [2018, c. 27, s. 492](#1039963-1192873){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039963-1192873.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-245-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 245"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Civil remedy

- **[[246]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  civil remedy of an employee against his employer is suspended or
  affected by sections 240 to 245.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of section 189

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Section 189 applies for the purposes of this
  Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1977-78, c. 27, s. 21
:::

### [DIVISION XIV.1]{.HLabel2}[Complaints Relating to Reprisals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1192703 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Complaint to Board

- **[[246.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any employee may make a complaint in
  writing to the Board if they believe that their employer has taken any
  of the following reprisals against them:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employer has taken action against the employee
    in contravention of subsection 173.01(5), 174.1(4) or 177.1(7) or of
    section 208, 209.3, 238, 239, 239.1 or 247.96;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2021, c. 26, s. 25\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} dismissing, suspending, laying off, or demoting the
    employee, imposing a financial or other penalty on the employee, or
    otherwise taking any disciplinary action against the employee,
    because the employee

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has made a complaint under this Part, other than
      a complaint under section 240,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has provided information regarding the wages,
      hours of work, annual vacation or conditions of work of any
      employee to the Head or provided any other assistance to the
      Minister or the Head in the exercise or performance of the
      Minister's or the Head's powers, duties and functions under this
      Part

    - [(ii.1)]{.lawlabel} has provided information regarding the wages,
      hours of work, annual vacation or conditions of work of any
      employee or provided any other assistance to an external
      adjudicator or a member of the Board in the exercise or
      performance, in accordance with subsections 12.001(2) or 14(5), of
      the powers, duties or functions conferred on the Board by this
      Part,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} has testified or is about to testify in a
      proceeding taken or an inquiry held under this Part, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} has exercised, or sought to exercise, any right
      conferred on the employee by this Part;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} taking into account the fact that the employee has
    taken any of the actions referred to in subparagraphs (b)(i) to (iv)
    in any decision with respect to the promotion or training of the
    employee; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} threatening to take any of the reprisals referred
    to in paragraph (b) or (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the fact that an employer
  complies with Divisions X and XI does not

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} affect the employee's rights under this Division;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prevent the Board from deciding under section 246.4
    that the employer has taken a reprisal against them or from making
    any order under that section, including a reinstatement order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employee shall not make a complaint under
  subsection (1) if they have made a complaint that is based on
  substantially the same facts under either subsection 240(1) or
  247.99(1), unless that complaint has been withdrawn.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for making complaint

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A complaint referred to in subsection (1) shall be
  made to the Board not later than 90 days after the day on which the
  employee knew or, in the Board's opinion, ought to have known of the
  action or circumstances giving rise to the complaint.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A complaint made under subsection (1) is itself
  evidence that the reprisal was actually taken and, if a party to the
  complaint proceedings alleges that the reprisal was not taken, the
  burden of proof is on that party.

::: HistoricalNote

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of complaint

- **[[246.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the Board is satisfied that the
  employee must take measures before the Board may continue to deal with
  the complaint made under subsection 246.1(1), it may, at any time,
  suspend consideration of the complaint, in whole or in part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Board suspends a complaint, the Board shall
  notify the employee in writing and specify in the notice

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the measures that the employee must take; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period of time within which the employee must
    take those measures.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}End of suspension

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The suspension ends when, in the Board's opinion, the
  measures specified in the notice have been taken.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 356](#1036971-1192734){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036971-1192734.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 506](#1036971-1192735){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036971-1192735.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rejection of complaint

- **[[246.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Board may reject a complaint
  made under subsection 246.1(1), in whole or in part,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the Board is satisfied that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the complaint is not within its jurisdiction,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the complaint is frivolous, vexatious or not
      made in good faith,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the complaint has been settled in writing
      between the employer and the employee,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} there are other means available to the employee
      to resolve the subject matter of the complaint that the Board
      considers should be pursued,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the subject matter of the complaint has been
      adequately dealt with through recourse obtained before a court,
      tribunal, arbitrator or adjudicator, or

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a complaint made by an employee
      who is subject to a collective agreement, the collective agreement
      covers the subject matter of the complaint and provides a third
      party dispute resolution process; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if consideration of the complaint was suspended
    under subsection 246.2(1) and if, in the Board's opinion, the
    measures specified in the notice under subsection 246.2(2) were not
    taken within the specified time period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of rejection of complaint

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Board rejects a complaint, it shall notify the
  employee in writing, with reasons.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 356](#1036971-1192750){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036971-1192750.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 506](#1036971-1192751){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036971-1192751.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Board orders

**[[246.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246.4 .sectionLabel}** If the Board
determines that a complaint under subsection 246.1(1) is justified, the
Board may, by order, require the employer to cease engaging in or to
rescind the reprisal and, if applicable, to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} permit the employee who has made the complaint to
  return to the duties of their employment;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} reinstate the employee;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} pay to the employee compensation not exceeding the
  sum that, in the Board's opinion, is equivalent to the remuneration
  that would, but for the reprisal, have been paid by the employer to
  the employee;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} pay to the employee compensation not exceeding the
  sum that, in the Board's opinion, is equivalent to any financial or
  other penalty imposed on the employee by the employer; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} do any other thing that the Board considers equitable
  for the employer to do to remedy or counteract any consequence of the
  reprisal.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 356](#1036971-1192760){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036971-1192760.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decisions final

- **[[246.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every decision of the Board made
  under this Division is final and shall not be questioned or reviewed
  in any court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No review by [certiorari]{.otherLang
  lang="la"}, etc.

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No order shall be made, process entered or proceeding
  taken in any court, whether by way of injunction,
  [certiorari]{.otherLang lang="la"}, prohibition, [quo
  warranto]{.otherLang lang="la"} or otherwise, to question, review,
  prohibit or restrain the Board in any proceedings under this Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 356](#1036971-1192767){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036971-1192767.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Enforcement of orders

- **[[246.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any person affected by an order of
  the Board under section 246.4, or the Head on the request of such a
  person, may, after 14 days from the day on which the order is made, or
  from the day provided in the order for compliance, whichever is later,
  file in the Federal Court a copy of the order, exclusive of reasons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On filing in the Federal Court under subsection (1),
  an order of the Board shall be registered in the Court and, when
  registered, has the same force and effect, and all proceedings may be
  taken in respect of it, as if the order were a judgment obtained in
  that Court.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 356](#1036971-1192774){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036971-1192774.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 583](#1040496-1282177){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040496-1282177.html"}
:::

Version](section-246.6-20190729.html "Link to previous version of section 246.6"){.PITLink}

**[[246.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246.7 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2017,
c. 20, s. 356\]]{.Repealed}

### [DIVISION XV]{.HLabel2}[Payment of Wages]{.HTitleText2} {#h-343240 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of wages

**[[247]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-247 .sectionLabel}** Except as otherwise
provided by or under this Part, an employer shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} pay to any employee any wages to which the employee
  is entitled on the regular pay-day of the employee as established by
  the practice of the employer; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} pay any wages or other amounts to which the employee
  is entitled under this Part within thirty days from the time when the
  entitlement to the wages or other amounts arose.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1977-78, c. 27, s. 21
:::
