[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Civil liability of directors

**[[251.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-251.18 .sectionLabel}** Directors of a
corporation are jointly and severally liable for wages and other amounts
to which an employee is entitled under this Part, to a maximum amount
equivalent to six months' wages, to the extent that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the entitlement arose during the particular
  director's incumbency; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} recovery of the amount from the corporation is
  impossible or unlikely.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 42, s. 37
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cooperatives

**[[251.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-251.19 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes
of section 251.18 and subsection 257(3), cooperatives shall be deemed to
be corporations.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 42, s. 37
:::

#### [Information and Returns]{.HTitleText3} {#h-343679 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information and returns

- **[[252]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-252
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall furnish any
  information that the Head may require that relate to their employees,
  including their wages, their hours of work and their general holidays,
  annual vacations and conditions of work as well as any returns that
  the Head may require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records to be kept

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employer shall make and keep for a period of at
  least 36 months after work is performed the records required to be
  kept by regulations made under paragraph 264(1)(a) and those records
  shall be available at all reasonable times for examination by the
  Head.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (2) do not apply in respect of
  hours worked by employees who are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} excluded from the application of Division I under
    subsection 167(2); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} exempt from the application of sections 169 and 171
    pursuant to regulations made under paragraph 175(1)(b).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 252
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 18
- 1993, c. 42, s. 38
- [2015, c. 36, s. 90](#1036339-1259439){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036339-1259439.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 608](#1040685-1282368){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040685-1282368.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-252-20200901.html "Link to previous version of section 252"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to furnish information

- **[[253]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-253
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where the Head is authorized to
  require a person to furnish information under this Part or the
  regulations, the Head may require the information to be furnished by a
  notice to that effect served by personal service, by registered mail
  addressed to the latest known address of the addressee, or by any
  other means prescribed by regulation, and that person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the notice is sent by registered mail, is deemed
    to have received the notice on the seventh day after the day on
    which it was mailed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall furnish the information within such
    reasonable time as is specified in the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of service

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A certificate purporting to be signed by the Head
  certifying that a notice was sent by registered mail or by any other
  means prescribed by regulation to the addressee, accompanied by a true
  copy of the notice and by an identifying post office certificate of
  the registration or other proof, prescribed by regulation, that the
  notice has been sent or received, is admissible in evidence and is
  proof of the statements contained in the certificate, without proof of
  the signature or official character of the person appearing to have
  signed the certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of failure to comply

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the Head is authorized to require a person to
  furnish information under this Part or the regulations, a certificate
  of the Head certifying that the information has not been furnished is
  admissible in evidence and in the absence of any evidence to the
  contrary is proof of the statements contained in it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of documents

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A certificate of the Head certifying that a document
  annexed to it is a document or a true copy of the document made by or
  on behalf of the Head shall be admitted in evidence and has the same
  force and effect as if it had been proven in the ordinary way.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of authority

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A certificate under this section signed or purporting
  to be signed by the Head is admissible in evidence without proof of
  the Head's appointment or signature.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
  Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22) does
  not apply in respect of notices referred to in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 253
- 1993, c. 42, s. 39
- [2017, c. 20, s. 374](#1037092-1122615){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037092-1122615.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 609](#1040687-1282380){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040687-1282380.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-253-20190401.html "Link to previous version of section 253"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Information Related to Employment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1418636 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy --- employee

- **[[253.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-253.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employer must, within the first
  30 days of an employee's employment, provide the employee with a copy
  of any materials that the Head makes available and that contains
  information respecting employers' and employees' rights and
  obligations under this Part and, within 30 days after updated
  materials are made available, provide the employee with a copy of the
  updated materials.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Materials to be posted

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer must post and keep posted the most recent
  version of the materials referred to in subsection (1), in readily
  accessible places where it is likely to be seen by employees.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an employee's employment is terminated by the
  employer, the employer must, not later than the last day of the
  employee's employment, provide the employee with a copy of the most
  recent version of the materials referred to in subsection (1) that
  relate to terminations of employment.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 502](#1040071-1418645){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040071-1418645.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 622](#1040760-1418662){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040760-1418662.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employment statement

- **[[253.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-253.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An employer must, within the first
  30 days of an employee's employment, provide the employee with a
  written statement containing information relating to their employment
  that is prescribed by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Updated information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer must provide an employee with an updated
  employment statement within 30 days after any change is made to the
  information contained in the last statement that was provided to the
  employee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employer's duties

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An employer must retain a copy of any employment
  statement provided under this section for 36 months after the
  employee's employment with the employer ends and, on request, the
  employee must be provided with additional copies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations
  prescribing the information that must be included in a employment
  statement provided under this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 27, s. 502](#1040071-1418656){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040071-1418656.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pay statement

- **[[254]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-254 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  employer shall, at the time of making any payment of wages to an
  employee, furnish the employee with a statement in writing setting out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the period for which the payment is made;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the number of hours for which the payment is made;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the rate of wages;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} details of the deductions made from the wages; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the actual sum being received by the employee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by order, exempt any employer from
  any or all of the requirements of subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 68
:::

#### [Deductions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-343718 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General rule

- **[[254.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-254.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No employer shall make deductions
  from wages or other amounts due to an employee, except as permitted by
  or under this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permitted deductions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The permitted deductions are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} those required by a federal or provincial Act or
    regulations made thereunder;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} those authorized by a court order or a collective
    agreement or other document signed by a trade union on behalf of the
    employee;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} amounts authorized in writing by the employee;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} overpayments of wages by the employer; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} other amounts prescribed by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Damage or loss

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding paragraph (2)(c), no employer shall,
  pursuant to that paragraph, make a deduction in respect of damage to
  property, or loss of money or property, if any person other than the
  employee had access to the property or money in question.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations
  prescribing:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} deductions that an employer is permitted to make in
    addition to those permitted by this section; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which the deductions permitted by
    this section may be made by the employer.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 42, s. 40
:::

#### [Combining Federal Works, Undertakings and Businesses]{.HTitleText3} {#h-343737 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders of Minister combining federal
works, undertakings and businesses

- **[[255]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-255
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where associated or related federal
  works, undertakings and businesses are operated by two or more
  employers having common control or direction, the Minister may, after
  affording to the employers a reasonable opportunity to make
  representations, by order, declare that for all purposes of this Part
  the employers and the federal works, undertakings and businesses
  operated by them that are specified in the order are, respectively, a
  single employer and a single federal work, undertaking or business.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an order is made under subsection (1), the
  employers to which it applies are jointly and severally liable to the
  employees employed in the federal works, undertakings and businesses
  to which the order applies for overtime pay, vacation pay, holiday pay
  and other wages or amounts to which the employees are entitled under
  this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. 17(2nd Supp.), s. 17
- 1977-78, c. 27, s. 25
:::

#### [Offences and Punishment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-343745 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences and punishment

- **[[256]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-256
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person is guilty of an offence
  who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} contravenes any provision of this Part or the
    regulations, other than a provision of Division IX, subsection
    239.1(2), 239.2(1), 251.001(9) or 252(2) or any regulation made
    under section 227 or paragraph 264(1)(a) or (a.1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} contravenes any order made under this Part or the
    regulations; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} discharges, threatens to discharge or otherwise
    discriminates against a person because that person

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} has testified or is about to testify in any
      proceedings or inquiry taken or had under this Part, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has given any information to the Minister or the
      Head regarding the wages, hours of work, annual vacation or
      conditions of work of an employee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who is guilty of an offence under
  subsection (1) is liable on summary conviction

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an employer that is a corporation,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not more than
      \$50,000,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a second offence, to a fine of not more than
      \$100,000, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} for each subsequent offence, to a fine of not
      more than \$250,000; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in all other cases,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not more than
      \$10,000,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a second offence, to a fine of not more than
      \$20,000, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} for each subsequent offence, to a fine of not
      more than \$50,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Second or subsequent offence

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1.1), in
  determining whether a person convicted of an offence has committed a
  second or subsequent offence, an earlier offence may be taken into
  account only if the person was convicted of the earlier offence within
  the five-year period immediately before the day on which the person is
  convicted of the offence for which sentence is being imposed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences --- employers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every employer that contravenes any provision of
  Division IX, subsection 239.1(2) or 239.2(1) or any regulation made
  under section 227 is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
  conviction and liable to a fine of not more than \$250,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every employer who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} fails to keep any record that, by subsection 252(2)
    or any regulation made under paragraph 264(1)(a) or (a.1), the
    employer is required to keep, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} refuses to make available for examination by the
    Head at any reasonable time any such record kept by the employer,

  is guilty of an offence and liable on summary conviction to a fine of
  not more than \$1,000 for each day during which the refusal or failure
  continues.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 256
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 19
- 2012, c. 19, s. 436
- [2015, c. 36, s. 91](#1036346-1259442){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1036346-1259442.html"}
- [2017, c. 20, s. 375](#1037099-1122622){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037099-1122622.html"}
- [2017, c. 20, s. 400](#1037192-1259444){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037192-1259444.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 610](#1040694-1282383){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040694-1282383.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-256-20200901.html "Link to previous version of section 256"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

- **[[257]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  complaint or information under this Part may relate to one or more
  offences by one employer in respect of one or more of his employees.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Proceedings in respect of an offence under this Part
  may be instituted at any time within but not later than three years
  after the time when the subject-matter of the proceedings arose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's consent required

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No proceeding against a director of a corporation in
  respect of an offence under this Part shall be instituted except with
  the consent of the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 257
- 1993, c. 42, s. 41
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to pay arrears of wages

- **[[258]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-258
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where an employer has been convicted
  of an offence under this Part in respect of any employee, the
  convicting court shall, in addition to any other punishment, order the
  employer to pay to the employee any overtime pay, vacation pay,
  holiday pay or other wages or amounts to which the employee is
  entitled under this Part the non-payment or insufficient payment of
  which constituted the offence for which the employer was convicted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reinstatement of pay and position

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an employer has been convicted of an offence
  under this Part in respect of the discharge of an employee, the
  convicting court may, in addition to any other punishment, order the
  employer

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to pay compensation for loss of employment to the
    employee not exceeding such sum as in the opinion of the court is
    equivalent to the wages that would have accrued to the employee up
    to the date of conviction but for such discharge; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to reinstate the employee in his employ at such
    date as in the opinion of the court is just and proper in the
    circumstances and in the position that the employee would have held
    but for such discharge.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When inaccurate records kept

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In determining the amount of wages or overtime for
  the purposes of subsection (1), if the convicting court finds that the
  employer has not kept accurate records as required by this Part or the
  regulations, the employee affected shall be conclusively presumed to
  have been employed for the maximum number of hours a week allowed
  under this Part and to be entitled to the full weekly wage therefor.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 71
- 1977-78, c. 27, s. 27
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to comply with order

**[[259]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-259 .sectionLabel}** An employer that fails
to comply with an order of a convicting court made under section 258 is
guilty of an offence punishable on summary conviction and liable to a
fine of not more than \$1,000 for each day during which the failure
continues.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 259
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 20
- 2012, c. 19, s. 437
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-259-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 259"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Imprisonment precluded in certain cases

- **[[259.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-259.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a person is convicted of an
  offence under this Part punishable on summary conviction, no
  imprisonment may be imposed as punishment for the offence or in
  default of payment of any fine imposed as punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery of penalties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a person is convicted of an offence under this
  Part and the fine that is imposed is not paid when required, the
  prosecutor may, by filing the conviction, enter as a judgment the
  amount of the fine and costs, if any, in a superior court of the
  province in which the trial was held, and the judgment is enforceable
  against the person in the same manner as if it were a judgment
  rendered against the person in that court in civil proceedings.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 20
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identity of complainants

- **[[260]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-260 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  person makes a complaint under this Part and requests that their name
  and identity be withheld, their name and identity must not be
  disclosed unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the disclosure is necessary for the purposes of a
    prosecution;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Head determines that the disclosure is in the
    public interest; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Head determines that the disclosure is
    necessary for the investigation of the complaint to be carried out
    and the complainant consents to the disclosure in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a determination is made under paragraph (1)(c) and
  the complainant refuses to provide their consent after being requested
  to do so in writing, the Head may deem the complaint to be withdrawn.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 260
- [2018, c. 27, s. 503](#1040073-1192958){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040073-1192958.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 612](#1040706-1282388){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040706-1282388.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-260-20190729.html "Link to previous version of section 260"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Civil remedy

**[[261]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-261 .sectionLabel}** No civil remedy of an
employee against his employer for arrears of wages is suspended or
affected by this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 73
:::

#### [Ministerial Orders]{.HTitleText3} {#h-343814 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders

**[[262]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-262 .sectionLabel}** Where by this Part or
the regulations the Minister is authorized to make any order in respect
of any matter, the order may be made to apply generally or in particular
cases or to apply to classes of employees or industrial establishments.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. L-1, s. 74
:::

#### [Pilot Projects]{.HTitleText3} {#h-343819 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[263]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-263 .sectionLabel}** Despite anything in
this Part, the Governor in Council may make any regulations that the
Governor in Council considers necessary respecting the establishment and
operation of one or more pilot projects for testing which possible
amendments to this Part or the regulations made under this Part would
improve and better protect employees' rights under this Part, including
regulations respecting the manner in which and the extent to which any
provision of this Part or the regulations made under this Part applies
to a pilot project and adapting any such provision for the purposes of
that application.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 263
- 1996, c. 11, s. 68
- 2018, c. 27, s. 504
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-263-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 263"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Repeal

**[[263.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-263.1 .sectionLabel}** Unless they are
repealed earlier, regulations made under section 263 are repealed on the
fifth anniversary of the day on which they come into force.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 27, s. 504
:::

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-343828 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[264]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-264 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may make regulations for carrying out the purposes
  of this Part and, without restricting the generality of the foregoing,
  may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} requiring employers to keep records of wages,
    vacations, holidays and overtime of employees and of other
    particulars relevant to the purposes of this Part or any Division
    thereof;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} requiring employers to keep records relevant to
    the purposes of this Part in respect of persons who are excluded
    under subsection 167(1.2) from the application of all or any of this
    Part;

  - [(a.2)]{.lawlabel} respecting the information that an employer must
    provide to the Head for the purpose of establishing that the
    performance of activities referred to in subsection 167(1.2) fulfils
    the requirements of a program referred to in that subsection, and
    the circumstances in which an employer must provide it;

  - [(a.3)]{.lawlabel} specifying the circumstances in which a person
    who performs activities referred to in subsection 167(1.2) must
    provide to an employer the information referred to in paragraph
    (a.2);

  - [(a.4)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose of subsection 167(1.2),
    specifying or describing secondary or post-secondary educational
    institutions, vocational schools, or equivalent educational
    institutions outside Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} designating any branch, section or other division
    of any federal work, undertaking or business as an industrial
    establishment for the purposes of this Part or any Division thereof;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} extending the application of this Part, in the
    manner and to the extent provided for in the regulations, to any
    class of persons;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} governing the production and inspection of records
    required to be kept by employers;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} for calculating and determining wages received by
    an employee in respect of his employment, including the monetary
    value of remuneration other than money and, for the purposes of any
    provision or provisions of this Part specified in the regulations,
    the regular rate of wages of employees;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} for calculating and determining the regular rate of
    wages, on an hourly basis, of employees who are paid on any basis of
    time other than hourly or who are not paid solely on a basis of
    time;

  - [(e.1)]{.lawlabel} respecting the calculation and payment of the
    wages and other amounts to which an employee whose wages are paid on
    a commission basis, on a salary plus commission basis or on any
    other basis other than time is entitled to under Divisions V, VII,
    VIII, X and XI;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the maximum number of hours that may
    elapse between the commencement and termination of the working day
    of any employee;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 27, s. 505\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} requiring an employer in any industrial
    establishment to notify employees, by the publication of such
    notices, in such manner as may be prescribed, of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the provisions of this Part or any regulation or
      order made under this Part,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the particulars of hours of work, including the
      hours at which shifts change,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the particulars of rest periods and meal
      periods, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} other matters related to hours and conditions of
      work of employees;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} providing for the payment of any wages of an
    employee to the Head or to another person in the event that the
    employee cannot be found or in any other case;

  - [(i.1)]{.lawlabel} providing for the application of any provisions
    of this Part or of the regulations made under this Part to persons
    and, in relation to those persons, employers who are otherwise
    excluded under subsection 167(1.2) from the application of this Part
    and adapting those provisions for the purpose of applying them to
    those persons and those employers;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} providing for the establishment of consultative or
    advisory committees to advise the Minister on any matters arising in
    relation to the administration of this Part;

  - [(j.1)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the circumstances and conditions for
    the purposes of subsection 251.01(3);

  - [(j.2)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the circumstances under which a
    complaint is not to be rejected under paragraph 251.05(1)(c);

  - [(j.3)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the conditions that are to be met
    before a complaint may be rejected under paragraph 251.05(1)(c);

  - [(j.4)]{.lawlabel} prescribing periods for the purposes of
    subsection 251.05(1.1); and

  - [(j.5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2020, c. 12, s. 4\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} for any other matter or purpose that under this
    Part is required or permitted to be prescribed by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporation of documents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A regulation made under paragraph (1)(a.4) that
  incorporates by reference, in whole or in part, a document may
  incorporate the document, regardless of its source, as it exists on a
  certain date, as amended to a certain date or as amended from time to
  time.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. L-2, s. 264
- R.S., 1985, c. 9 (1st Supp.), s. 21
- 2012, c. 31, s. 229
- 2015, c. 36, s. 92
- 2017, c. 33, s. 218
- 2018, c. 27, s. 505
- [2018, c. 27, s. 613](#1040716-1282389){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040716-1282389.html"}
- [2020, c. 12, s. 4](#1260408-1261920){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1260408-1261920.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-264-20210101.html "Link to previous version of section 264"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Application of Provincial Laws]{.HTitleText3} {#h-343851 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provincial Crown corporations

**[[265]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-265 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may by regulation direct that this Part applies in respect of any
employment, or any class or classes of employment, on or in connection
with a work or undertaking set out in the regulation that is, or is part
of, a corporation that is an agent of Her Majesty in right of a province
and whose activities are regulated, in whole or in part, pursuant to the
[Nuclear Safety and Control Act](/eng/acts/N-28.3).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 12, s. 4
- 1997, c. 9, s. 125
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusion from application

- **[[266]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-266 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may by regulation exclude, in whole or in part,
  from the application of any of the provisions of this Part any
  employment, or any class or classes of employment, on or in connection
  with a work or undertaking set out in the regulation whose activities
  are regulated, in whole or in part, pursuant to the [Nuclear Safety
  and Control Act](/eng/acts/N-28.3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On the recommendation of the Minister, the Governor
  in Council may make regulations relating to labour standards in
  relation to employment that is subject to a regulation made pursuant
  to subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 12, s. 4
- 1997, c. 9, s. 125
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of certain provisions

**[[267]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-267 .sectionLabel}** Subsections 121.2(3) to
(8) apply, with such modifications as the circumstances require, in
respect of a regulation made pursuant to subsection 266(2) except that
the references to "subsection (2)" in subsections 121.2(3) to (6) shall
be read as references to subsection 266(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 12, s. 4
:::

## [PART IV]{.HLabel1}[Administrative Monetary Penalties]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1281489 .Part}

### [Interpretation and Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1281490 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[268]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-268 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following definitions apply in this Part.

  [[department]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[department]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a department in, or other
      portion of, the federal public administration to which Part II
      applies, as provided under subsection
      123(2). ([ministère]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[employer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[employer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a violation related to Part II,
        the same meaning as in subsection 122(1); and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a violation related to Part III,
        the same meaning as in section
        166. ([employeur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[penalty]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[penalty]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an administrative monetary
      penalty imposed under this Part for a
      violation. ([pénalité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application --- department

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This Part applies to a department and to persons
  employed in a department only in respect of a violation that is
  related to Part II.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281502){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281502.html"}
:::

### [Purpose]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1281503 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of Part

**[[269]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-269 .sectionLabel}** The purpose of this
Part is to establish, as an alternative to the existing penal system and
as a supplement to existing enforcement measures, a fair and efficient
penalty system to promote compliance with Parts II and III of this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281507){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281507.html"}
:::

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1281508 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[270]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-270 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} designating as a violation that may be proceeded
    with in accordance with this Part

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the contravention of any specified provision of
      Part II or III or of any regulations made under those Parts,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the contravention of any direction, or of any
      direction of any specified class of directions, issued under any
      provision of Part II or of any regulations made under that Part,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the contravention of any order, or of any order
      of any specified class of orders, made or issued under any
      provision of Part II or III or of any regulations made under those
      Parts, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the failure to comply with any condition, or
      with any condition of any specified class of conditions, of a
      permit issued under section 176;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the determination of, or the method of
    determining, the amount payable as the penalty for each violation,
    penalties which may be different for individuals and for other
    persons and departments;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the circumstances under which, the
    criteria by which and the manner in which a penalty may be reduced;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the determination of a lesser amount
    than the penalty imposed that may be paid in complete satisfaction
    of the penalty if paid within the time and manner prescribed by
    regulation;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting the service of documents required or
    authorized under this Part, including the manner and proof of
    service and the circumstances under which documents are deemed to be
    served;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the method of calculating and
    determining the regular rate of wages for the purpose of section
    288;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} prescribing anything that by this Part is to be
    prescribed; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} generally, for carrying out the purposes and
    provisions of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction --- amount of penalty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The amount that may be determined under any
  regulations made under paragraph (1)(b) as the penalty for a violation
  may not exceed \$250,000.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281546){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281546.html"}
:::

### [Head's Powers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1281547 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers regarding notices of violation

**[[271]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-271 .sectionLabel}** The Head may

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} establish the form of notices of violation;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} designate persons, or classes of persons, who are
  authorized to issue notices of violation; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} establish a short-form description for each violation
  to be used in notices of violation.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281554){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281554.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 614](#1040721-1282398){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040721-1282398.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

**[[272]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-272 .sectionLabel}** Subject to any terms
and conditions specified by the Minister, the Head may delegate to any
qualified person or class of persons any of the powers the Head is
authorized to exercise or any of the duties or functions the Head is
authorized to perform for the purposes of this Part. The Head may make
the delegation subject to any terms and conditions that the Head
considers appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281558){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281558.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 614](#1040721-1282401){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040721-1282401.html"}
:::

### [Commission of Violations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1281559 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Violations

**[[273]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273 .sectionLabel}** Every person or
department that contravenes or fails to comply with a provision,
direction, order or condition designated by regulations made under
paragraph 270(1)(a) commits a violation and is liable to a penalty of an
amount to be determined in accordance with the regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281563){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281563.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability of parties to violation

**[[274]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-274 .sectionLabel}** If a corporation or a
department commits a violation, any of the following persons who
directed, authorized, assented to, acquiesced in or participated in the
commission of the violation is a party to the violation and is liable to
a penalty of an amount to be determined in accordance with the
regulations, whether or not the corporation or department has been
proceeded against in accordance with this Part:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any officer, director, agent or mandatary of the
  corporation;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any senior official in the department; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} any other person exercising managerial or supervisory
  functions in the corporation or department.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281570){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281570.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of violation --- employees

**[[275]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-275 .sectionLabel}** In any proceedings
under this Part against a person or a department in relation to a
violation, it is sufficient proof of the violation to establish that it
was committed by an employee or agent or mandatary of the person or of
the department, whether or not the employee or agent or mandatary has
been identified or proceeded against in accordance with this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281574){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281574.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of violation

- **[[276]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-276 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  person designated under paragraph 271(b) has reasonable grounds to
  believe that a person or a department has committed a violation, the
  designated person may issue a notice of violation and shall cause it
  to be served on the person or on the department in accordance with the
  regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The notice of violation shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} name the person or department that is believed to
    have committed the violation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} set out the relevant facts surrounding the
    violation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set out the penalty for the violation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} inform the person or department of their right to
    contest the facts of the alleged violation or the penalty, by way of
    review and appeal, and of the procedure to be followed to exercise
    that right;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} inform the person or department of the manner of
    paying the penalty set out in the notice; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} inform the person or department that, if they do
    not pay the penalty or exercise their right referred to in paragraph
    (d), they will be considered to have committed the violation and
    that they are liable for the penalty set out in the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy given by employer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the notice of violation is issued to an employer
  who has committed a violation by contravening a provision of Part II
  or a direction issued under that Part, the employer shall, without
  delay, give a copy of the notice to the [[work place
  committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} or [[health and safety
  representative]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as those terms are defined in
  subsection 122(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281589){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281589.html"}
:::

### [Rules About Violations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1281590 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain defences not available

- **[[277]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-277 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person or department named in a notice of violation does not have a
  defence by reason that the person or the department

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} exercised due diligence to prevent the violation;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reasonably and honestly believed in the existence
    of facts that, if true, would exonerate the person or the
    department.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Common law principles

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every rule and principle of the common law that
  renders any circumstance a justification or excuse in relation to a
  charge for an offence under Part II or III applies in respect of a
  violation to the extent that it is not inconsistent with this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281599){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281599.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing violation

**[[278]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-278 .sectionLabel}** A violation that is
committed or continued on more than one day constitutes a separate
violation for each day on which it is committed or continued.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281603){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281603.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Violation or offence

- **[[279]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-279
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Proceeding with any act or omission
  as a violation under this Part precludes proceeding with it as an
  offence under Part II or III, and proceeding with it as an offence
  under Part II or III precludes proceeding with it as a violation under
  this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a violation is not an offence
  and, accordingly, section 126 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)
  does not apply in respect of a violation.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281610){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281610.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

**[[280]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-280 .sectionLabel}** No notice of violation
in respect of a violation may be issued more than two years after the
day on which the subject-matter of the violation arises.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281614){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281614.html"}
:::

### [Reviews]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1281615 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for review

**[[281]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-281 .sectionLabel}** A person or a
department that is served with a notice of violation may, within 30 days
after the day on which the notice is served, or within any longer period
that the Head allows, make a request, in the manner prescribed by
regulation, to the Head for a review of the penalty or the facts of the
alleged violation, or both.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281619){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281619.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 615](#1040723-1282404){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040723-1282404.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation or cancellation of notice of
violation

**[[282]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-282 .sectionLabel}** At any time before a
request for review in respect of a notice of violation comes before the
Head, a person designated under paragraph 271(b) may cancel the notice
of violation or correct an error in it.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281623){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281623.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 615](#1040723-1282407){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040723-1282407.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review

- **[[283]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-283 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  receipt of a request for review made under section 281, the Head shall
  conduct the review of the notice of violation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules of procedure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Head may make rules governing the procedure with
  respect to reviews under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request treated as an appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Head may, if the Head considers it appropriate in
  the circumstances, treat the request for review as an appeal, in which
  case the Head shall so inform the applicant and refer the request for
  review to the Board, and the Board shall be considered to have an
  appeal before it for the purposes of this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281632){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281632.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 615](#1040723-1282415){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040723-1282415.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Object of review

- **[[284]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-284 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Head shall determine, as the case may be, whether the amount of the
  penalty for the violation was determined in accordance with the
  regulations or whether the applicant committed the violation, or both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Correction of penalty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Head determines that the amount of the penalty
  for the violation was not determined in accordance with the
  regulations, the Head shall correct the amount of the penalty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Head shall make a decision in writing and serve
  the applicant with a copy of the decision, with reasons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy given by employer

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a decision is made with respect to a notice of
  violation referred to in subsection 276(3), the employer shall,
  without delay, give a copy of the decision to the [[work place
  committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} or [[health and safety
  representative]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as those terms are defined in
  subsection 122(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation to pay

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the Head determines that the applicant committed
  the violation, the applicant is liable for the penalty that is set out
  in the decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision final

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the right of appeal under section 285,
  every decision made under this section is final and shall not be
  questioned or reviewed in any court.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281647){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281647.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 616](#1040730-1282423){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040730-1282423.html"}
:::

### [Appeal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1281648 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal

- **[[285]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-285 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person or a department may appeal a decision referred to in section
  284 to the Board, in writing, within 15 days after the day on which
  the decision is served.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grounds of appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The request for appeal shall contain a statement of
  the grounds of appeal.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281655){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281655.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 617(F)](#1040735-1282424){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040735-1282424.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Head informed of appeal

- **[[286]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-286 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Board shall inform the Head in writing when an appeal is brought under
  subsection 285(1) and provide the Head with a copy of the request for
  appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents provided to Board

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Head shall, on request of the Board, provide to
  the Board a copy of any document that the Head relied on for the
  purpose of making the decision being appealed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents provided to Head

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Board shall, on request of the Head, provide to
  the Head a copy of any document that is filed with the Board in the
  appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of Head

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Head may, in an appeal, present evidence and make
  representations to the Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281666){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281666.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 618](#1040737-1282434){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040737-1282434.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Object of appeal

- **[[287]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-287 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  an appeal under this Part, the Board shall determine, as the case may
  be, whether the amount of the penalty for the violation was determined
  in accordance with the regulations or whether the appellant committed
  the violation, or both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Correction of penalty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Board determines that the amount of the
  penalty for the violation was not determined in accordance with the
  regulations, the Board shall correct the amount of the penalty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Board shall make a decision in writing and
  provide the appellant and the Head with a copy of the decision, with
  reasons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy given by employer

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a decision is made with respect to a notice of
  violation referred to in subsection 276(3), the employer shall,
  without delay, give a copy of the decision to the [[work place
  committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} or [[health and safety
  representative]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as those terms are defined in
  subsection 122(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation to pay

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the Board determines that the appellant committed
  the violation, the appellant is liable for the penalty that is set out
  in the decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision final

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Every decision made under this section is final and
  shall not be questioned or reviewed in any court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No review by [certiorari]{.otherLang
  lang="la"}, etc.

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} No order shall be made, process entered or proceeding
  taken in any court, whether by way of injunction,
  [certiorari]{.otherLang lang="la"}, prohibition, [quo
  warranto]{.otherLang lang="la"} or otherwise, to question, review,
  prohibit or restrain the Board in any proceedings under this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281683){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281683.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 619](#1040739-1282437){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040739-1282437.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Wages

**[[288]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-288 .sectionLabel}** An employee who has
been summoned by the Board to attend at an appeal proceeding under this
Part and who attends is entitled to be paid by the employer at the
employee's regular rate of wages for the time spent at the proceeding
that would otherwise have been time at work.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281687){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281687.html"}
:::

### [Responsibility]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1281688 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment

**[[289]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-289 .sectionLabel}** If a person or a
department pays the penalty set out in a notice of violation, the person
or the department is considered to have committed the violation and
proceedings in respect of it are ended.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281692){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281692.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to act

**[[290]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-290 .sectionLabel}** A person or a
department that neither pays a penalty imposed under this Part nor
requests a review or an appeal in the specified time is considered to
have committed the violation and is liable for the penalty.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281696){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281696.html"}
:::

### [Recovery of Penalties]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1281697 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debt to Her Majesty

- **[[291]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-291 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  penalty constitutes a debt due to Her Majesty in right of Canada and
  is recoverable as such in the Federal Court or any other court of
  competent jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No proceedings to recover the debt may be instituted
  more than five years after the day on which the debt becomes payable.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281704){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281704.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

- **[[292]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-292 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Head may issue a certificate certifying the unpaid amount of any debt
  referred to in subsection 291(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Registration in the Federal Court or in any other
  court of competent jurisdiction of a certificate issued under
  subsection (1) has the same force and effect as a judgment of that
  court for a debt of the amount specified in the certificate and all
  related registration costs.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281711){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281711.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 620](#1040741-1282439){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040741-1282439.html"}
:::

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1281712 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Admissibility of documents

**[[293]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-293 .sectionLabel}** In the absence of
evidence to the contrary, a document that appears to be a notice of
violation issued under subsection 276(1) is presumed to be authentic and
is proof of its contents in any proceeding in respect of a violation.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281720){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281720.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

**[[294]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-294 .sectionLabel}** If the facts of a
violation are reviewed or appealed, the person who issued the notice of
violation shall establish, on a balance of probabilities, that the
applicant or the appellant committed the violation.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281724){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281724.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

**[[295]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-295 .sectionLabel}** The Head may, subject
to the regulations, make public the name of an employer who committed a
violation under this Part, the nature of the violation, the amount of
the penalty imposed and any other information prescribed by regulation.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2017, c. 20, s. 377](#1037103-1281728){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037103-1281728.html"}
- [2018, c. 27, s. 621](#1040743-1282442){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1040743-1282442.html"}
:::

### [Pilot Projects]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1281871 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[296]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-296 .sectionLabel}** Despite anything in
this Part, the Governor in Council may make any regulations that the
Governor in Council considers necessary respecting the establishment and
operation of one or more pilot projects for testing which possible
amendments to this Part or the regulations made under this Part would
improve compliance with Parts II and III of this Act, including
regulations respecting the manner in which and the extent to which any
provision of this Part or the regulations made under this Part applies
to a pilot project, and adapting any such provision for the purposes of
that application.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 22, s. 17](#1038889-1281883){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1038889-1281883.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Repeal of regulations

**[[297]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-297 .sectionLabel}** Unless they are
repealed earlier, regulations made under section 296 are repealed on the
fifth anniversary of the day on which they come into force.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2018, c. 22, s. 17](#1038889-1281887){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1038889-1281887.html"}
:::

## RELATED PROVISIONS {#related-provisions .scheduleTitleText}

- --- 2010, c. 12, s. 2178

  - ###### Deemed appointment {#deemed-appointment .MarginalNote}

    [2178]{.lawlabel} Any appeals officer, as defined in subsection
    122(1) of the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2), who is seized of
    an appeal under that Act immediately before the day on which section
    2174 comes into force is deemed, on that day, to have been appointed
    as an appeals officer under subsection 145.1(1) of that Act, as
    enacted by section 2174, solely for the purpose of performing their
    duties under section 146.1 of that Act in respect of the appeal.

- --- 2012, c. 19, s. 438

  - ###### Existing claims {#existing-claims .MarginalNote}

    [438]{.lawlabel} If, before the coming into force of section 239.2
    of the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as enacted by section
    434, an employer provides benefits to its employees under a
    long-term disability plan that is not insured with an entity that is
    licensed to provide insurance under the laws of a province and
    either benefits are being paid to one of those employees under that
    plan or an application for the payment of benefits under that plan
    has been submitted by one of those employees, that employer, on the
    coming into force of that section 239.2, is not required to insure
    that plan in accordance with that section 239.2 and may continue to
    provide benefits under that plan but only to the employee who is
    being paid benefits or to the employee who submitted an application
    for the payment of benefits.

- --- 2012, c. 19, s. 439

  - ###### Limitation --- second or subsequent offences {#limitation-second-or-subsequent-offences .MarginalNote}

    [439]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection 256(1.2) of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as enacted by section 436, in determining
    whether a person has committed a second or subsequent offence for
    the purposes of subsection 256(1.1) of that Act as enacted by that
    section, an earlier offence may be taken into account only if the
    person is convicted of the earlier offence on or after the day on
    which that section comes into force.

- --- 2012, c. 19, s. 564

  - ###### Definitions {#definitions .MarginalNote}

    [564]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in sections 565 to
    570.

    [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Canada Industrial
        Relations Board established by section 9 of the [Canada Labour
        Code](/eng/acts/L-2). ([Conseil]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

    [[Tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[Tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Canadian Artists and
        Producers Professional Relations Tribunal established by
        subsection 10(1) of the [Status of the Artist
        Act](/eng/acts/S-19.6), as that Act read immediately before the
        coming into force of this section. ([Tribunal]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

- --- 2012, c. 27, s. 31

  - ###### Death or disappearance {#death-or-disappearance .MarginalNote}

    [31]{.lawlabel} Section 206.5 of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as enacted by section 6, applies only with
    respect to a death or disappearance that occurs after the day on
    which section 6 comes into force.

- --- 2012, c. 31, s. 230

  - ###### Complaints, notices and payment orders {#complaints-notices-and-payment-orders .MarginalNote}

    [230]{.lawlabel} The [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as it read
    immediately before the day on which this section comes into force,
    applies

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to complaints that allege that an employer
      contravened any provision of Part III of that Act, any provision
      of regulations made under that Part or any order within the
      meaning of that Part and that were received by the Minister of
      Labour before that day;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to notices of unfounded complaint issued under
      subsection 251.1(2) of that Act that relate to complaints referred
      to in paragraph (a); and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to payment orders issued under subsection
      251.1(1) of that Act

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} before that day, and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on or after that day, if the inspector made
        the finding that resulted in the payment order during the course
        of an inspection under Part III of that Act that began before
        that day or as a result of dealing with a complaint referred to
        in paragraph (a).

- --- 2012, c. 31, s. 231

  - ###### Payment orders and notices {#payment-orders-and-notices .MarginalNote}

    [231]{.lawlabel} The [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as it read
    immediately before the day on which this section comes into force,
    applies to any payment orders and notices of unfounded complaint
    issued before that day under section 251.1 of that Act.

- --- 2013, c. 40, s. 199

  - ###### Pending proceedings {#pending-proceedings .MarginalNote}

    - [199]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The [Canada Labour
      Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as it read immediately before the coming
      into force of this section, applies to

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any proceedings --- commenced before that
        coming into force --- with respect to which a health and safety
        officer or a regional health and safety officer may exercise
        powers or perform duties or functions under Part II of that Act,
        as it read immediately before that coming into force; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any procedure --- commenced before that coming
        into force --- relating to a refusal to work commenced under
        sections 128 to 129 of that Act, as it read immediately before
        that coming into force.

    - ###### Appeal {#appeal .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} With respect to directions issued by a health and
      safety officer under Part II of the [Canada Labour
      Code](/eng/acts/L-2), an employer, employee or trade union that
      feels aggrieved by a direction may appeal the direction in writing
      to an appeals officer within 30 days after the date of the
      direction being issued or confirmed in writing. The appeal is
      deemed to have been brought under subsection 146(1) of that Act.

    - ###### Health and safety officers {#health-and-safety-officers .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), health and
      safety officers or regional health and safety officers designated
      under subsection 140(1) of the [Canada Labour
      Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as it read immediately before the coming
      into force of this section, continue to act, respectively, as
      health and safety officers or regional health and safety officers.

- --- 2014, c. 20, s. 143

  - ###### Paragraphs 125.1(c) to (e) of [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) {#paragraphs-125.1c-to-e-of-canada-labour-code .MarginalNote}

    [143]{.lawlabel} Paragraphs 125.1(c) to (e) of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2) do not apply to an employer, on or after the
    day on which section 140 comes into force but before a day to be
    fixed by order of the Governor in Council for the purposes of this
    section, if the employer complies with the requirements set out in
    those paragraphs as they read immediately before the day on which
    section 140 comes into force, as if those paragraphs were still in
    force.

- --- 2014, c. 20, s. 144

  - ###### Products in work place {#products-in-work-place .MarginalNote}

    [144]{.lawlabel} Paragraphs 125.1(c) to (e) of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2) do not apply to an employer, on or after the
    day fixed by order of the Governor in Council for the purposes of
    section 143 but before a day to be fixed by order of the Governor in
    Council for the purposes of this section, in respect of hazardous
    products that are in the work place on the day fixed by order of the
    Governor in Council for the purposes of section 143, if the employer
    complies with the requirements set out in those paragraphs as they
    read immediately before the day on which section 140 comes into
    force, as if those paragraphs were still in force.

- --- 2014, c. 20, s. 145

  - ###### Same meaning {#same-meaning .MarginalNote}

    - [145]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless a contrary intention
      appears, words and expressions used in sections 143 and 144 have
      the same meanings as in section 122 of the [Canada Labour
      Code](/eng/acts/L-2).

    - ###### References {#references .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of sections 143 and 144,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} [[controlled product]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm},
        [[hazard symbol]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, [[Ingredient Disclosure
        List]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, [[label]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} and
        [[material safety data sheet]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in paragraphs
        125.1(c) to (e) of the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as
        that Act read immediately before the day on which section 140
        comes into force, have the same meanings as in the [Hazardous
        Products Act](/eng/acts/H-3), as that Act read immediately
        before the day on which section 114 comes into force;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any references to the Ingredient Disclosure
        List in paragraph 125.1(e) of the [Canada Labour
        Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as that Act read immediately before the
        day on which section 140 comes into force, are considered to be
        references to the Ingredient Disclosure List as it read
        immediately before the day on which section 114 comes into
        force; and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any references to regulations, or to anything
        prescribed by regulation, in paragraphs 125.1(c) to (e) of the
        [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as that Act read
        immediately before the day on which section 140 comes into
        force, are considered to be references to those regulations as
        they read immediately before that day.

- --- 2017, c. 12, s. 14

  - ###### [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) --- existing applications {#canada-labour-code-existing-applications .MarginalNote}

    [14]{.lawlabel} If the Canada Industrial Relations Board has, during
    the period beginning on June 16, 2015 and ending immediately before
    the day on which section 1 comes into force, received an application
    for certification referred to in paragraph 28(2)(a) of the [Canada
    Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) or an application for an order made
    under subsection 38(1) or (3) of that Act, and the application has
    not been finally disposed of before that coming into force, that
    application is to be dealt with and disposed of in accordance with
    that Act as it read immediately before that coming into force.

- --- 2017, c. 20, s. 382, as amended by 2018, c. 22, s. 24

  - ###### Appeals --- subsection 129(7) or 146(1) {#appeals-subsection-1297-or-1461 .MarginalNote}

    [382]{.lawlabel} The [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as it read
    immediately before the day on which this section comes into force,
    applies with respect to any appeal made before that day under
    subsection 129(7) or 146(1) of that Act.

- --- 2017, c. 20, s. 383

  - ###### Complaints --- subsection 240(1) {#complaints-subsection-2401 .MarginalNote}

    [383]{.lawlabel} The [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as it read
    immediately before the day on which this section comes into force,

- --- 2017, c. 20, s. 384

    [384]{.lawlabel} Division XIV.1 of Part III of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2) does not apply with respect to reprisals

  - ###### Administrative fee {#administrative-fee .MarginalNote}

    [389]{.lawlabel} Section 251.131 of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2) does not apply with respect to

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a payment order issued as a result of an

- --- 2017, c. 20, s. 390

  - ###### Part IV of the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) {#part-iv-of-the-canada-labour-code .MarginalNote}

    [390]{.lawlabel} Part IV of the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2)
    does not apply to violations committed before the day on which this
    section comes into force.

- --- 2017, c. 20, s. 392

  - ###### Persons who occupy a position {#persons-who-occupy-a-position .MarginalNote}

    - [392]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} All of the persons who occupy a
      position within the Department of Employment and Social
      Development and carry out powers, duties or functions that are in
      whole or in part in support of or related to the powers, duties
      and functions of appeals officers under Part II of the [Canada
      Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) or those of the Minister of Labour
      under sections 242, 251.11 and 251.12 of that Act immediately
      before the day on which this section comes into force occupy their
      position within the Administrative Tribunals Support Service of
      Canada beginning on a day to be fixed by order of the Governor in
      Council.

    - ###### No change in status {#no-change-in-status .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsection (1) is to be construed as
      affecting the status of such a person, except that the person,
      beginning on the day fixed by the order referred to in subsection
      (1), occupies their position within the Administrative Tribunals
      Support Service of Canada.

    - ###### Transfer of money {#transfer-of-money .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any money that is appropriated by an Act of
      Parliament, for the fiscal year that includes the day fixed by the
      order referred to in subsection (1), to defray any charges and
      expenses of the Department of Employment and Social Development
      related to an appeal under Part II or III of the [Canada Labour
      Code](/eng/acts/L-2) or to the powers, duties and functions of the
      Minister of Labour under sections 242, 251.11 and 251.12 of that
      Act and that is unexpended on that day is deemed, on that day, to
      be an amount appropriated to defray the charges and expenses of
      the Administrative Tribunals Support Service of Canada.

- --- 2017, c. 33, s. 214

  - ###### Subsection 175(2) of [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) {#subsection-1752-of-canada-labour-code .MarginalNote}

    [214]{.lawlabel} Subsection 175(2) of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2) continues to apply in respect of the making of
    any regulations under paragraph 175(1)(a) or (b) of that Act for

    248 of that Act.

- --- 2018, c. 22, s. 18

  - ###### Requests received before coming into force {#requests-received-before-coming-into-force .MarginalNote}

    [18]{.lawlabel} A request for an exemption under subsection 135(3)
    of the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as it read on the day
    before the day on which section 7 comes into force, that is received
    by the Minister before the day on which that section comes into
    force is to be dealt with in accordance with subsections 135(3)
    to (5) of that Act as they read on the day before the day on which
    that section comes into force. If the request is approved on or
    after the day on which that section comes into force, the exemption
    may be granted for a period of not more than one year.

- --- 2018, c. 27, s. 517

  - ###### Section 179 of [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) {#section-179-of-canada-labour-code .MarginalNote}

    [517]{.lawlabel} Section 179 of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as enacted by section 448 of this Act, applies
    to each person who, on the day on which that section 448 comes into
    force, is 17 years of age and is employed by an employer as if they
    were 18 years of age so long as they remain employed by that
    employer in the position they held on that day.

- --- 2018, c. 27, s. 519

  - ###### Subsection 189(1.1) of [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) {#subsection-1891.1-of-canada-labour-code .MarginalNote}

    [519]{.lawlabel} Subsection 189(1.1) of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2) applies only if the first day on which the
    second employer referred to in that subsection carries out the
    federal work, undertaking or business is on or after the day on
    which section 457 of this Act comes into force.

- --- 2018, c. 27, s. 522

  - ###### Individual termination of employment {#individual-termination-of-employment .MarginalNote}

    [522]{.lawlabel} If an employer gives notice to an employee under
    paragraph 230(1)(a) of the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2)
    before the day on which section 485 of this Act comes into force,
    Division X of Part III of the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2),
    as it read immediately before that coming into force, applies to the

- --- 2018, c. 27, s. 523

  - ###### Reimbursement of work-related expenses {#reimbursement-of-work-related-expenses .MarginalNote}

    [523]{.lawlabel} Division XII.1 of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2) applies only in respect of expenses incurred on

    Code](/eng/acts/L-2), section 239 of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as it read immediately before the coming into

    Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as it read immediately before the day on which

  - ###### Section 167.1 of [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) {#section-167.1-of-canada-labour-code .MarginalNote}

- --- 2024, c. 17, s. 256

  - ###### Definitions {#definitions-1 .MarginalNote}

    - [256]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply
      in this section.

      [[commencement day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[commencement day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the day on
          which this section comes into force. ([date de
          référence]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

      [[former Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[former Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the [Canada Labour
          Code](/eng/acts/L-2) as it read immediately before
          commencement day. ([ancienne loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

      [[new Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[new Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the [Canada Labour
          Code](/eng/acts/L-2) as it reads on commencement
          day. ([nouvelle loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

    - ###### Ongoing complaint {#ongoing-complaint .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} In any complaint made under subsection 251.01(1)
      of the former Act that relates to an amount referred to in
      subsection 230(1) or 235(1) of that Act and that is ongoing on the
      commencement day, the [[Head]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} or the
      [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as those expressions are defined
      in section 2 of that Act, or the court, as the case may be, must
      consider subsections 230(1.01) and 235(1.1) of the new Act as if
      they were in force at the time of the termination of employment.

    - ###### For greater certainty {#for-greater-certainty .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, for the purposes of
      subsection (2), a complaint is ongoing until all procedures for
      review and appeal available at law have been exhausted.

    - ###### Past termination {#past-termination .MarginalNote}

      [(4)]{.lawlabel} In any complaint made under subsection 251.01(1)
      of the new Act that relates to an amount referred to in subsection
      230(1) or 235(1) of that Act with respect to a termination of
      employment that occurred before the commencement day, the
      [[Head]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as that expression is defined in
      section 2 of that Act, must consider subsections 230(1.01) and
      235(1.1) of the new Act as if they were in force at the time of
      the termination.
:::
::::

- --- 2018, c. 27, s. 312

  - ###### [2012, c. 27]{.HistoricalNote} {#c.-27 .MarginalNote}

    [312]{.lawlabel} On the first day on which both section 35 of the
    Helping Families in Need Act has produced its effects and section
    310 of this Act is in force, subsection 206.1(3) of the [Canada
    Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) is replaced by the following:

    :::: section
    ::: AmendedText
    - ###### Aggregate leave --- employees {#aggregate-leave-employees .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} The aggregate amount of leave that may be taken
      by more than one employee under this section in respect of the
      same event, as described in paragraphs (1)(a) to (c), shall not
      exceed 71 weeks, but the amount of leave that may be taken by one
      employee under this section in respect of the same event shall not
      exceed 63 weeks.
    :::
    ::::
